- **[MED]** MED-001: `note.py` `is_interrogative()` — an auxiliary-verb opener makes every imperative a "question", so real clinician advice is silently dropped
  - Triage: Fix-now · Fix route: fix-on-fast
  - Why it matters: the opener list contains `do`, `have`, `can`, `will`… so "Do your home exercise programme twice a day" and "Have a look at the stretch sheet" both read as interrogative. Both match `advice_home_exercise` cues, both are clinician-owned, and both are therefore dropped from the note entirely. That is a silent omission of clinician-spoken advice — the exact thing the completion gate's "no material clinician-spoken content silently dropped" criterion forbids.
  - Current behaviour: `tokens[0] in _INTERROGATIVE_OPENERS` → interrogative.
  - Desired behaviour: WH-openers stay interrogative on their own; auxiliary openers count only when followed by a personal-pronoun subject ("do you", "have you", "can we"). A trailing `?` remains the primary signal.
  - Pattern siblings: none found — `is_interrogative` has one call site (`ExtractiveNoteProvider._route`).
  - Verification: fixture tests for the imperative-vs-question pair; full suite.
  - Regression risk: `_route` only; a narrower question test can only ADMIT more content into clinician-owned sections, so the role gate stays the binding control.
  - /fix decision: Applied · /fix notes: `note.py` — split `_INTERROGATIVE_OPENERS` into `_WH_OPENERS` + `_AUX_OPENERS` with a `_SUBJECT_TOKENS` follow-token rule; added `test_imperative_advice_is_not_a_question` and an extractive routing test proving "Do your home exercise programme twice a day" reaches `advice_home_exercise`; siblings none; ruff + mypy + 645 tests green · /fix date: 2026-08-04 · /fix applied by: Claude Code
- **[MED]** MED-002: `note.py` `MockNoteModelProvider._flip_laterality` — the laterality mutation silently no-ops on a capitalised token
  - Triage: Fix-now · Fix route: fix-on-fast
  - Why it matters: `token.replace(normalise_token(token), flipped)` compares a lowercased needle against the ORIGINAL token, so "Left" → "Left". Whisper capitalises every segment-initial word, so this is the common case, not an edge case. The `laterality_flip` cell would then produce output identical to `faithful`, and Axis B's laterality row would pass while testing nothing — a silently-degrading adversarial instrument is precisely what the fixture matrix cannot afford, since the matrix IS this phase's safety evidence.
  - Current behaviour: mutation applied only when the token is already lowercase; no check that anything changed.
  - Desired behaviour: replace the matched core case-insensitively, preserving surrounding punctuation, and fail loudly when no laterality token exists.
  - Pattern siblings: `_flip_negation` (deletes by normalised match — correct), `_substitute_name` (whole-token replacement — correct), `_change_dose` (digit regex — correct). Checked; no sibling carries the defect.
  - Verification: a capitalised-"Left" fixture asserting the flip actually happens.
  - Regression risk: mock provider only; never constructed in app code.
  - /fix decision: Applied · /fix notes: `note.py` — index the normalised core in the lowercased token and splice, preserving punctuation; `test_laterality_flip_handles_a_capitalised_token` added; siblings verified clean; ruff + mypy + 645 tests green · /fix date: 2026-08-04 · /fix applied by: Claude Code
- **[LOW]** LOW-001: `note.py` `MockNoteModelProvider` — `over_omission` on a single-utterance fixture silently equals `faithful` — Triage: Fix-now; Decision: Applied — raises `NoteProviderError` when there is nothing to omit, matching the loud-failure rule the other inapplicable behaviours already follow · /fix date: 2026-08-04 · /fix applied by: Claude Code
- **[LOW]** LOW-002: `note.py` `MockNoteModelProvider._mutate` — only the FIRST assertion is inspected, while the error says "in the transcript" — Triage: Fix-now; Decision: Applied — mutators now return `None` instead of raising, `_mutate` walks the assertions and mutates the first that can carry the class, and raises only when NO utterance can; messages reworded to "no utterance contains …" · /fix date: 2026-08-04 · /fix applied by: Claude Code
- **[LOW]** LOW-003: `note.py` `MAX_ASSERTION_CHARS = 4000` — an unusually long single VAD segment makes the shipping provider raise a raw pydantic `ValidationError` instead of a typed error — Triage: Fix-now; Decision: Applied — raised to 20 000 chars (~3 000 words; no real utterance reaches it) with the bound's two roles documented; per-field config limits stay Task 3.2's · /fix date: 2026-08-04 · /fix applied by: Claude Code

#### PR-HIGH-001: `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/logging_setup.py:145` — tripwire ignores exception and stack rendering, so a note-validation traceback can persist clinical text
- Classification: 🆕
- Triage: Fix-now
- Fix route: premium-only
- Why it matters: logs are plaintext rotating files and the threat model treats “no clinical content in logs” as an explicit non-flow. A routine defensive `logger.exception(...)` around note construction can therefore persist note text even though the central tripwire claims to protect direct stdlib-logger misuse.
- Current behaviour: `PayloadTripwireFilter.filter()` scans only `record.getMessage()`. Handler filters run before the formatter appends `record.exc_info` / `record.stack_info`; a production `setup_logging()` proof that logged a caught `NoteSpan` `ValidationError` produced `dropped_delta=0` and wrote the synthetic `SECRET` span text into the log traceback.
- Desired behaviour: the centralized tripwire must scan the complete content a handler can emit — message plus formatted exception and stack information — and drop the record before either file or stderr output when any payload signature appears.
- Pattern to follow: keep the single `PayloadTripwireFilter` attached to every production handler, but build its scan text from `record.getMessage()` plus safely rendered `exc_info` / `stack_info` (or use an equivalent handler-level pre-write guard) before signature matching.
- Pattern siblings: protocol-envelope, transcript-model, and note-model validation exceptions on both rotating-file and stderr handlers; all share this one filter and need the same fix.
- Invariant: no formatted log record may reach any handler output when its message, exception traceback, or stack rendering contains a registered clinical/payload signature.
- Verification: add a production-handler regression test that catches a `NoteSpan` validation error containing a synthetic marker, calls `logger.exception("note validation failed")`, and asserts the drop counter increments and neither the marker nor traceback reaches the log; repeat through the stderr handler or assert both handlers share the fixed filter; run ruff, mypy, and the full pytest suite.
- Regression risk: formatting exception data inside a filter can recurse, mutate `record.exc_text`, or duplicate work across handlers; keep rendering side-effect-free and accept conservative false-positive drops when traceback text contains a payload field name.
- /fix decision: Applied
- /fix notes: `logging_setup.py` — `PayloadTripwireFilter.filter()` now scans `PayloadTripwireFilter._scan_text(record)`: the message PLUS a rendered `exc_info` traceback PLUS `stack_info`. Rendering is side-effect-free per the finding's `Regression risk` line — `record.exc_text` is READ as a fallback (for records whose `exc_info` was cleared after formatting, as `QueueHandler.prepare` does) but never written back, so the formatter keeps sole ownership of that cache and two handlers cannot disagree about what was scanned; the accepted cost is that a two-handler logger renders the traceback twice per bad record, documented in the method. No recursion path: `traceback.format_exception` does not log. **Pattern siblings** need no separate fix — protocol-envelope, transcript-model and note-model exceptions all flow through this ONE filter instance, and `test_both_production_handlers_share_the_fixed_tripwire` pins that both production handlers carry the same object. Scope expansion beyond Task 1.4's `:57-87` was operator-approved before this leg. **Invariant demonstrated, not asserted**: the two new channel tests were run against the PRE-FIX scan surface (`_scan_text` temporarily reduced to `record.getMessage()`) and BOTH FAILED — `dropped_record_count()` did not move (2 == 2, not 3) and pytest's captured log showed the traceback that would have been written, carrying `input_value={'span_text': 'SECRETMARK...`, which is exactly the leak the peer reported and the thing I could not reproduce in leg 1. The probe edit was reverted immediately and left no residue (`git diff` carries only intended hunks; HEAD unmoved at `f7bff5c`). Also in this leg, from my own leg-1 finding: the module docstring's over-claim ("even misuse of the stdlib logger cannot leak payload content") is replaced with a precise statement of what the tripwire does and does not guarantee — it catches content arriving with a REGISTERED SIGNATURE in message, traceback or stack, and it cannot detect bare clinical text with no signature (`logger.info("%s", span.span_text)`), which is why `log_event`'s whitelist is the primary control and this is the backstop. Four new tests in `tests/test_logging_setup.py`: the note-validation traceback reproduction, the `stack_info` channel, a clean-operational-exception negative control (the fix must not drop every error log), and the shared-filter sibling assertion. Suites: ruff clean, mypy strict clean on 24 source files, pytest 651 passed (647 before).
- /fix date: 2026-08-04
- /fix applied by: Claude Code

(The peer's round records 645 passed / 2 skipped under a workspace-local pytest temp root; those two execute and pass here, so the peer's run had two tests that never ran.)
- **PR-HIGH-001**
  - Peer's original labels, recorded so drift stays measurable: severity **HIGH** · triage **Fix-now** · fix route premium-only · classification 🆕
  - Verified materiality: **behavioral** — the correct fix changes what the filter scans, so it is not docs-only; it is not a regression (nothing in Phase 3A altered the filter's scan surface); it is not invalid.
  - Verified severity: **med** — a DOWNGRADE from the peer's HIGH, on the live-exposure fact below. Recording the borderline honestly: a reviewer who weights "a control that does not do what its own docstring promises, in a Critical-Constraint area" over "no reachable path today" could justifiably hold this at HIGH. The composer disposes; this is my read, not a ruling.
  - Evidence (what I actually observed): **mechanism CONFIRMED, empirical claim NOT independently reproduced.** `PayloadTripwireFilter.filter()` (`logging_setup.py:142-152`) builds its scan text from `record.getMessage()` alone, and the tripwire is attached with `addFilter` on both the rotating-file and stderr handlers (`:147`, `:158`) — the pre-format position — so any content reaching a handler's output solely through `exc_info` / `stack_info` rendering is never scanned and cannot increment the drop counter. I could NOT run the peer's reproduction: script execution is not granted in this activation (venv python, PowerShell, and `python3` against a scratchpad script were each denied), so the peer's specific `dropped_delta=0` + leaked-span-text observation stands as the PEER's evidence, not mine, and the concrete rendering of clinical text into a traceback — which depends on the raising site (interpolated values, pydantic's input echo, or a rendered source line) — is unverified here. The filter gap itself is unconditional and does not depend on that detail.
  - Refutation attempts that failed to refute it: `logger.error("...: %s", exc)` IS covered, because the exception text lands inside `getMessage()` — the gap is specific to the `exc_info`/`stack_info` channel; moving the filter to logger level would not help, since logger filters are also pre-format; and no third-party record can arrive carrying `exc_info`, because `setup_logging` attaches these handlers to a NAMED logger with `propagate = False` and nothing attaches them to root.
  - Live-exposure fact — the basis for the downgrade: a repo-wide search of `desktop/src` for `.exception(`, `exc_info`, `stack_info`, `excepthook`, `traceback` returns exactly ONE hit, `session_store.py:402` — the parameter name in `def __exit__(self, *exc_info: object)`, which never logs. There is **no exception-logging call site in the shipping code today**, so the defect is a latent gap in a defence-in-depth control, not a leak in flight. What makes it worth fixing rather than accepting is the module docstring's active over-claim — "even misuse of the stdlib logger cannot leak payload content" (`logging_setup.py:3-12`) — which invites precisely the `logger.exception(...)` that Phase 7's `TaskThread` failure paths would naturally introduce.
  - Fix-induced by Phase 3A? **No — pre-existing.** The filter body is unchanged Phase-1-of-project code (Step 4); Phase 3A Task 1.4 only APPENDED strings to `_PAYLOAD_SIGNATURES`. In this phase's terms the finding is 🆕 pre-existing, not ⚡.
  - Inside Phase 1's declared task list? **No.** Task 1.4's declared scope is the signature list (`logging_setup.py:57-87`); the scan surface is the filter body (`:138-152`), which no Phase 1 task names. Fixing it is a small scope expansion against pre-existing code, not completion of a declared task — the routing decision is the composer's.
  - Production-impacting? **No.** No new runtime dependency, environment variable, migration, deploy-side config, or dev/prod parity drift; the fix is an in-process change to one filter method plus its test.
  - Process note (my own miss, recorded rather than hidden): rounds 1–2 treated `logging_setup.py` as a changed file but scoped the missed-issue pass to changed LINES. The mismatch between the docstring's promise and the filter's scan surface sits in unchanged lines of a changed file — exactly the blind spot a cross-family peer exists to close, and it did.

#### PR-MED-001: `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/logging_setup.py:190` — `_scan_text` can miss the exception text the production formatter actually emits
- Classification: ⚡ Fix-induced
- Triage: Fix-now
- Fix route: premium-only
- Why it matters: the Round-3 fix promises to scan the complete text a handler can persist, but `logging.Formatter` treats an existing `record.exc_text` as its authoritative exception cache and otherwise formats the exact three-element `record.exc_info` tuple. The filter currently does neither in all states. A record can therefore pass the tripwire and write a registered clinical signature to the plaintext rotating log, so the fix has not fully restored the critical "no clinical content in logs" invariant.
- Current behaviour: when `record.exc_info` is present, `_scan_text` renders only `exc_info[1]` and reaches `record.exc_text` through an `elif`. A production-handler probe with a clean `OSError` in `exc_info` and `span_text='clinical payload'` in the already-populated `exc_text` produced `dropped_delta=0` and wrote the signature. A second probe detached the exception object's own traceback while retaining the original traceback in the tuple: `_scan_text` saw no signature, while the standard formatter rendered the tuple's signature-bearing source line. In both probes `_scan_text` correctly left `record.exc_text` untouched; the defect is selection, not cache mutation.
- Desired behaviour: scan any existing `record.exc_text` without modifying it, and render the exact `record.exc_info` tuple (not only its exception value) when deriving the uncached traceback. Scanning both is an acceptable conservative superset; no filter path may populate or replace the formatter-owned cache.
- Pattern to follow: mirror the standard formatter's exception inputs side-effect-free — use the complete `(type, value, traceback)` tuple and treat a non-empty cached `exc_text` as output-bearing text — while retaining the existing message and `stack_info` scan.
- Pattern siblings: rotating-file and stderr handlers share this one filter instance, so one central fix covers both; protocol-envelope, transcript-model, and note-model exception records all use the same path. `stack_info` is already scanned correctly and needs no sibling change.
- Invariant: if a registered signature appears in the message, cached exception text, exact `exc_info` rendering, or stack rendering that either production handler can emit, that handler drops the record; `_scan_text` never writes `record.exc_text`.
- Verification: add one production-handler regression with both clean `exc_info` and signature-bearing pre-populated `exc_text`, plus one synthetic exact-tuple traceback whose exception object's own `__traceback__` differs; assert both records drop, the log stays empty of the marker, and `record.exc_text` is object/value-identical before and after filtering. Retain the clean-operational-exception negative control and shared-filter assertion; run ruff, mypy, and the full suite.
- Regression risk: scanning cached and exact-tuple forms can duplicate traceback work and conservatively drop an operational traceback whose source text contains a registered field name. That is preferable to a plaintext leak, but the existing clean-`OSError` negative control must remain so the fix does not become "drop every error". No function signature or caller changes.
- /fix decision: Applied
- /fix notes: `logging_setup.py:_scan_text` — two changes, exactly the `Desired behaviour` line: `elif record.exc_text` became a separate `if`, so a populated cache is scanned whether or not `exc_info` is present; and `format_exception(exc_info[1])` became `format_exception(*exc_info)`, so the EXACT tuple is rendered rather than the exception object's own `__traceback__`. Both inputs are now scanned as the conservative superset the finding sanctions. `record.exc_text` is still never written — the docstring now states why both are read and that the two can diverge. **Invariant demonstrated, not asserted:** both new tests were run against the pre-fix selection logic and BOTH FAILED, reproducing the peer's two probes with my own evidence — (a) `dropped_delta=0` with `span_text='PRECACHEDMARKERXYZ'` in the captured output, (b) `dropped_delta=0` while the formatter printed the tuple's frame `raise RuntimeError("operational failure")  # span_text='left knee is tender'`. That closes the leg-1 gap where these were the peer's evidence and not mine. The probe edit was reverted and left no residue. Two tests added per the `Verification` line — a production-handler record carrying clean `exc_info` plus signature-bearing pre-populated `exc_text` (asserting the drop, the marker's absence from the log, and `record.exc_text is` the same object after filtering), and a detached-`__traceback__` record whose signature exists ONLY in a traceback frame's source line so nothing but the exact-tuple rendering can catch it. The clean-`OSError` negative control and the shared-filter assertion are retained and still pass, so the fix did not become "drop every error". No signature or caller change. Suites: ruff clean, mypy strict clean on 24 files, pytest 653 passed at this point.
- /fix date: 2026-08-04
- /fix applied by: Claude Code

#### PR-MED-002: `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/note.py:982` — non-faithful mock behaviours can silently collapse to `faithful`
- Classification: 🆕 Pre-existing
- Triage: Fix-now
- Fix route: fix-on-fast
- Why it matters: `MockNoteModelProvider` is the adversarial instrument for the Phase-3A safety matrix. If a requested failure behaviour returns byte-identical output to `faithful`, that matrix cell can go green while exercising no failure at all — the same evidence-corruption class Round 1 fixed for capitalised laterality and one-utterance over-omission. The affected inputs are realistic: zero pain/dose values, decimal values beginning with zero, and a patient named Wilson.
- Current behaviour: `_change_dose` returns after replacing the first digit run with twice its integer value, so `0` and the leading `0` in `0.5` remain unchanged; `_substitute_name` returns after replacing `Wilson` with `Wilson`; and `fabricated_fact` replaces the first assertion with the fixed fabricated sentence even when that sentence is already the transcript assertion. Post-fix probes confirmed `dose_change("0 mg daily")`, `dose_change("0.5 mg daily")`, `name_substitution("Wilson reports pain")`, and a transcript equal to `_FABRICATED_TEXT` all produce output identical to `faithful` without raising.
- Desired behaviour: every non-`faithful` behaviour must either produce output unequal to `faithful` or raise `NoteProviderError` because the fixture cannot express that failure class. No mutator may stop at a candidate that leaves the assertion unchanged when a later token or utterance could be changed.
- Pattern to follow: the existing loud-failure rules in `_mutate` and the `over_omission` branch — continue searching past inapplicable/no-op candidates, then raise only when no real mutation is possible.
- Pattern siblings: `_change_dose`, `_substitute_name`, and the `fabricated_fact` branch are confirmed siblings. `_flip_laterality` always swaps to the opposite token; `_flip_negation` always deletes a matched token; the remaining behaviours add/remove/change structure and did not share the no-op root cause.
- Invariant: for any request, a non-`faithful` mock behaviour either returns sections unequal to `MockNoteModelProvider("faithful")` or raises `NoteProviderError`; it never silently succeeds with faithful output.
- Verification: add parameterized regressions for `0`, `0.5`, an initial `Wilson`, and transcript text exactly equal to `_FABRICATED_TEXT`; include inputs with a no-op candidate followed by a mutable candidate to prove the scan continues. Keep the all-behaviours determinism and non-faithful-differs tests, then run ruff, mypy, and the full suite.
- Regression risk: mock provider and adversarial fixtures only — no shipping app code constructs this provider. Changes can alter expected Axis-B fixture text, so update exact expected outputs rather than weakening the invariant to warning-code-only assertions.
- /fix decision: Applied
- /fix notes: `note.py` — fixed at BOTH levels my leg-1 tuple identified. Level 2 (the choke point): `_mutate` now rejects a mutation whose rejoined text equals the original and KEEPS SCANNING, raising only when no utterance yields a real change. Level 1 (the mutators): `_change_dose` walks every digit run via `finditer` and skips runs that double to themselves; `_substitute_name` skips a token that already IS `_SUBSTITUTE_NAME`; and `fabricated_fact` moved into `_fabricate`, which retexts the first assertion that differs from `_FABRICATED_TEXT` and raises when none does. `_MUTATION_REQUIREMENT` wording updated so the raise message stays true ("a numeric token that changes when doubled", "a name-like token other than Wilson"); `_SUBSTITUTE_NAME` moved above the mapping that now interpolates it. **A probe established which level carries which guarantee, rather than my assuming both were needed:** reverting ONLY `_change_dose` to its pre-fix form left exactly ONE test failing — the `0.5` mutability case — while the zero-dose raise, the no-op-then-mutable scan, and the whole 72-cell invariant matrix still passed. So the `_mutate` guard is the SAFETY invariant (never silently faithful, and it generalises to any mutator Phase 5 adds), and the mutator changes are the CAPABILITY fix (they recover a genuinely mutable dose, `0.5` → `0.10`, instead of skipping the utterance). Neither is redundant, and neither is dead code. Probe reverted, no residue. Tests added per the `Verification` line: a parameterized invariant over every non-`faithful` behaviour × six fixtures (72 cells) asserting each either differs from `faithful` or raises `NoteProviderError`, plus targeted regressions for `0`, `0.5`, an initial `Wilson`, a transcript equal to `_FABRICATED_TEXT`, and a no-op-followed-by-mutable pair proving the scan continues. Exact expected outputs were updated, not weakened. Suites: ruff clean, mypy strict clean on 24 files, pytest 730 passed.

- **PR-MED-001** — `logging_setup.py:188-196`
  - Peer's original labels: severity **MED** · triage **Fix-now** · fix route premium-only · classification **⚡ fix-induced**
  - Verified materiality: **behavioral** — a code change is needed; but see the regression note below, because "fix-induced" and "regression" are not the same thing here.
  - Verified severity: **low** — a DOWNGRADE from the peer's MED. I am the author of the code under review, so weight this accordingly; my reasoning is below and the composer should feel free to restore MED.
  - Evidence (established by inspection): CONFIRMED, both halves. (a) `:192` reaches `record.exc_text` through an `elif`, so when `exc_info` is present the cached text is never scanned — plainly what `elif` does. (b) `:191` renders `traceback.format_exception(exc_info[1])`, which walks `value.__traceback__`, while `logging.Formatter.format()` uses the cached `exc_text` when set and otherwise `formatException(record.exc_info)` on the **full three-element tuple**. So when `exc_info[2]` and `exc_info[1].__traceback__` differ, filter and formatter read different objects. The peer's characterisation is exact, including that `_scan_text` never mutates the cache — the defect is SELECTION, not cache mutation.
  - **Not a regression against the pre-fix baseline — stated precisely rather than used as a defence.** Before Round 3 nothing beyond `getMessage()` was scanned at all, so no state got worse; this is an INCOMPLETE fix, not a fix that broke something. The peer's ⚡ label is still fair in `/review`'s sense — the code path is new this session — and I accept it.
  - Basis for the downgrade to low: reaching the gap needs THREE things at once — (i) an exception-logging call site, of which `desktop/src` still has exactly zero (re-checked this leg; the only `exc_info` hit remains `session_store.py:402`, a parameter name that never logs); (ii) a divergent `exc_text` or a hand-built mismatched `exc_info` tuple, which needs a custom formatter (none exists, none planned — `setup_logging` uses plain `logging.Formatter`) or deliberate manual construction; and (iii) clinical content in that divergent text. The peer's probe (a) is a genuine demonstration, but it CONSTRUCTED the leak state by hand-populating `exc_text` with content the exception never carried; it does not show a path that arises on its own.
  - **Disposition recommendation notwithstanding the severity: Fix now.** Severity is not priority. The correction is one branch (`elif` → `if`) plus passing the full tuple, and the alternative is knowingly leaving an incomplete control behind a docstring I rewrote last leg to claim completeness — which would re-create the exact over-claim that produced PR-HIGH-001.
  - Scope — inside the existing operator approval, NOT a new expansion. Reasoning: when disposing PR-HIGH-001 the operator approved expanding into `logging_setup.py`'s scan surface (`:138-152`) for the defect class *"the tripwire must scan what a handler can actually emit."* PR-MED-001 is that same class, at that same method, against that same invariant — it is the completion of the approved fix, not a new front. Nothing further is opened up: same file, same function, no new caller, no signature change. If the composer prefers the stricter reading that each finding needs its own nod, the cost of asking is trivial either way.
  - Production-impacting: **No.** No new dependency, env var, migration, deploy-side config, or parity drift.

- **PR-MED-002** — `note.py:982-983`, `:1034-1041`, `:1062-1069`, `:1078-1083`
  - Peer's original labels: severity **MED** · triage **Fix-now** · fix route fix-on-fast · classification **🆕 pre-existing**
  - Verified materiality: **behavioral**

  - Evidence (established by inspection): CONFIRMED, all four inputs. `_change_dose:1066` computes `str(int(match.group()) * 2)`, and `int("0") * 2 == 0`, so `"0"` is replaced by `"0"` and `:1068` returns as though it had succeeded; `_DIGITS_RE` is `\d+`, so on `"0.5"` it matches only the leading `"0"` and the same no-op fires. `_substitute_name:1081` assigns `_SUBSTITUTE_NAME` (`"Wilson"`, `:939`) over a name-like token, so a transcript whose name IS Wilson is replaced by itself and `:1082` returns. `fabricated_fact:983` retexts assertion[0] to `_FABRICATED_TEXT` unconditionally, so a transcript equal to that constant is unchanged. In every case the behaviour returns faithful output and raises nothing.
  - **The defect is at two levels, and a fix that only patches the mutators would be incomplete:** `_mutate:1034-1041` stops at the first assertion whose mutator returned non-`None`, so a no-op "success" also prevents it from reaching a LATER utterance that could be genuinely mutated. My Round 1 notes claim "Pattern siblings … Checked; no sibling carries the defect." I checked the siblings for the CASE-SENSITIVITY bug, not for the general no-op-replacement class, and three sites carried it. That is a pattern-sibling miss, and 🔁 is the label that records it usefully.

A behaviour that silently emits faithful output does not produce its Axis B behaviour, so this is completion of a declared task, not an expansion.

#### PR-MED-001: `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/note.py:1030` — byte-different mock output can still fail to produce its named adversarial behaviour
- Classification: 🔁 Same-family
- Triage: Fix-now
- Fix route: fix-on-fast
- Why it matters: `MockNoteModelProvider` is the oracle-driving adversarial instrument for the Phase-5 matrix. A formatting-only change can satisfy the new `other != faithful` guard while producing neither a fabricated fact nor a substituted name; the checker can then fire on exact reconstruction and let a matrix cell appear exercised by the wrong failure class.
- Current behaviour: `_fabricate` compares raw strings, so a transcript assertion equal to `_FABRICATED_TEXT` except for terminal punctuation (the realistic `"...last Tuesday."`) is replaced by the same clinical statement without the full stop and reported as `fabricated_fact`. `_substitute_name` skips only the raw token `"Wilson"`; `"Wilson,"` or `"WILSON"` is recognised as name-like and replaced by `"Wilson"`, changing punctuation/case but not the name. `_mutate` then sees byte inequality at `:1060` and stops before any later genuinely substitutable name. The 72-cell regression asserts only inequality or `NoteProviderError`, so all of these states pass it.
- Desired behaviour: each named mock behaviour either makes a class-specific semantic change or raises `NoteProviderError`; punctuation/case-only changes must count as inapplicable and must not stop the search for a later valid target.
- Pattern to follow: use the existing shared normalisation when deciding whether fabricated content or the substitute name is already present; preserve surrounding punctuation when replacing a genuinely different name, and continue scanning after semantically unchanged candidates just as `_change_dose` now continues after zero runs.
- Pattern siblings: confirmed at `_fabricate`, `_substitute_name`, and the `_mutate` choke point. Laterality, dose and negation make class-specific token changes; structural add/remove behaviours are not the same root cause for the Phase-1 fixture contract.
- Invariant: a successful non-faithful mock result must carry the requested Axis-B failure class, not merely serialize differently from faithful output.
- Verification: add regressions for `_FABRICATED_TEXT + "."`, `"Wilson, reports pain"`, `"WILSON reports pain"`, and a Wilson-with-punctuation utterance followed by a genuinely different later name; assert raise-or-class-specific change and that the later target is reached. Strengthen the relevant matrix oracle beyond raw model inequality, then retain determinism and run the normal Phase-1 QA commands.
- Regression risk: mock/fixture output only; exact expected strings may change because a real name substitution should preserve punctuation. No shipping caller constructs this provider.
- /fix decision: Applied
- /fix notes: VERIFIED FIRST (this leg verifies before fixing): all three claims hold by inspection — `_fabricate` compared raw strings, `_substitute_name` skipped only the exact token `"Wilson"`, and `_mutate`'s guard was raw equality. Fixed as a CLASS, not three instances. One predicate, `_same_statement(left, right)`, built on the existing shared `content_tokens` normalisation, now answers "is this the same clinical statement?" at every site: `_fabricate`, `_substitute_name` (which also preserves surrounding punctuation on a real substitution, like `_flip_laterality`), and the `_mutate` choke point — so a punctuation/case/whitespace-only change counts as inapplicable and the scan CONTINUES to a later valid target instead of stopping. **Plus a structural backstop at the single exit of `generate_sections`:** every non-`faithful` result is fingerprinted against the faithful one — (section, content tokens, `source_coords`) — and a behaviour that did not distinguish itself raises rather than returning faithful output under an adversarial label. That is what closes the class rather than the instance: any behaviour a later phase adds inherits the guarantee at the exit, without having to remember this history. The fingerprint is deliberately blind to punctuation/case and deliberately SENSITIVE to coordinates, since a correctly-worded assertion citing the wrong interval is a genuine failure class. **A probe measured each layer:** reverting all three per-site predicates left the backstop catching every case — the only failures were which MESSAGE was raised, not bad output escaping. So the backstop is load-bearing for the class; the per-site predicates buy a precise diagnosis and, crucially, let the scan reach a later valid target instead of aborting the whole generation. Both probes reverted, no residue. Tests: the four regressions the `Verification` line names (`_FABRICATED_TEXT + "."`, `"Wilson,"`, `"WILSON"`, and a cosmetic-Wilson utterance followed by a genuinely different later name, asserting the later target is reached with its punctuation preserved), plus a lowercase-`wilson` case; the matrix oracle is strengthened from raw model inequality to the same statement-level fingerprint, written out INDEPENDENTLY in the test file so a bug in the provider's own comparator cannot hide itself; the matrix grew to 12 behaviours × 8 fixtures. Suites: ruff clean, mypy strict clean on 24 files, pytest 772 passed.
- /fix date: 2026-08-04
- /fix applied by: Claude Code

#### PR-LOW-001: `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/logging_setup.py:197` — `_scan_text` still selects fewer `exc_info` states than the production formatter
- Classification: ⚡ Fix-induced
- Triage: Fix-now
- Fix route: premium-only
- Why it matters: the tripwire now claims to mirror the standard formatter's exception inputs. A truthy, explicitly supplied `exc_info` tuple whose value slot is `None` can carry a signature in its traceback frame; the filter skips the tuple, then the production formatter renders and persists it to the plaintext rotating log.
- Current behaviour: `_scan_text` renders only when `exc_info is not None and exc_info[1] is not None`. The Python 3.14 formatter used by both handlers checks only `if record.exc_info`, then calls `formatException(record.exc_info)` and appends the resulting `exc_text`. `Logger._log` preserves an explicitly supplied tuple, so `(RuntimeError, None, tb)` is a reachable record state. The filter does correctly leave `record.exc_text` untouched; this is a third selection mismatch, not a cache mutation.
- Desired behaviour: mirror the formatter's truthiness selection for `exc_info` and render the exact tuple side-effect-free, while continuing to scan any independent pre-cached `exc_text` and never assigning the cache.
- Pattern to follow: use the same truthy `record.exc_info` predicate as `logging.Formatter.format`, retain `traceback.format_exception(*exc_info)`, the separate cached-text `if`, and the existing fail-closed exception guard.
- Pattern siblings: both production handlers share this filter and formatter, so the central predicate fix covers file and stderr. Message, cached exception text, ordinary tuple rendering and stack selection were independently re-read and need no sibling change.
- Invariant: for every exception state the standard production formatter renders, any registered signature in the exact tuple rendering or pre-cached exception text causes the record to be dropped; filtering never writes `record.exc_text`.
- Verification: construct a production-handler record with a truthy three-element `exc_info` tuple whose value is `None` and whose tuple traceback has a registered signature only in its source line; assert the record drops, neither handler persists the marker, and `record.exc_text` remains unchanged. Retain the clean exception, divergent-cache, exact-tuple, stack, and shared-filter controls.
- Regression risk: malformed truthy tuples may now fail during side-effect-free rendering and be conservatively dropped by the existing fail-closed guard. There are still no shipping exception-logging call sites, so live exposure is lower than the earlier gaps; no caller signature changes.
- /fix decision: Applied
- /fix notes: VERIFIED FIRST: confirmed by inspection — the filter's predicate was `exc_info is not None and exc_info[1] is not None` while `logging.Formatter.format` tests `if record.exc_info:`, so a truthy `(RuntimeError, None, tb)` (reachable by passing `exc_info=` explicitly, which `Logger._log` preserves) was skipped by the filter and rendered by the formatter. Fixed as a CLASS: the three predicates are now COPIED from the formatter, not re-derived — `if record.exc_info:` / `if record.exc_text:` / `if record.stack_info:` — with a comment stating that parity, never a new special case, is the rule here, because rounds 3, 4 and 5 were all the same class (a scan condition narrower than the formatter's). **Completeness argument, which is what round 6 should test:** `Formatter.format` appends exactly four things — the formatted message, `exc_text`, the rendered `exc_info`, and `stack_info` — and all four are now scanned on the formatter's own predicates, making the scan a superset of what either handler can emit in every record state. That claim rests on two preconditions now written into the docstring: both handlers share ONE stock `logging.Formatter`, and its format string interpolates only `asctime`/`levelname`/`name`/`message`. A later phase that adds a custom formatter, a second formatter, or a record factory must revisit `_scan_text`. Rendering the record through a real formatter instead of mirroring it was considered and REJECTED: `formatException` writes `record.exc_text`, so it would need a record copy — trading a tested predicate mirror for a subtler one, against an explicit invariant. **Invariant demonstrated:** the new test was run against the pre-fix predicate and FAILED (`filter()` returned True on the truthy-tuple record); probe reverted, no residue. The no-cache-write half is asserted against the FILTER directly rather than after a full `handle()`, because pytest's own capture handler formats the record once ours refuses it and formatting is what legitimately populates the cache — an artifact of the harness, not of this filter. The clean-exception, divergent-cache, exact-tuple, stack and shared-filter controls are all retained and pass. Suites: ruff clean, mypy strict clean on 24 files, pytest 772 passed.
- /fix date: 2026-08-04
- /fix applied by: Claude Code

- Source: Codex peer-review

- Findings: 0 CRIT / 0 HIGH / 1 MED / 1 LOW
- Classification: 0 🆕 pre-existing / 0 ⚡ fix-induced / 2 🔁 same-family
- Finding verification: 4 candidates; 2 dropped; 0 downgraded
  - "`complete_session` accepts a parsed note carrying blocking warnings" — DROPPED as assigned later work rather than a Phase-1 custody defect: Task 1.5 owns decrypt/parse/session/digest custody verification, while the later write/action gates own unresolved-warning enforcement; no shipping note writer exists in this state.
  - "A corrupt or unreadable audio header falls back to the directory name for note binding" — DROPPED on the permitted Phase-1 contract: `_resolve_session_identity()` makes a readable header authoritative, uses the well-formed directory name as the documented fallback, and fails closed only when neither yields an identity.
- Claim 1 verdict: PARTLY CONFIRMED. Against the installed CPython 3.14.6 implementation, the copied `exc_info` / `exc_text` / `stack_info` predicates are faithful and conservatively scan both a rendered tuple and an independent cache. Both production handlers do share one stock `logging.Formatter`, and no Phase-1 change breaks that topology. The wider completeness claim is false, however: the formatter's base string also interpolates `levelname` and `name`, while `_scan_text()` scans only `record.getMessage()`; PR-LOW-001 is a concrete formatter-visible state that escapes.
- Claim 2 verdict: NOT CONFIRMED. The single-exit fingerprint makes silent equivalence to `faithful` unrepresentable under its chosen relation, but that relation proves only "different section/tokens/coordinates," not "the named Axis-B failure class occurred." Current behaviours reach the exit with wrong-class or structurally invalid output; PR-MED-001 records the class-level gap. The fingerprint also omits `speaker`, so it would reject a future speaker-attribution behaviour even though that difference is clinically material.
- Invented-text judgment: the executor's outcome is usually right but its stated reason is not sufficient. With normal word-tokenized requests, each fixed multiword `invented_*` assertion cites only the first transcript word, so exact-coordinate reconstruction still fails even when the same sentence appears elsewhere in the transcript; that coordinate mismatch is what preserves the unsupported-assertion class. Merely landing duplicate text in a clinician-owned section does not itself make the assertion unsupported, and the model types permit a manually authored one-word `TranscriptWord` containing the whole phrase, so a behaviour-specific postcondition remains the sound proof.
- Invariant re-check: proposals remain excluded from sections; transcript assertions retain one contiguous coordinate interval; non-transcript assertions require confirmed matching evidence; note decrypt/parse/session/digest failures all precede key deletion; explicit note deletion retains custody on unlink failure; note-model repr/JSON families retain tripwire signatures. No new custody or model-shape finding survived verification.
- Downstream breakage check: `complete_session`'s keyword-only addition leaves existing callers unchanged; the note models and providers have no shipping caller yet; both log handlers share the same formatter/filter topology. The two findings are therefore localized to the logging backstop and the test-only adversarial provider, with no additional caller regression found.
- Validation: static review plus three read-only, bytecode-disabled probes; no pytest run and no repository temp path created. The logging probe showed `PayloadTripwireFilter.filter()` returning `True` while the configured formatter emitted a registered signature from `record.name`. Mock probes showed `obeys_injection` returning a management-plan assertion for an ordinary clinician examination with no injected instruction, and `negation_flip` returning `span_text=''`, which fails fresh `NoteSpan` validation. `git diff --check` is clean apart from the known LF→CRLF notices. Executor-reported baseline retained: ruff clean, mypy strict clean on 24 files, pytest 772 passed.

#### PR-MED-001: `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/note.py:1016` — the structural fingerprint proves difference, not the requested adversarial failure class
- Classification: 🔁 Same-family
- Triage: Fix-now
- Fix route: premium-only
- Why it matters: `MockNoteModelProvider` is the safety matrix's adversarial instrument. A cell that returns a different result for the wrong reason can exercise the wrong checker—or no checker—and still look like valid evidence that the named Axis-B class was generated. The round-5 backstop closes semantic no-ops, but not this broader recurring class.
- Current behaviour: the exit accepts any change to section, normalized content tokens, or coordinates. Existing counterexamples are reachable: `dose_change` changes the default fixture's pain score (`3 out of ten`) despite there being no dose; `obeys_injection` blindly duplicates the last utterance into `management_plan` even when the request contains no injection (an ordinary confirmed-clinician examination returns successfully); and `negation_flip` on a one-token `"not"` utterance deletes the only token, then `_retext()` uses validation-skipping `model_copy()` to return an invalid blank `NoteSpan`. Conversely, the relation ignores `speaker`, so a legitimate future speaker-misattribution behaviour would be rejected as indistinguishable when its text, section, and coordinates stay fixed.
- Desired behaviour: keep the fingerprint as a no-op backstop, but require every behaviour to prove its own applicability and class-specific postcondition before the common exit. An inapplicable behaviour must raise `NoteProviderError`; no behaviour other than the deliberately malformed one may return a model that fails fresh validation. If future behaviours are meant to inherit a generic guard, the registered equivalence/projection must include every material dimension they are allowed to change, including speaker attribution, rather than claiming the current three-field fingerprint is universal.
- Pattern to follow: pair each behaviour with a small class-specific validator—dose context plus numeric change, an actual injected-instruction utterance selected from the request, a nonblank validated result after negation removal, exact coordinate non-reconstruction for invented content—and run that validator immediately before the shared fingerprint check. Reconstruct changed pydantic models through validated constructors (or `model_validate`) instead of `model_copy(update=...)` at the adversarial mutation boundary.
- Pattern siblings: `_change_dose`, `_injected_assertion`, `_flip_negation` + `_retext`, all four `_invented_assertion` behaviours, and any later behaviour that changes `speaker`, provenance, or section structure without changing the current fingerprint fields.
- Invariant: a successful non-`faithful` mock result carries the specifically requested Axis-B failure class and is structurally valid unless the requested class is `malformed_output`; byte/semantic inequality from `faithful` is necessary but not sufficient.
- Verification: add independent-oracle regressions showing that a pain score/date/measurement without a dose is inapplicable to `dose_change` (or rename and re-contract the behaviour as a general numeric change); an ordinary final utterance is inapplicable to `obeys_injection`, while a non-final injected utterance is found and used; a lone-negation utterance raises rather than returning a blank span; every successful non-malformed result round-trips through fresh pydantic validation; and a deliberately added speaker-only test behaviour is either represented by the generic projection or explicitly registered with its own oracle. Add an invented-text fixture whose exact text is already reconstructible at the cited coordinates and assert raise-or-genuine unsupported output.
- Regression risk: mock/fixture code only—no shipping caller constructs this provider—but expected matrix outputs and error messages will change, and tightening applicability may expose fixtures that never contained the class their label claimed to test.
- Scope-expansion disposition: In scope — this is the completion contract of Phase 1 Task 1.3 and the explicit class-closure claim made by the round-5 fix.

#### PR-LOW-001: `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/logging_setup.py:211` — `_scan_text` still omits fields emitted by the configured formatter
- Classification: 🔁 Same-family
- Triage: Fix-now
- Fix route: premium-only
- Why it matters: the tripwire now makes an explicit completeness claim, and future logging work can safely rely on that claim only if every formatter-visible signature-bearing field is actually scanned. Plaintext log output remains a forbidden clinical-data flow even when a record carries the signature outside its message/traceback channels.
- Current behaviour: CPython 3.14.6 computes the base output with `formatMessage(record)`. The configured format is `%(asctime)s %(levelname)s %(name)s %(message)s`, but `_scan_text()` starts with only `record.getMessage()`. A `LogRecord` whose name is `span_text='left knee'` and whose message is `routine event` passes the filter while the stock production formatter emits the registered signature. The same gap exists for a custom `levelname`. Current shipping logger names and standard levels are fixed and non-clinical, so reachability is lower than the exception-channel defects and the severity is LOW; nevertheless, the stated preconditions do not make the completeness proof true.
- Desired behaviour: either scan/validate every non-generated field in the configured base format (`name` and `levelname`, with `asctime` recognized as formatter-generated), or narrow the claim and enforce as code-level preconditions that logger names and level names are fixed non-clinical values. Pin the exact shared formatter topology so a format-field change cannot silently widen output beyond the scan again.
- Pattern to follow: centralize the production format string and its permitted record fields, include `record.name` and `record.levelname` in `_scan_text()` (or validate them before handler construction), and add a regression that compares filter disposition with the actual shared formatter output for signatures placed one at a time in every interpolated record field. Keep the now-correct `exc_info` / `exc_text` / `stack_info` predicate parity unchanged.
- Pattern siblings: rotating-file and stderr handlers share the gap; `record.name` and `record.levelname` are the omitted formatter inputs. `asctime` is generated from the clock and `message` is already scanned. No custom formatter, record factory, propagating parent, or third-party handler exists in the current production topology.
- Invariant: under the declared production formatter contract, no record whose actual formatted output contains a registered payload/clinical signature reaches either handler.
- Verification: construct records with the signature only in `name` and only in a custom level name, run them through the production tripwire and formatter, and assert both are dropped; assert both handlers share the same stock formatter object and the pinned format fields; retain the full exception-cache/tuple/stack suite and clean-record controls.
- Regression risk: validating logger names can reject an accidentally dynamic setup name, while scanning custom level names can conservatively drop a record; shipping uses only `scribe-app` / `scribe-host` and standard levels, so no current caller should change.
- Scope-expansion disposition: In scope — `logging_setup.py`'s Phase 1 Task 1.4 tripwire and the round-5 class-closure claim are the reviewed surface.
- /fix decision: Applied
- /fix notes: CONFIRMED first: `_scan_text` started from `record.getMessage()` alone while the configured format also interpolates `levelname` and `name`, so a record named `span_text='left knee'` passed the filter and the formatter emitted the signature. This is the FOURTH instance of one class, so the fix is not a fifth hand-added case — **the base-field list is no longer written down at all.** `LOG_FORMAT` is now a module constant used BOTH to build the handlers' single formatter and to derive `_SCANNED_FIELDS` (`_interpolated_fields()` parses `%(field)s` tokens out of it), and `_scan_text` sweeps every derived field. Adding a field to the format automatically adds it to the scan; a hand-maintained list is precisely how this recurred four times. `asctime` and `message` are the two documented exclusions: the formatter REGENERATES `asctime` from the clock (so a caller cannot smuggle content through it — now asserted by test, not assumed), and `message` is covered by `getMessage()`. The round-3/4/5 `exc_info`/`exc_text`/`stack_info` predicate parity is untouched. **Completeness claim, restated — and the previous one was partly false: round 5's docstring enumerated the interpolated fields CORRECTLY and then drew a false conclusion from them, claiming a superset while scanning only `message`.** The new claim: every channel `Formatter.format` emits is scanned — the interpolated base fields (derived, so they cannot drift), `exc_text`, the rendered `exc_info`, `stack_info` — each on the formatter's own predicate. Preconditions, all now pinned by tests rather than prose: both handlers are built by `setup_logging` from `LOG_FORMAT` and share ONE stock `logging.Formatter` object; `asctime` is formatter-generated. A custom formatter, a second format string, a `LogRecord` factory, or a propagating parent handler would each break the claim. **Invariant demonstrated:** the new class regression was run against the pre-fix scan and FAILED on `levelname`; probe reverted, no residue. Three tests added — the class regression (for EVERY derived field, assert the signature is visible in the real formatter's output AND the filter drops it), the `asctime`-is-regenerated control, and the shared-formatter/pinned-format assertion. Suites: ruff clean, mypy strict clean on 24 files, pytest 775 passed.
- /fix date: 2026-08-04
- /fix applied by: Claude Code

### Round 8 — 2026-08-04 — Phase 2 (Tasks 2.1–2.2), independent cross-family peer review
- Source: Codex peer-review
- Round status: Closed
- Files reviewed: `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/transcription.py`, `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/note.py`, `desktop/tests/test_transcription.py`, `desktop/tests/test_note.py`; plan context limited to Phase 2, Critical Constraints, and Validation / Verification as requested.
- Verification method: static code, test, caller, and type-boundary review; one in-memory numerical probe of the existing embedding algorithm. No pytest run; relied on the executor-reported ruff, mypy-strict, and 791-test results. No files or directories were created.
- **Task 2.1 correctness:** subtracting the 24-band mean removes the additive log-power gain component while preserving relative spectral-envelope shape. Keeping the low-band centroid outside that mean is sound: it is a separately-scaled power ratio, and mixing it into the mel mean would couple unlike units. The `1e-10` floors are far below realistic speech-band power and prevent non-finite values; their small residual does not invalidate the current feature or fixtures.
- **Task 2.1 test integrity:** independently reconstructed the pre- and post-fix feature paths. Before normalisation the loud/quiet comb embeddings differ by `4.6009…4.6104` in every mel band; after normalisation the maximum full-vector difference is `0.0052`. The three-segment fixture changes from pre-fix `[s1, s1, s2]` to `[s1, s2, s1]`, and the decisive 2×2 fixture changes from level clustering `[s1, s2, s2, s1]` to voice clustering `[s1, s2, s1, s2]`. All three new tests therefore genuinely fail before the fix for the claimed reason; none is an always-green fixture.
- **Task 2.2 role safety:** `SpeakerRolePreselection` is not a `str`, so a caller cannot pass the result itself as `clinician_speaker`; extracting `.preselected_clinician_speaker` is an explicit, visible promotion. Unresolved and merged results are `None`, and `_route` refuses every clinician-owned section when the request has no confirmed label or the utterance is interrogative. No shipping pipeline caller invokes `speaker_role` or `build_note_request` yet, so the authored weights currently decide no clinical content.
- Finding verification: 1 candidate; 1 dropped; 0 downgraded. *Candidate: the inner preselected label is still a raw string and could be fed directly to `build_note_request` without a runtime confirmation artifact.* Dropped as a current Phase-2 finding: the code and plan explicitly make that extraction visible rather than impossible, no current pipeline consumes it, and Task 7.5 already owns the mandatory per-session confirmation control and candidate quotations. This remains a binding Task-7.5 integration check, not a missed defect in Tasks 2.1–2.2.
- No new findings — converged.

#### PR-MED-001: `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/note_config.py:517` — profile binding is an optional helper, not a generation-time construction requirement
- Classification: 🔁 Same-family
- Triage: Fix-now
- Fix route: premium-only
- Why it matters: a profile identifier controls how canonical clinical sections will be mapped and which populated sections may be dropped. The Task 3.1 Done-when says a session cannot generate without a bound profile, but a regex-valid invented identifier can currently cross the request/provider boundary without ever being resolved against the config whose digest accompanies it. Phase 6 could therefore wire a perfectly type-checking caller that bypasses the only membership check and later attributes or maps the note under a profile that never existed.
- Current behaviour: `bind_template_profile()` validates zero/unknown/ambiguous cases only when a caller elects to call it. `note.build_note_request()` still accepts a raw `template_profile_id: str`, `NoteRequest` and `GeneratedNote` validate only its lexical pattern, and both providers generate sections without consulting the profile. For example, an empty `NoteConfig()` plus its valid digest does not prevent a request carrying `template_profile_id="ghost"` from being built and passed to `ExtractiveNoteProvider.generate_sections()`. The tests at `test_note_config.py:275-304` exercise the helper in isolation and therefore do not prove the stated generation invariant or the claimed single resolution point.
- Desired behaviour: make the generation-facing construction API require evidence produced by resolving a profile against the same resolved `NoteConfig`/`config_digest`; a freely typed identifier must not be enough. Zero profiles, an unknown explicit id, and an unchosen multi-profile config must fail before a provider can run. The sole-profile path should auto-bind, while only the multi-profile/no-id result may drive a chooser.
- Pattern to follow: the existing nominal boundaries in `note.py` — `NoteProposal` versus `NoteAssertion`, and `SpeakerRolePreselection` versus the confirmed speaker label — make an unsafe promotion explicit in the type/call graph. Use an equivalent bound-profile value or a config-owned request builder, then route the Phase 6 controller through that one API; do not rely on a docstring instruction to call `bind_template_profile()` first.
- Pattern siblings: `note.py:590-620` (`build_note_request` raw string input), `note.py:542-571` (`NoteRequest` lexical-only profile validation), and `note.py:456-496` (`GeneratedNote` lexical-only persistence). No shipping caller exists yet; the current direct call sites are tests.
- Invariant: every provider request and persisted note profile id was resolved from the exact config identified by its `config_digest`; no request reaches `generate_sections()` from a zero-profile, unknown-profile, or unresolved multi-profile state.
- Verification: add an end-to-end construction test proving `NoteConfig()` plus an arbitrary valid id cannot build a generation request; prove sole-profile auto-binding succeeds without a chooser; prove multi-profile/no-id and unknown-id fail before provider invocation; and prove a profile resolved from one config cannot be paired with another config's digest. Update the existing provider tests through the bound construction helper rather than fabricating profile strings.
- Regression risk: this changes a core test/helper signature and must avoid a `note.py` ↔ `note_config.py` import cycle. There are no shipping callers today, so the migration is contained; Phase 6 gains the enforced integration boundary it otherwise has to remember manually.
- Scope-expansion disposition: In scope — this is the explicit Task 3.1 Done-when and the construction claim made by the new `bind_template_profile` API.
- /fix decision: Applied
- /fix notes: Made the binding STRUCTURAL per the finding's Pattern-to-follow. `note_config.BoundTemplateProfile` (frozen, digest-pattern-validated) is the evidence type only `bind_template_profile` produces — it now returns the binding carrying THAT config's digest, so profile/digest cross-pairing is impossible without hand-construction in plain sight (the accepted visible-promotion convention, reserved for fixtures). New generation-facing `note_config.build_note_request(document, binding, ...)` is the sole public constructor; `note.build_note_request` renamed to package-private `_assemble_note_request`, removed from `__all__`, docstring redirects to the boundary (boundary lives config-side to avoid the import cycle the finding warned about). Pre-fix hole DEMONSTRATED by a transient probe test (invented id `never-configured` + unrelated digest reached `generate_sections()` — probe run passed, then probe reverted). Siblings: `note.py` raw-string builder FIXED (demoted); `NoteRequest` lexical-only validation DEFERRED — the type stays the persistence/fixture floor, membership cannot live on it without the cycle, carried by the sole public builder; `GeneratedNote` lexical-only persistence DEFERRED — Phase 6's write path receives the resolved pair from the same binding. Tests migrated through the bound helper (`test_note.py` `_TEST_CONFIG`/`_BINDING`; both former fabricated-string call sites); new `TestGenerationBoundary` + binding-digest tests pin the invariant (fabricated id cannot reach a request; sole profile binds without a chooser; unknown id fails before provider invocation; binding carries its own config's digest). Verified: full ruff clean / mypy strict 26 files / pytest 852 passed.
- /fix date: 2026-08-10
- /fix applied by: Claude Code

#### PR-MED-002: `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/note_config.py:130` — Unicode layout and direction controls bypass the single-line config validator
- Classification: 🔁 Same-family
- Triage: Fix-now
- Fix route: fix-on-fast
- Why it matters: autofill expansions and prefill seeds are intended to be shown for confirmation as the exact wording that may enter a clinical note. Unicode line/paragraph separators and bidirectional formatting controls can make the rendered wording differ in layout or visual order from the logical string being digested and saved, allowing pasted or adversarial config to hide/reorder content despite the claimed single-line validation and exact-text confirmation boundary.
- Current behaviour: `_no_control_chars()` rejects only C0, DEL, and C1 code points (`< U+0020` or `U+007F..U+009F`). Embedded `U+2028`/`U+2029` line separators and format controls such as `U+202E` (right-to-left override) and the bidi isolate controls are above that range and pass. The one shared validator feeds `_LabelText`, `_TriggerText`, and `_AssertionText`, so the gap reaches display labels, triggers, autofill expansion text, and prefill seed text.
- Desired behaviour: keep one shared validator, but reject Unicode line/paragraph separators and invisible format controls capable of changing display direction/order (a conservative `unicodedata.category()` rejection for `Zl`, `Zp`, and `Cf` is appropriate for this deliberately single-line clinical config). Preserve ordinary printable non-ASCII clinical text.
- Pattern to follow: strengthen `_no_control_chars()` at the single schema choke point rather than adding per-model checks, retaining typed Pydantic validation and whole-load failure.
- Pattern siblings: all `_LabelText`, `_TriggerText`, and `_AssertionText` fields; no separate fix should be added to individual config models.
- Invariant: accepted config text has one logical/display line and contains no invisible format control that can reorder or conceal the exact wording presented for clinician confirmation.
- Verification: add embedded `U+2028`, `U+2029`, bidi override, and bidi-isolate rejection cases through both a label and an assertion-bearing rule/prefill; retain a positive case with ordinary accented and non-Latin clinical text; assert `load_note_config()` wraps these failures as `NoteConfigInvalidError` without returning a partial config.
- Regression risk: an existing override containing hidden layout/format characters will begin failing loudly and require cleanup. That is the intended safe failure; ordinary Unicode prose must remain accepted.
- Scope-expansion disposition: In scope — Task 3.2 owns the single config-text validator and malformed/adversarial config rejection.
- /fix decision: Applied
- /fix notes: Strengthened `_no_control_chars` at the single choke point exactly as specified: after the existing C0/DEL/C1 range check, any character whose `unicodedata.category()` is in `_REJECTED_TEXT_CATEGORIES` (`Zl`/`Zp`/`Cf`) raises with a message naming the code point. Red/green demonstrated: 6 new tests (U+2028, U+2029, U+202E, U+2066, U+200B through a label, an autofill expansion, AND a prefill seed, plus a loader-wrap case) all failed `DID NOT RAISE` against the pre-fix validator, then passed after the fix; a positive case pins ordinary accented/CJK/non-Latin clinical text as accepted. Siblings: none to touch by design — all three `Annotated` text types share the one validator, per the finding's own sibling note. Accepted regression documented in the finding stands: an existing override carrying hidden layout characters now fails loudly (`NoteConfigInvalidError`) rather than loading. Verified: full ruff clean / mypy strict 26 files / pytest 848 passed at this fix's checkpoint (852 after PR-MED-001's tests).
- /fix date: 2026-08-10
- /fix applied by: Claude Code

#### PR-MED-001: `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/note_config.py:540` - the frozen binding carries two independently supplied fields and the public request model still bypasses it
- Classification: Same-family
- Triage: Fix-now
- Fix route: premium-only
- Why it matters: the template profile controls how clinical sections are mapped and which populated sections may be dropped. Round 10 intended to make membership in the exact digested config a structural precondition of provider generation, but a Phase-6 caller can still construct a type-correct cross-config binding - or construct the exported request model directly - without ever resolving membership. The new API therefore reduces accidental misuse but does not establish the invariant its docstrings and tests claim.
- Current behaviour: `BoundTemplateProfile` has only `template_profile` and regex-valid `config_digest` fields (`note_config.py:540-556`) and no validator tying them together; `frozen=True` prevents assignment after construction but does not authenticate their origin. `build_note_request` then blindly copies those two values (`:617-623`) and receives no `NoteConfig` against which it could detect forgery or replay. Separately, `NoteRequest` remains a public Pydantic constructor and is exported in `note.__all__` (`note.py:542-571`, `:1421-1444`); `test_note.py:80-83,659-670` explicitly preserves direct construction. The new tests prove only that `bind_template_profile` returns the right digest on its honest path: `test_a_fabricated_profile_id_cannot_reach_a_request` never presents the public builder with a hand-constructed `BoundTemplateProfile`, and `test_binding_carries_its_own_configs_digest` never cross-pairs one profile with another config's digest.
- Desired behaviour: the generation-facing path must validate profile membership and derive - not accept independently - the digest from the exact resolved config. A concrete safe shape is a config-owned builder that accepts `NoteConfig` plus an optional selected profile id and performs binding internally, or a bound value that stores the source `NoteConfig` and selected id and exposes profile/digest only as validated derived properties. Make the raw request type/assembler consistently package-private (or add an enforceable shipping-source construction guard) so Phase 6 has one public, type-checking path rather than two bypasses.
- Pattern to follow: `TemplateProfile._check_profile` and `NoteConfig._check_config` already validate relationships at construction instead of trusting paired fields. Apply the same relational-validation pattern to profile/config evidence, and apply the underscore/`__all__` convention used for `_assemble_note_request` to the raw request construction surface.
- Pattern siblings: `note_config.py:600-623` blindly trusts the wrapper; `note.py:542-571` exposes the lexical-only `NoteRequest` constructor; `note.py:1421-1444` exports it; `test_note_config.py:341-379` overstates what the honest-path tests prove; `test_note.py:80-83,659-670` retains the direct bypass by policy.
- Invariant: every `NoteRequest` accepted by a shipping provider carries a template profile that is a member of the exact resolved `NoteConfig` whose derived digest the request records; a profile/digest cross-pair and a zero/unknown/unselected profile state are unrepresentable through public shipping APIs.
- Verification: build configs A and B with the same profile id but different mappings/rules, attempt to pair A's profile with B's digest through every public construction surface, and require failure; prove no public raw `NoteRequest` constructor remains available to shipping code; retain sole-profile auto-bind and unknown/multi-profile failures; add a Phase-6-shaped test that loads config, selects/binds, builds the request, and obtains the selected profile/id/digest needed for mapping and persistence through the one supported path; run ruff, mypy strict, and the full pytest suite.
- Regression risk: hardening the nominal boundary will change fixture construction and may require moving raw request validation tests to a package-private helper. There are no shipping `note_config` callers yet, so this is the low-cost point to settle the API; keep the builder on the config side to avoid the existing `note.py` <-> `note_config.py` import cycle.
- Scope-expansion disposition: In scope - this is the Round-10 PR-MED-001 invariant and Task 3.1 Done-when (a session cannot generate without a bound profile), tested against the fixes' stated structural guarantee.
- /fix decision: Applied
- /fix notes: Applied the finding's SECOND Desired shape — derive, don't accept. `BoundTemplateProfile` now stores `source_config: NoteConfig` + `template_profile_id`, validates MEMBERSHIP at construction (`_check_membership`, the `TemplateProfile._check_profile` relational pattern the finding named), and exposes `template_profile` / `config_digest` only as DERIVED properties — so the cross-pair, zero/unknown-profile, and unselected states are unrepresentable through ANY construction of the type, not merely the honest path; `frozen` no longer carries the guarantee. `bind_template_profile` keeps sole-profile auto-bind / ambiguous-refusal / typed unknown-id error and constructs the relational shape. The `NoteRequest` bypass: removed from `note.__all__`, docstring states the exact posture (TYPE public for annotations; CONSTRUCTION package-private for shipping source, enforced by scan because a pydantic model cannot refuse its own constructor), and `test_no_shipping_source_constructs_a_raw_note_request` scans every `src/scribe_desktop` module for `NoteRequest(` construction outside `note.py`. Pre-fix cross-pair DEMONSTRATED by transient probe (config A's profile + config B's digest constructed cleanly and crossed the public builder; probe run passed, then reverted). Verification bar met: `test_cross_config_pairing_is_unrepresentable` attacks all four public surfaces (old supplied-pair shape refused by `extra="forbid"`; direct construction derives both values; membership rejected; builder requests carry their source config's digest); Phase-6-shaped flow test obtains profile/id/digest through the one supported path; sole-profile/unknown/multi failures retained. Siblings: `note_config.py` builder now consumes derived values (fixed); `note.py` `NoteRequest` export removed + docstring (fixed); `test_note_config.py` honest-path overstatement fixed by the attack tests; `test_note.py` direct fixture construction RETAINED BY POLICY per the finding's own framing (the fixture matrix needs raw requests; scan covers shipping source only). One executor-authored test bug caught and fixed during verification (called the request's `config_digest` string as a method). Verified: ruff clean / mypy strict 26 files / pytest 856 passed.

- /fix applied by: Claude Code
