#### PR-MED-001: `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/session.py:519,537-553` — `discard()` can delete key custody after a generation lease is acquired
- Classification: 🆕 Pre-existing
- Triage: Fix-now
- Fix route: premium-only
- Why it matters: `SessionController` promises any-thread-safe controls (`session.py:245-248`), and `GenerationInProgressError` says a custody-destructive operation is refused while the lease is held (`:207-210`). That invariant is false for a precise interleaving: thread A passes `_refuse_while_generating("discard")` at `:519`, drops `_lock`, and blocks in `worker.stop()` at `:538`; thread B then acquires the state-agnostic lease at `begin_generation()` (`:584-597`); thread A reacquires `_lock` at `:539` and reaches key-first `discard_session()` at `:553` without another guard. Key deletion/crypto destruction can therefore run while `controller.generating` is true. The current intended same-session generation states normally avoid a live capture worker, so this is MED rather than HIGH, but the lease is deliberately global and target-agnostic and the irreversible custody contract cannot depend on that caller convention.
- Current behaviour: lease exclusion is checked only before the unlocked worker-stop interval; no reservation prevents acquisition during the interval and no post-stop guard exists.
- Desired behaviour: make discard and generation acquisition mutually exclusive across the whole two-lock discard operation. Reserve the custody transition under `_lock` before stopping the worker and make `begin_generation()` refuse/serialize against that reservation; clear it on every exit. A bare post-stop recheck is insufficient because refusal at that point leaves the session claiming RECORDING/PAUSED after its worker has already been stopped.
- Pattern to follow: the controller's existing guarded state flags (`_generation`, `_LiveSession.transcribing`) establish ownership under `_lock` before work crosses a thread boundary. Use the same reservation shape for this destructive transition while preserving the established rule that the worker join stays outside `_lock`.
- Pattern siblings: no other Phase 6 custody-destructive path has this gap. `complete()`, `complete_recovered()`, `discard_recovered()`, `destroy_recovered_crypto()`, and `_retire_locked()` keep guard and mutation in one locked critical section; `finish()` releases the lock around `worker.stop()` but does not delete a key or retire crypto.
- Invariant: after `begin_generation()` returns, no start/Complete/Discard/retirement/recovered-key-destruction mutation may begin until that exact lease ends; conversely, generation acquisition must not enter after a custody-destructive operation has reserved its transition.
- Verification: add a deterministic two-thread barrier test using the existing `TestDiscardStartRace` worker-wrapper pattern: pause `discard()` inside `worker.stop()`, attempt `begin_generation()`, and prove either the lease acquisition is refused/serialized until discard completes or discard is refused before any key/crypto mutation. Assert there is never a state in which the lease is held while `discard_session()` deletes the key, and retain the existing worker-stop-outside-lock/deadlock regression.
- Regression risk: holding `_lock` across `worker.stop()` can deadlock with capture callbacks, while merely rechecking after stop can strand a half-stopped live session. The repair needs explicit transition ownership plus `finally` cleanup, and must preserve concurrent-discard idempotence and the wrong-session protection pinned by `TestDiscardStartRace`.
- Scope-expansion disposition: In scope — Task 6.3 is specifically the cross-thread custody coordinator, and the phase's Done condition says the lease blocks Discard for its full lifetime.

#### LEG 1 verified tuples (executor `stage-6`, 2026-08-11 — verification only, no code changed)
- PR-MED-001: **real=yes; materiality=behavioral; verified severity=MED (agrees with the peer's MED — no drift)**. Evidence, confirmed line-by-line against the current tree: `discard()`'s only lease check is `:519` in the FIRST locked section; `_lock` is released for `worker.stop()` (`:537-538`); `begin_generation()` (`:584-597`) checks only `self._generation is None` — state-agnostic, blind to an in-flight discard — so a lease acquired in that window survives into discard's SECOND locked section, which reaches key-first `discard_session()` (`:553`) with no re-guard: key deletion + in-memory crypto destruction while `generating` is true. Two sharpenings from the trace, recorded for the fix leg: (1) the window exists on EVERY discard, not only worker-bearing ones — the RLock is released and reacquired between the two `with` blocks even when `worker is None` (a QUEUED discard), so the repair must span the whole two-lock operation, exactly as the peer's reservation shape does and a stop-scoped guard would not; (2) the SEVERITY question resolves to MED on reachability, per the round-42 MED-003 wrong-by-contract precedent: a same-session generation can never coincide with a live capture worker (generation targets a QUEUED session, whose `worker` is already None after `finish()`; RECORDING/PAUSED have no transcript to generate from; FAILED is not a generation target), and every shipped custody caller — including Phase 7's planned Generate — runs on the single GUI thread via queued signals, so the interleaving requires a non-GUI-thread custody caller that does not exist in shipped wiring. The controller's DOCUMENTED contract (`:245-248` any-thread safety) is what the defect violates, which is precisely Task 6.3's remit — hence real and in-scope, but not a normal-flow custody violation → not HIGH, no practitioner pause required beyond the composer's disposition.
- Fix-shape sanity check, as instructed: the peer's reservation shape is SOUND and both of its stated hazards are confirmed real — a bare post-stop recheck would strand a half-stopped session (worker stopped, state still RECORDING/PAUSED, store open), and holding `_lock` across `worker.stop()` would deadlock against `_on_capture_failure`, which takes the lock from the worker callback thread. Two additions the fix leg must handle: the reservation needs `try/finally` clearing on EVERY exit of `discard()` (including the concurrent-discard early return at `:548-549` and an exception from `discard_session`), and it must refuse only `begin_generation` — never a second concurrent `discard()` — or the `TestDiscardStartRace` idempotence/wrong-session pins break.
- /fix decision: Applied
- /fix notes: Applied exactly per the Desired-behaviour reservation shape plus both LEG-1 additions. (1) NEW `SessionController._custody_reservations: int` — a COUNTER, not a flag, because concurrent discards are legal and one finishing must not strip the other's reservation. (2) `discard()` reserves under the FIRST lock as the LAST statement of the section (after every refusal path, so no failure can leak a reservation), then wraps the worker join and the whole second section in `try/finally`, decrementing under the lock on EVERY exit — success, the concurrent-discard early return, and a `discard_session` exception. The reservation spans the full two-lock operation, covering both the worker-join window and the no-worker lock-release/reacquire instant the LEG-1 trace added. No lock is held across `worker.stop()` (the `_on_capture_failure` deadlock hazard) and no post-stop recheck exists (the strand hazard) — both rejected shapes stayed rejected. (3) `begin_generation()` refuses while any reservation is held, raising `SessionActivityError` deliberately rather than `GenerationInProgressError` (no generation is in progress; the conflict is an in-flight discard — reasoned in code). The invariant holds by lock serialization in both directions: lease-first → discard refused at entry (unchanged); discard-first → acquisition refused until the reservation clears. Reservation refuses ONLY `begin_generation` — `start()` is untouched, so `TestDiscardStartRace`'s concurrent-start wrong-session pin is preserved unchanged. Verification added as prescribed: `test_begin_generation_refused_inside_discard_unlocked_window` reproduces the EXACT interleaving deterministically via the `TestDiscardStartRace` worker-wrapper pattern (acquisition attempted from inside the unlocked worker-stop window → refused; key deleted with `generating` False throughout; reservation cleared → a fresh lease acquires after), and `test_custody_reservation_clears_when_discard_fails` pins the `finally` discipline (injected `discard_session` failure → reservation released, generation available, real discard still works). Composer verification of the suites pending (verify=composer); expected pytest 1170 + 2 = 1172.
- /fix date: 2026-08-11
- /fix applied by: Claude Code (executor `stage-6`, leg a5)

#### PR-MED-001: `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/note.py:1668,1753,1869` — provider output can bypass the proposal/clinician-confirmation path
- Classification: Pre-existing
- Triage: Fix-now
- Fix route: premium-only
- Why it matters: Flow 1 says the provider contributes transcript assertions and every `autofill`/`prefill` assertion must originate as a proposal whose exact text the clinician resolved. That invariant is not enforced at the provider boundary. A valid `NoteModelProvider` can return a `GeneratedSection` containing a `prefill` `NoteAssertion` with a self-constructed matching `ConfirmationDecision`, shown-text digest, and config digest. `NoteDraft` accepts it; `finalise_note` carries provider sections through without correlating them to `note_proposals`/`resolutions`; Check 3 emits only the non-blocking `clinician_asserted` review for prefill provenance (`note_check.py:906-919`); and `write_note` verifies the self-consistent fields but has no emitted-proposal membership to check (`session_store.py:786-821`). The assertion can therefore reach `note.enc` without ever being displayed or confirmed. The current shipping `ExtractiveNoteProvider` does not exercise the gap, so this is MED rather than HIGH, but the provider Protocol is the deliberate Phase-3B extension seam and the artifact-level confirmation claim must not depend on every provider behaving.
- Current behaviour: `compose_draft()` accepts `provider.generate_sections(request)` wholesale (`note.py:1750-1766`). `NoteDraft._check_draft()` validates section order and duplicate assertion IDs only (`:1668-1692`), and `finalise_note()` validates resolutions only against `draft.note_proposals` before merging the untouched base sections (`:1826-1869`). No stage establishes that every non-transcript assertion came from one emitted proposal and one supplied confirmation resolution.
- Desired behaviour: confine the provider/base-note side of the draft to transcript-provenance assertions, and re-establish that stage-specific invariant at finalisation so a forged/validator-skipping draft cannot bypass it. Every final non-transcript assertion must be introduced only by the proposal-resolution loop. If Phase 3B later adds model-authored provenance, give it a distinct explicit contract rather than widening the `autofill`/`prefill` path.
- Pattern to follow: the existing `NoteProposal`/`NoteAssertion` type split and the config/request use-time boundary — enforce the semantic route at the consuming boundary, not by trusting an implementation convention or enumerating the two current providers.
- Pattern siblings: `ExtractiveNoteProvider` and every current `MockNoteModelProvider` behaviour emit transcript-provenance base assertions and should remain valid; the future Phase-3B provider is the important sibling because the plan promises it can use this seam without bypassing confirmation.
- Invariant: every `autofill`/`prefill` assertion in a final note corresponds one-to-one to a `NoteProposal` emitted in that draft and a confirmed `ProposalResolution` whose displayed-text digest matches; provider base sections contain transcript assertions only.
- Verification: add a stub provider returning a fully schema-valid `prefill` assertion with fabricated-but-internally-consistent confirmation evidence and no proposal; prove compose/finalise refuses it and no `note.enc` can be written. Retain the legitimate Extractive/Mock pipeline tests and the confirmed/declined/pending proposal matrix; add a validator-skipping `NoteDraft` case if finalisation claims to re-establish the invariant.
- Regression risk: a stage-specific restriction can accidentally reject the existing adversarial Mock provider or pre-empt Phase 3B's future provenance design. Keep `GeneratedSection` broad for final saved notes; narrow only provider/base-draft ingestion and finalisation, with the future model provenance left to its own explicit phase contract.
- Scope-expansion disposition: In scope — Task 6.1 owns the two-stage pipeline and explicitly claims unconfirmed content cannot cross it; this is the direct enforcement gap in that claim.

#### LEG 1 verified tuples (executor `stage-6`, 2026-08-11 — verification only, no code changed)
- PR-MED-001: **real=yes; materiality=behavioral; verified severity=MED (agrees with the peer — no drift); scope=IN-SCOPE (agrees with the peer's call).** Evidence, confirmed stage-by-stage against the current tree: a `prefill`-provenance `NoteAssertion` with fabricated-but-internally-consistent evidence is honestly CONSTRUCTABLE (every evidence field is public; the validator checks presence and internal consistency, never the decision's provenance); `NoteDraft._check_draft` validates ordering/duplicate-ids/digest-format only, so a provider-returned base section carrying it passes; `finalise_note` correlates resolutions against `draft.note_proposals` only and `_merge_confirmed` carries base sections untouched; Check 3 gives prefill provenance only the non-blocking `clinician_asserted` review (its NOT-checked residue explicitly records prefill has no trigger claim to re-verify); `write_note`'s canonical re-validation passes an honestly-constructed assertion and its digest checks are all self-consistent-satisfiable, with no emitted-proposal membership to verify against. End to end: writable `note.enc` containing never-displayed, never-confirmed clinician-authored content. SHARPENING for the fix leg: the AUTOFILL provenance is also partially open — `_rule_for_assertion` re-derives the content-derived proposal id and re-verifies trigger PRESENCE, but never compares the assertion's TEXT to the rule's expansion entry, so a provider computing a legitimately-fireable rule's id can attach arbitrary text; the transcript-only base-section confinement closes both provenances at once. SEVERITY: MED confirmed on reachability — `ExtractiveNoteProvider` and every `MockNoteModelProvider` behaviour construct `provenance="transcript"` spans exclusively (verified at each construction site), so no shipping or test provider exercises the gap; it is reachable only by a future or hand-authored provider, and the Protocol seam is exactly Phase 3B's extension point, which is why it must close now — but that is contract-level exposure, not a normal-flow violation, the same grading as rounds 27/42. SCOPE: IN-SCOPE, stated plainly — Task 6.1's Done-when ("refuses a note containing an unconfirmed proposal"), global property 4, the Critical Constraint ("no clinician-authored text enters a note without explicit per-assertion confirmation of the exact inserted wording"), and `NoteDraft`'s own docstring ("cannot smuggle unconfirmed content past finalise_note by construction") all DECLARE this invariant; only its enforcement is incomplete — the rounds-10–13 arc shape (claim ahead of structure), an enforcement completion, not a widening of any declared check.
- Fix-direction sanity check, as instructed: SOUND, with the peer's own regression caveats confirmed load-bearing. Confining the DRAFT's base sections to transcript provenance breaks neither shipping provider (both emit transcript-only at every construction site) nor the adversarial matrix (it constructs `GeneratedNote`/`GeneratedSection` directly, and `GeneratedSection` must stay BROAD — final notes legitimately hold composed non-transcript assertions; the confinement belongs on `NoteDraft`/compose ingestion plus a finalisation re-check for validator-skipping drafts, never on the shared section type). It does not pre-empt Phase 3B: the plan already reserves `model` provenance its own explicit contract (Task 5.1's containment note), so 3B widens deliberately rather than inheriting the `autofill`/`prefill` path. Re-finalisation loops are unaffected (retract-and-refinalise re-enters from the draft + resolutions, never feeds a `GeneratedNote` back through `NoteDraft`).
- /fix decision: Applied
- /fix notes: Applied at the CONSUMING boundary exactly as the Desired behaviour and the no-content-escapes lesson prescribe — the CLASS is confined, no provider or spelling is enumerated. (1) `NoteDraft._check_draft` now refuses ANY base-section assertion whose `note_span.provenance != "transcript"`, however complete its evidence fields — closing the prefill demonstration AND the leg-1 autofill text-vs-entry sharpening in one move, since no non-transcript assertion of any provenance can enter a draft at all. `compose_draft` needs no separate check: the provider's sections become `NoteDraft.note_sections`, so the refusal fires at ingestion as the type's own `ValidationError` (consistent with the module's structural refusals; documented in `compose_draft`'s docstring). Verification added exactly as prescribed: `_SmugglingProvider` (schema-valid, fabricated-but-internally-consistent prefill evidence, no emitted proposal) is refused at compose (`test_compose_refuses_a_smuggling_provider`); the validator-skipping draft is refused at finalisation before any `GeneratedNote` exists, so nothing can reach `write_note` (`test_finalise_refuses_a_validator_skipping_draft`); and the legitimate-provider regression is pinned (`test_legitimate_providers_are_untouched_by_the_confinement`). #### PR-MED-001: `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/session.py:507-523,525-587` — an in-flight Discard does not exclude Complete, so both terminal actions can mutate the same queued session
- Classification: Pre-existing
- Triage: Fix-now
- Fix route: premium-only
- Why it matters: `SessionController` promises that all controls are safe from any thread (`session.py:245-248`), but a queued-session `discard()` reserves and then necessarily releases `_lock` between its two critical sections (`:528-565`). The reservation is consulted only by `begin_generation()` (`:625-636`), not by `complete()` (`:507-523`). In the valid interleaving where Discard passes its first section and thread B enters `complete()` during that unlocked gap, Complete verifies/deletes custody, transitions the shared `live.session` QUEUED → WRITTEN, clears `_live`, and returns success. Discard then reacquires the lock, sees WRITTEN rather than its sole early-return state DISCARDED (`:573-575`), calls key-first `discard_session()` and removes the session directory (`:576-579`), then raises `IllegalTransitionError` trying WRITTEN → DISCARDED (`:580`). One caller has therefore been told the session completed as WRITTEN while the overlapping call removed its artifacts, and the Discard caller raises only after its destructive side effect. Shipped GUI callbacks are single-threaded, so this is MED rather than HIGH, but it violates the controller's explicit any-thread contract at a custody transition.
- Current behaviour: `_custody_reservations` excludes only generation acquisition; `complete()` can enter and complete the exact live snapshot while a queued `discard()` owns the two-lock transition.
- Desired behaviour: once Discard reserves its transition, Complete must refuse or serialize before `complete_session()` performs any custody mutation. Preserve concurrent-Discard idempotence and the existing concurrent-Start snapshot behavior; do not hold `_lock` across `worker.stop()`.
- Pattern to follow: `begin_generation()` already consumes `_custody_reservations` under the controller lock (`session.py:625-636`). Reuse that transition-ownership signal at the conflicting Complete entry rather than adding a post-mutation state repair or another unlocked check.
- Pattern siblings: `complete()` is the only same-live-session terminal operation that can legally pass its state gate while a queued Discard is between lock sections. `start()` deliberately operates on a new snapshot and is covered by `TestDiscardStartRace`; overlapping Discards remain admitted by the counter; recovered Complete/Discard operations keep guard and mutation inside one locked section.
- Invariant: after `discard()` reserves a live session's custody transition, no conflicting Complete may verify/delete custody or return WRITTEN for that session until the reservation is released; exactly one terminal action wins before either reports success.
- Verification: add a deterministic two-thread barrier at the queued Discard's first-lock exit (an instrumented lock or equivalent rendezvous, never a scheduler sleep), attempt `complete()` while the reservation is held, and prove it refuses/serializes before `complete_session`; then release Discard and assert it alone reaches DISCARDED with key-first deletion. Retain the round-27 generation-window test, concurrent-Discard cleanup, worker-stop-outside-lock, and `TestDiscardStartRace` pins.
- Regression risk: a broad reservation refusal could accidentally reject the intentionally legal second Discard or the concurrent-Start/new-session path; holding `_lock` across `worker.stop()` would reintroduce the capture-callback deadlock. Keep the new exclusion scoped to conflicting Complete semantics and under the existing lock.
- Scope-expansion disposition: In scope — Task 6.3 owns the any-thread custody coordinator and the defect is in the reservation-protected terminal transition added/hardened in this phase.

#### LEG 1 verified tuples (executor `stage-6`, 2026-08-11 — verification only, no code changed)
- PR-MED-001: **real=yes; materiality=behavioral; verified severity=MED (agrees with the peer — no drift); scope=IN-SCOPE; class=BOUNDED.** Trace CONFIRMED step-by-step against the current tree: a QUEUED discard reserves and releases `_lock` (the window exists with `worker=None` — the round-27 leg-1 sharpening); `complete()`'s entry checks are the lease and `_require_state(QUEUED)` ONLY — `_custody_reservations` is consulted nowhere in it — so a thread-B Complete slots into the window, verifies, DELETES the key, transitions QUEUED→WRITTEN, clears `_live`, returns success; discard's second section then sees WRITTEN (not its sole early-return state DISCARDED), runs `discard_session` — key unlink now a no-op, `rmtree` REMOVES the directory including the `note.enc` that Complete's contract deliberately leaves on disk — and only then raises `IllegalTransitionError` on WRITTEN→DISCARDED. One honest nuance recorded, which SUPPORTS the MED grade: by the time the rmtree runs, Complete has already legitimately destroyed the key after full verification, so every removed artifact is already cryptographically dead — the harm is the contract violation itself (two terminal actions both mutating one session, the destructive act preceding the failure report, and inconsistent caller outcomes), not a wrongful key deletion. SEVERITY: MED confirmed on the identical reachability argument as round 27 — the interleaving needs a non-GUI-thread custody caller; every shipped and Phase-7-planned caller runs on the single GUI thread; wrong-by-contract per the round-42 MED-003 precedent. SCOPE: in-scope — Task 6.3's coordinator, the sibling gap of the round-27 repair (the reservation exists; one consumer is missing).
- **CLASS BOUNDEDNESS — verdict: BOUNDED. One point-fix (`complete()` consults `_custody_reservations` under its existing single locked section) closes the class.** The enumeration behind the verdict, checked entry point by entry point against the current tree: `discard()` is the ONLY custody-mutating operation with an unlocked window — `complete()`, `complete_recovered()`, `discard_recovered()`, `destroy_recovered_crypto()`, `start()` (including `_retire_locked`), and `end_generation()` each hold `_lock` for their entire body, and the operations that DO release the lock mid-flight (`finish()`, `pause()`, `transcribe()`) mutate no custody (store sealing/closing is not key/crypto destruction, and their races are covered by the PR-HIGH-001 snapshot pins and the `transcribing` flag). What can slot into discard's window, case by case: `begin_generation` — guarded (round 27); `complete` — THIS finding, the one missing consumer; a SECOND `discard` — deliberately admitted and safe (the counter admits it, the DISCARDED early-return plus `discard_session`'s idempotence handle either completion order); `start` — deliberately admitted and pinned safe by `TestDiscardStartRace` (retirement destroys the in-memory key copy only, on-disk custody untouched, and discard's snapshot discipline protects the new session); `mark_queued`/`transcribe`/`finish`/`pause`/`resume` — state-excluded (a QUEUED discardee fails their state gates; conversely for a RECORDING/PAUSED/FAILED discardee it is `complete()` that is state-refused, so the Complete conflict is exactly and only the QUEUED case the peer traced); the recovered coordinator ops — single locked sections over RECOVERED directories, disjoint from the live session by reachability (the recovery listing excludes the controller's non-terminal session and checkout discipline blocks list actions) — passing the LIVE session's directory/crypto to a recovered op would be parameter misuse of a differently-scoped API, a contract boundary worth naming, not a concurrency hole; the 24 h sweep — externally protected (rounds 42/18 wiring, re-verified in round 25). Cross-pairs: complete-vs-complete serializes on the single section (the loser fails `_require_live`); generation-vs-complete is guarded at complete's entry today. CONCLUSION: after the one fix, every conflicting custody-mutating entry point is guarded, deliberately-admitted-and-pinned, state-excluded, scope-disjoint, or externally protected — a holistic custody-operation serialization layer is NOT needed and would re-litigate the load-bearing worker-join-outside-lock design for no additional coverage.
- Fix-shape note for the fix leg: mirror `begin_generation`'s consumer shape — check `_custody_reservations` inside `complete()`'s existing locked section at entry (beside the lease check, before `_require_state`), refusing with a typed activity error naming the in-flight discard; the reservation must keep refusing NOTHING else (the second-discard and start admissions are pinned), and no lock may be held across any worker join (unchanged — complete has no join).
- /fix decision: Applied
- /fix notes: Applied exactly per the leg-1 fix-shape note and the peer's Pattern to follow. `complete()` now consults `_custody_reservations` inside its EXISTING single locked section at entry — after the lease check, before `_require_state` — refusing with `SessionActivityError("a discard is completing; the session cannot be completed")`; the in-code comment records the invariant (exactly one terminal action wins before either reports success) and the deliberate non-broadening (second discards and concurrent `start()` stay admitted; the reservation's only consumers are `begin_generation` and now `complete`). No lock is held across any worker join (complete has none; discard unchanged). Verification added exactly as prescribed: `test_complete_refused_inside_discard_unlocked_window` is a deterministic TWO-THREAD barrier — a `_BarrierWorker` injected into the QUEUED session rendezvouses via `threading.Event`s (no scheduler sleeps): the discard thread parks INSIDE its unlocked window with the reservation held, the main thread's `complete()` is proven refused BEFORE `complete_session` mutates anything (key file asserted intact at that instant), then the released discard alone reaches DISCARDED with key-first deletion and directory removal, `controller.session` ending None. Retained untouched and still green by construction: the round-27 generation-window barrier test, the reservation `finally`-discipline test, concurrent-discard idempotence, worker-stop-outside-lock, and both `TestDiscardStartRace` pins (the reservation still refuses nothing they exercise). Class closure per the accepted leg-1 enumeration: with this consumer added, every entry point that can slot into discard's window is guarded, admitted-and-pinned, state-excluded, scope-disjoint, or externally protected. #### PR-MED-001: `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/session.py:331-336,538-600` — concurrent Start can expose a still-reserved Discard target to recovery and the expiry sweep
- Classification: Same-family
- Triage: Fix-now
- Fix route: premium-only
- Why it matters: `discard()` snapshots live session X, increments the custody reservation, and releases `_lock` around `worker.stop()` (`session.py:561-578`). The deliberately admitted `start()` can then retire X and install Y: `_retire_locked()` destroys only X's old in-memory crypto while deliberately leaving X's `key.dpapi` and directory recoverable (`session.py:331-336,740-756`). From that instant, the controller/UI active-session exclusion names Y, not still-reserved X (`ui/main_window.py:126-134`), and `list_recoverable_sessions()` will list any non-excluded X with a live key blob (`ui/models.py:148-218`). A recovery worker can therefore call `unwrap_key_from_file(X)` (`transcription.py:1007`) before Discard resumes, creating a second `SessionCrypto` for X's key. Discard then deletes X's custody blob and destroys only its old snapshot crypto (`session.py:578-600`), while the separately unwrapped recovered key can survive and continue being used. Cryptographic deletion has returned success while an app-owned plaintext key remains in memory. The same missing reserved-ID protection lets the 24 h sweep target X after retirement; recovered Complete/Discard likewise accept a directory without testing whether that session ID is reserved (`session.py:675-688`). The normal GUI is single-threaded, so reaching this scheduling window still requires a non-GUI custody caller/injected race and the finding is MED rather than HIGH, consistent with rounds 27 and 29, but it falsifies the controller's documented any-thread custody claim and the asserted class closure.
- Current behaviour: `_custody_reservations` is a target-agnostic count consumed by live Complete and generation acquisition only. It proves an operation is in flight but cannot keep that operation's session ID in the recovery/sweep protection set after `start()` replaces `_live`, nor can a recovered coordinator call test whether its directory is the reserved target.
- Desired behaviour: retain target-aware custody reservations for every in-flight Discard and treat each reserved session ID as protected from recovery checkout, recovered terminal operations, and expiry sweeping until that Discard exits. Preserve the deliberately admitted Start and second-Discard behavior unless the product intentionally changes that policy.
- Pattern to follow: the existing checkout/active-session identity sets at the recovery and sweep boundaries, but source the additional IDs from controller-owned per-session reservation counts rather than from the mutable current `_live` pointer. Re-check the same reservation by resolved session identity inside recovered coordinator entry points so a stale/direct callback fails closed as well as disappearing from the list.
- Pattern siblings: `MainWindow._live_session_ids()`, `app.sweep_protected_ids()`, `RecoveryScreen` checkout protection, `complete_recovered()`, `discard_recovered()`, and the raw recovery unwrap boundary. A global boolean is insufficient: overlapping Discards need independent per-ID lifetime, and one completion must not unprotect another target.
- Invariant: from the instant Discard reserves session X until its `finally` releases the reservation, no path may unwrap X's on-disk key, complete/discard X through the recovered coordinator, or sweep X; starting session Y must not make X externally recoverable during that interval.
- Verification: add a deterministic barrier that parks Discard(X) after reservation, admits `start()` to install Y, and then proves X is absent from the recovery listing and included in the sweep protection set; direct recovered coordinator/checkout attempts for X must refuse before unwrapping or mutating custody. Release Discard and prove its key-first deletion completes, the reservation clears, and Y's custody is intact. Retain the round-27 generation barrier, round-29 Complete barrier, second-Discard counter/finally coverage, and worker-stop-outside-lock regression. The existing `TestDiscardStartRace` proves only that Discard keeps targeting X rather than Y; it does not prove X remains unavailable to a third custody path during the reservation.
- Regression risk: a global protection bit can retain unrelated expired sessions, and a set without per-ID reference counts can be cleared too early by overlapping Discards. Do not move `worker.stop()` under the controller lock or reintroduce the capture-callback deadlock; keep the repair identity-scoped across the current two-lock shape.
- Scope-expansion disposition: In scope — Task 6.3 declares one lease-aware coordinator for live and recovered custody, and this is the missed composition between its admitted live-session retirement and recovered/sweep boundaries.

#### LEG 1 verification (executor `stage-6`, 2026-08-11, folded into this verify-then-fix leg per the practitioner's disposition)
- PR-MED-001: **real=yes; materiality=behavioral; severity=MED (agrees — same non-GUI-thread reachability grading as rounds 27/29); scope=IN-SCOPE.** Confirmed against the current tree exactly as the peer traced: the reservation was a target-agnostic count consumed only by `begin_generation`/`complete`; `_retire_locked` deliberately leaves X's `key.dpapi` + directory recoverable while destroying only the in-memory copy; the recovery exclusion and sweep protection were sourced from the MUTABLE live pointer, which the admitted `start()` swaps mid-window; and neither recovered-coordinator op tested the reservation by identity. My round-29 BOUNDED enumeration is honestly FALSIFIED on its recovered-path premise — I judged the recovered surface "scope-disjoint by reachability (the listing excludes the controller's non-terminal session)" without noticing that the admitted start() changes that exclusion's REFERENT while the reservation is still held. The composition of two individually-sound admissions was the gap; recorded so round 31 verifies the structural fix rather than re-deriving the class.
- /fix decision: Applied
- /fix notes: Applied the practitioner-approved STRUCTURAL fix exactly as Desired behaviour / Pattern to follow prescribe. (1) `SessionController._custody_reservations` is now a TARGET-AWARE `dict[str, int]` — per-session-id counts with independent lifetime (`_reserve_custody_locked`/`_release_custody_locked`; overlapping discards of one id nest, releasing one never unprotects another target; the global-flag insufficiency the finding names is structurally excluded). `discard()` reserves ITS TARGET's id in its first locked section and releases in the same `finally`; the coarse consumers (`begin_generation`, `complete`) keep their exact refusal semantics (`if self._custody_reservations:` over a dict — messages and round-27/29 pins unchanged). (2) NEW `reserved_session_ids()` on the controller (+ `SessionControllerLike` + `FakeController`) is THE protection source: `MainWindow._live_session_ids` unions it with the non-terminal live session (the exclusion no longer depends on the mutable pointer alone), and `app.sweep_protected_ids` unions it into the sweep exemption (docstrings at both sites record the round-30 lesson). (3) The recovered-coordinator entry points fail closed by RESOLVED identity: `complete_recovered`/`discard_recovered` call the new `_refuse_reserved_target_locked`, which — only while reservations exist — resolves the directory's identity through the imported single `_resolve_session_identity` definition (header authoritative, never a caller claim; a resolution failure propagates typed, fail-closed) and refuses a reserved target with a typed `SessionActivityError`; `destroy_recovered_crypto` refuses COARSELY while any reservation is held, documented in code — it carries no directory to resolve, and coarse is a strict superset of scoped, failing toward safety at the cost of a transient retry (`MainWindow._destroy_recovered_crypto`'s catch widened to `(GenerationInProgressError, SessionActivityError)`, reference retained either way). (4) `RecoveryScreen` re-checks the exclusion set at CLICK time for BOTH Resume and list-Discard (`_selection_blocked`: the rendered list can be stale; refusal happens BEFORE any key unwrap or custody mutation, fail-CLOSED on a provider error — deliberately unlike `refresh()`'s fail-open listing posture, reasoned in code). #### PR-MED-001: `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/app.py:128-131`; `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/ui/main_window.py:141-144` — protected-id snapshots can omit a reserved Discard target
- Classification: Fix-induced
- Triage: Fix-now
- Fix route: premium-only
- Why it matters: both protection helpers read controller state in multiple separately locked calls. For a QUEUED/FAILED live session X, `sweep_protected_ids()` can read `active_session_ids() == ∅` and then `reserved_session_ids() == ∅`; Discard(X) can next reserve X at `session.py:590`, and the deliberately admitted `start()` can retire X and install Y at `session.py:332-378`; the helper's final `controller.session` read then sees Y and returns a set that omits X while X's reservation is still live. `sweep_sessions()` may consequently sweep X during the very lifetime the round-30 fix promises to protect. `MainWindow._live_session_ids()` has the same split read in the opposite two-call form, so a recovery refresh — and the click-time stale-selection check that reuses this provider — can likewise observe neither reserved X nor live X even though the reservation is live at return. With a stale X item (obtainable when a refresh snapshots before `start(X)` and scans the directory after its key is created), the second split snapshot can let the recovery runner reach `unwrap_key_from_file(X)` during Discard(X), recreating the surviving-plaintext-key outcome round 30 was meant to close. Reachability still needs non-GUI-thread controller calls, matching rounds 27/29/30's MED grading rather than HIGH, but it violates the controller's explicit any-thread custody contract and falsifies full-lifetime protection.
- Current behaviour: `reserved_session_ids()` is individually correct and locked, as are `active_session_ids()` and `session`, but neither caller obtains the reservation set and current non-terminal live id in one linearizable controller operation. The comments call the resulting unions THE protection source even though their constituent observations can describe different controller states.
- Desired behaviour: expose one controller-owned atomic snapshot that returns every custody-protected id — all current reservation keys plus the current non-terminal live session id — under a single acquisition of `SessionController._lock`, and use that one snapshot for both the sweep and recovery-list/click provider. Preserve `active_session_ids()` if its narrower Phase-2 API is still useful, but do not compose the round-30 protection invariant from separate public reads.
- Pattern to follow: `reserved_session_ids()` already copies controller-owned state under `_lock`; widen that pattern into a single purpose-named snapshot whose whole union is constructed inside the critical section, then make both external consumers delegate to it rather than reconstructing controller state.
- Pattern siblings: `app.sweep_protected_ids()` and `MainWindow._live_session_ids()` are the two shipping consumers and both carry the same race. `RecoveryScreen._selection_blocked()` reuses the latter provider, so it is covered by the same fix; no third shipping consumer of `reserved_session_ids()` exists.
- Invariant: at every linearization point, the protected-id snapshot contains session X if X is either the controller's current non-terminal session or has a positive custody-reservation count. Therefore a Discard(X) → Start(Y) transition cannot produce a snapshot omitting X before Discard(X)'s `finally` releases its last per-id reservation.
- Verification: add deterministic barriers around the old split-read boundary for BOTH consumers: begin the snapshot while X is QUEUED/FAILED and unreserved, pause after the reservation read, run Discard(X) to reservation plus admitted Start(Y), then resume and prove the returned set still contains X. Pin recovery listing/click refusal and an expiry sweep using that atomic snapshot, plus two simultaneous reservations for different ids where releasing one leaves the other protected. Retain all round-27/29/30 barriers, worker-stop-outside-lock, reservation-failure cleanup, admitted Start, and overlapping same-id Discard regressions.
- Regression risk: replacing the existing APIs wholesale could break Phase-2 callers that intentionally want only ACTIVE states, while taking the controller lock across filesystem listing/sweeping would create unnecessary lock hold time. Snapshot only the ids under `_lock`, release it, then perform filesystem work; keep the narrower observer for its existing callers.
- Scope-expansion disposition: In scope — this is the unclosed protection-consumer edge of round 30's Task-6.3 custody fix, not a new subsystem.

#### LEG 1 verification (executor `stage-6`, 2026-08-11, folded into this verify-then-fix leg per the practitioner's disposition)
- PR-MED-001: **real=yes; materiality=behavioral; severity=MED (agrees — the rounds-27/29/30 reachability grading); scope=IN-SCOPE; classification Fix-induced agreed** — the split-read union was written by the round-30 repair at exactly these two consumer sites. Confirmed against the code: `sweep_protected_ids` composed THREE separately-locked controller reads (`active_session_ids()`, `reserved_session_ids()`, `session`) and `_live_session_ids` TWO; each read is individually correct, but a Discard(X)-reserve plus admitted Start(Y) interleaved BETWEEN reads yields a returned set naming Y and omitting still-reserved X — a non-linearizable snapshot presented as THE protection source, re-opening the round-30 exposure for the reservation's lifetime.
- /fix decision: Applied
- /fix notes: Applied the practitioner-approved atomic-snapshot fix exactly as Desired behaviour prescribes. (1) NEW `SessionController.custody_protected_ids()` — every custody-protected id (all reservation keys PLUS the current non-terminal live session id, active states being a subset of non-terminal) constructed inside a SINGLE `_lock` acquisition; ids only under the lock, filesystem work after (docstring records the round-31 lesson: composing the union from separate public reads was itself a race). (2) BOTH external consumers now delegate to that one snapshot: `app.sweep_protected_ids` is `controller.custody_protected_ids() | extra` (nothing else read), and `MainWindow._live_session_ids` returns the snapshot directly — which also covers `RecoveryScreen._selection_blocked`, the provider's only other consumer. `active_session_ids()` is PRESERVED untouched for its Phase-2 callers, and `reserved_session_ids()` remains as the reservation-only diagnostic view with its docstring redirected to the snapshot. Both overclaiming docstrings corrected to state the atomic-snapshot rationale. Protocol (`SessionControllerLike`) + `FakeController` gained `custody_protected_ids` (the fake mirrors the union semantics). (3) Verification, one honest note on shape: the fix makes an INTRA-call pause point structurally unrepresentable — the whole union is built under one lock acquisition, so there is no "after the reservation read" boundary left to instrument inside the new method. The tests therefore pin the invariant from every angle that remains observable: `test_sweep_protected_ids_is_one_atomic_snapshot` and `test_live_session_ids_is_one_atomic_snapshot` are split-read POISON probes — controller stand-ins whose `reserved_session_ids`/`active_session_ids`/`session` reads fail the test loudly, proving each consumer performs exactly ONE snapshot call and composes nothing (deterministically RED against the pre-fix composition, which is the old split-read boundary made mechanical); the round-30 pointer-swap barrier now additionally asserts `{X, Y} ⊆ custody_protected_ids()` while Discard(X) is parked and Y is live (the real-controller linearizable state the split read could misreport); `test_expiry_sweep_skips_reserved_discard_target` runs an ACTUAL `sweep_sessions` off the atomic snapshot (reserved directory `skipped_active` with custody intact; released → `expired`, transient by construction); and `test_reservations_for_different_targets_are_independent` pins the two-simultaneous-reservations/release-one case in the snapshot. RETAINED untouched: all round-27/29/30 barriers, worker-stop-outside-lock, reservation-failure `finally` cleanup, admitted Start, and overlapping same-id Discard regressions (the round-30 UI stale-click and listing tests pass unchanged through the fake's mirrored snapshot). Composer verification pending (verify=composer); expected pytest 1182 + 4 = 1186.

#### PR-MED-001: `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/session.py:464-478,549-616` — transcription can start after Discard reserves custody
- Classification: Same-family
- Triage: Fix-now
- Fix route: premium-only
- Why it matters: `SessionController` promises all controls are safe from any thread (`session.py:250-253`), and the Discard guard's own comment says destroying the key under a live transcriber is wrong-by-contract (`:561-570`). The guard covers only transcribe-first ordering. In the inverse valid interleaving, a PROCESSING session has `transcribing == False`; thread A passes that check, reserves X, and releases `_lock` (`:549-593`); thread B then enters `transcribe()`, whose locked entry checks only state and `live.transcribing`, sets the flag, and begins using X's directory/crypto outside the lock (`:464-479`) without consulting the reservation; thread A reacquires `_lock` and calls key-first `discard_session()`, destroying that same crypto while the new transcriber is live (`:594-608`). The transcriber will normally fail closed before a durable transcript survives, and shipped GUI controls disable Discard while PROCESSING, so — consistently with the prior round-42 MED-003 and rounds-27/29/30 reachability grading — this is MED rather than HIGH. It still falsifies the any-thread custody contract and can leave an in-flight operation holding already-decrypted clinical buffers after Discard reports cryptographic deletion.
- Current behaviour: `discard()` tests `live.transcribing` only before its unlocked interval; `_custody_reservations` is consumed by generation acquisition, live Complete, recovered coordinator operations, snapshot consumers, and recovered-key destruction, but not by `transcribe()`.
- Desired behaviour: make Discard reservation and transcription acquisition mutually exclusive under `_lock` in both orders. Transcribe-first must keep making Discard refuse; Discard-first must make `transcribe()` refuse before invoking the transcriber or touching session crypto/store state. Preserve unrelated-session behaviour and keep the long transcriber call outside `_lock`.
- Pattern to follow: the round-27/29 consumer shape already used by `begin_generation()` and `complete()` — consult the controller-owned reservation while holding `_lock`, before installing an in-flight flag or crossing an unlocked boundary. Prefer an identity-scoped check against the PROCESSING live session id if avoiding coarse over-blocking is useful.
- Pattern siblings: the existing `test_discard_refused_while_transcribing` covers only transcribe-first ordering; add the inverse barrier beside it. No other surviving custody consumer gap was found in the final surface scan.
- Invariant: once Discard reserves session X, no transcription of X may begin or obtain X's crypto/store handle until that reservation is released; if transcription acquired first, Discard must refuse before reserving or deleting custody.
- Verification: add a deterministic two-thread rendezvous that parks Discard(X) after reservation on a PROCESSING session, attempts `transcribe()` from the other thread, and proves refusal occurs before the supplied transcriber callable runs and before any transcript/store mutation. Release Discard and prove it alone destroys X key-first and clears the reservation. Retain the existing transcribe-first refusal test, rounds-27/29/30/31 barriers, reservation-failure cleanup, worker-stop-outside-lock, admitted Start, and overlapping Discard regressions.
- Regression risk: a broad reservation check may transiently block transcription of a different live session Y while Discard(X) is parked after admitted Start; an identity-scoped check avoids that but must read the live snapshot and reservation map in the same critical section. Do not hold `_lock` across the transcriber call or `worker.stop()`.
- Scope-expansion disposition: In scope — Task 6.3 owns the any-thread custody coordinator and this is a same-family missing consumer of the reservation introduced for its two-lock Discard transition.

#### LEG 1 verification (executor `stage-6`, 2026-08-12, folded into this verify-then-fix leg per the practitioner's disposition)
- PR-MED-001: **real=yes; materiality=behavioral; severity=MED (agrees — the rounds-27/29/30/31 reachability grading); scope=IN-SCOPE; Same-family classification agreed.** Confirmed against the code: `transcribe()`'s locked entry checked only `_require_state(PROCESSING)` and `live.transcribing` — never `_custody_reservations` — so in the inverse of the round-42 guard's order, a PROCESSING-session Discard could reserve, release `_lock`, and a `transcribe()` entering the window would install its flag and hand X's directory/crypto to the transcriber outside the lock, with Discard's second section then destroying that crypto beneath it. The guard was one-sided; the reservation had every consumer except this one.
- /fix decision: Applied
- /fix notes: Applied the round-27/29 consumer shape exactly as prescribed, IDENTITY-SCOPED per the finding's own regression note: `transcribe()`'s locked entry now checks `live.session.session_id in self._custody_reservations` — the live snapshot and the reservation map read in the SAME critical section, so a different session Y installed by an admitted Start is never transiently blocked by X's reservation — refusing with a typed `SessionActivityError` BEFORE the `transcribing` flag is installed, before the transcriber callable is invoked, and before any crypto/store use; the long transcriber call stays outside `_lock` (unchanged), and transcribe-first still makes Discard refuse via the existing `transcribing` guard — mutual exclusion now holds in BOTH orders. Verification added as prescribed: `test_transcribe_refused_inside_discard_unlocked_window`, the deterministic inverse two-thread rendezvous (event barriers, no sleeps): Discard(X) of a PROCESSING session parks with the reservation held; `transcribe()` from the other thread refuses with the callable proven un-run and the key intact at that instant; the released discard alone reaches DISCARDED key-first with the reservation clearing. The existing transcribe-first refusal, rounds-27/29/30/31 barriers, reservation-failure cleanup, worker-stop-outside-lock, admitted Start, and overlapping-Discard regressions all retained untouched. HONEST-RESIDUE DOCUMENTATION applied per the practitioner's explicit decision (the reason the pass converges here): the `SessionController` class docstring no longer claims blanket any-thread safety — it states the ACCURATE contract (custody operations serialized through the lock + the per-session reservation, covering discard's window in both orders, sufficient for the shipped single-GUI-thread queued-signal usage) and NAMES the residue (full arbitrary-thread custody safety deferred to a future dedicated holistic serialization hardening; the six MED races of rounds 27–32 each needed a non-GUI-thread custody caller that shipped wiring does not have; do not add one without that hardening — candidate Phase-8 threat-model item); the round-42 discard-guard comment corrected to reference the stated contract and the now-two-sided mutual exclusion. #### MED-001: `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/ui/main_window.py:217` (`_on_recovered`) + `ui/recovery.py` `set_generation_blocked` — the residue guard leaves an in-flight recovery's view swap reachable during generation
- Triage: Fix-now
- Fix route: fix-on-fast (localized UI wiring; no custody-coordinator change)
- Why it matters: Phase 6 handed Phase 7 the explicit mandate to keep view swaps UNREACHABLE during generation, or a refused `_destroy_recovered_crypto` reference can be overwritten unzeroized. `set_generation_blocked` disables recovery BUTTONS (blocks a NEW resume start during a lease) but does not stop a resume that was ALREADY in flight when the lease was acquired. That resume's completion fires `_on_recovered` → `transcript_screen.show_document(...)` → `_reset_generation_state()` → `_release_lease()`, releasing the lease mid-review and swapping the view — silently discarding the in-progress note and dropping the lease's custody protection while the compose worker may still hold the queued session's `(directory, crypto)`; in the narrow sub-case where a recovered key is already retained, `_on_recovered`'s `_recovered_crypto = crypto` overwrites it unzeroized.
- Current behaviour: Generate can be clicked while a recovery resume is in flight; the resume completing then releases the held lease and swaps the view.
- Desired behaviour: a `GenerationLease` is never held concurrently with a recovery resume, so `_on_recovered` (fired only when a resume completes) can never run while a lease is held.
- Pattern to follow: the existing mutual-exclusion posture — `set_generation_blocked` already blocks a resume STARTING during a lease; add the symmetric guard blocking a generation STARTING during a resume (a click-time refusal like `recovery._selection_blocked`).
- Invariant: no `GenerationLease` is held while `RecoveryScreen.is_busy` is True; therefore `_on_recovered` never runs while `TranscriptScreen.is_busy`.
- Verification: offscreen test `test_generate_refused_while_recovery_in_flight` — with `recovery_busy_provider` returning True, `generate()` acquires no lease and surfaces a message (Generate also disabled); with it False, generation proceeds unchanged.
- Regression risk: the transcript screen's Generate enablement and the main-window construction wiring. Mitigated by the default `recovery_busy_provider=None` (no gating), so existing transcript tests are unchanged.
- Severity rationale (MED not HIGH): reachability needs two concurrent heavy-ML flows (a recovery resume plus a live session reaching QUEUED); the unzeroized-key sub-case is largely blocked by the checkout-exclusion invariant (a checked-out recovery blocks a second resume, so `_recovered_crypto` is normally None when a fresh `_on_recovered` fires); and no wrong-session data loss occurs (a QUEUED session's Complete/Discard/Start are all UI-unavailable after the swap). In-scope (Phase 7's residue mandate), Fix-now, non-production → headless AUTO-DISPOSABLE.
- /fix decision: Applied
- /fix notes: Added `recovery_busy_provider: Callable[[], bool] | None` to `TranscriptScreen`; `_update_controls` disables Generate while a recovery is in flight and `generate()` re-checks it at click time (fail-closed, like the recovery screen's stale-selection re-check) and refuses with a message before acquiring the lease. `MainWindow` passes `recovery_busy_provider=self._recovery_in_flight` (returns `recovery_screen.is_busy`). Combined with the existing `set_generation_blocked` (resume refused during a lease), the two flows are now mutually exclusive at their start points, so no lease is ever held while a recovery resume is in flight → `_on_recovered` can never fire during a lease → the view swap never releases a held lease and never overwrites a retained recovered key. Test `test_generate_refused_while_recovery_in_flight` added. Composer-VERIFIED green (round-34 resume): ruff clean, mypy strict 29 files, UI+lease suites 211 pass (+1, none regressed).

#### PR-MED-001: `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/ui/transcript.py:454` + `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/ui/note.py:447` — failed "complete without note" releases the generation lease permanently while review state remains live
- Classification: Pre-existing
- Triage: Fix-now
- Fix route: premium-only
- Why it matters: `abandon_note_and_complete()` releases the lease before calling `complete_without_note()`. That operation can fail after the note-removal attempt or during transcript fsync/decrypt verification, retaining the session key and leaving the controller QUEUED. `NoteScreen.abandon()` catches that failure and deliberately does not clear the review. The application is therefore still visibly mid-review, but the authoritative controller lease is already gone and `generation_active_changed(False)` has unblocked recovery. An unrelated recovery can now start and later swap the transcript view, clearing the retained review state; the original queued session has also lost the lease protection the UI still appears to rely on.
- Current behaviour: `TranscriptScreen._release_lease()` runs unconditionally before `controller.complete_without_note()`. There is no rollback or lease reacquisition if completion raises.
- Desired behaviour: the exact held lease remains authoritative until delete-note-and-complete succeeds. On failure, the same lease and review must remain held and recovery/view swaps must remain blocked.
- Pattern to follow: add a controller operation that consumes the exact `GenerationLease`, performs `complete_session(..., delete_note=True)` while that lease remains installed, and clears the lease only after success. A failure should leave the controller lease unchanged. The UI should clear its local lease and emit `generation_active_changed(False)` only after that operation succeeds.
- Pattern siblings: `save_note()` correctly releases only after `write_note()` returns; `complete()` and `complete_without_note()` already keep their state transition after successful custody verification.
- Invariant: a failed alternate terminal action must never turn an in-progress review into an unleased QUEUED session.
- Verification: traced `NoteScreen.abandon()` → `MainWindow._on_note_abandon()` → `TranscriptScreen.abandon_note_and_complete()` → `_release_lease()` → `SessionController.complete_without_note()`. Any exception from the final call returns to `NoteScreen.abandon()` before `clear()`, while the release signal has already unblocked recovery.
- Regression risk: combining lease consumption with completion must preserve key-retention-on-failure, note-unlink-first ordering, and the existing discard-reservation exclusion. Do not implement this as release-then-reacquire, which leaves a concurrency window.
- Scope-expansion disposition: In scope — this is the explicit Phase 7 delete-note exit and directly violates the phase's lease-lifecycle invariant.
- LEG-1 verification (executor `stage-7`, round 35): real=yes; materiality=behavioral; severity=MED (agree — latent-until-a-completion-failure, but a real lease-lifecycle inversion); scope=IN-SCOPE; Pre-existing agreed. Evidence: `transcript.py:462` calls `self._release_lease()` before `controller.complete_without_note()` at `:463`; on a `complete_without_note` raise, `NoteScreen.abandon()` (`note.py:452-458`) catches and does NOT clear, so the review stays visible while the controller lease is already gone and recovery unblocked.
- /fix decision: Applied
- /fix notes: Changed `SessionController.complete_without_note` to CONSUME THE EXACT held lease: `complete_without_note(lease: GenerationLease)` requires `self._generation is lease` (identity, like `with_generation_custody`), keeps the existing `_custody_reservations` exclusion, runs `complete_session(..., delete_note=True)` UNDER the lock (the pre-existing `complete()`/`complete_without_note()` pattern — no new unlocked window), and clears `self._generation = None` ONLY after the WRITTEN transition — so a completion failure (raise) leaves `_generation` unchanged (lease held), the session QUEUED, and the key retained (note-unlink-first inside `complete_session`). `TranscriptScreen.abandon_note_and_complete()` now calls `controller.complete_without_note(lease)` FIRST and only `_clear()`s (releasing the local mirror + emitting `generation_active_changed(False)`) AFTER it returns — on failure it raises to `NoteScreen.abandon()` with the lease + review fully held, recovery still blocked. Protocol + `FakeController` updated (fake consumes the lease on success, raises `generation_error` to simulate failure). Tests: `test_completion_failure_keeps_the_lease` (corrupt-transcript → `complete_without_note(lease)` raises → lease still held, key retained, QUEUED), `test_deletes_note_and_completes` / `test_completes_without_a_note_present` rewritten to pass the held lease and assert it is consumed, `test_refused_with_foreign_lease` (a non-held lease is refused, key intact); the UI `test_abandon_note_and_complete_without_one` still green (fake success path). Composer verification pending.
- /fix date: 2026-08-12
- /fix applied by: Claude Code (executor `stage-7`, round 35)

#### PR-MED-002: `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/ui/note.py:461` — the copy-enabled branch permits copying a note with unresolved blocking errors
- Classification: Pre-existing
- Triage: Fix-now
- Fix route: fix-on-fast
- Why it matters: the shipping constant currently prevents exposure, so this is MED rather than HIGH. However, the intended `copy_enabled=True` outcome makes the button and text selection available solely from the shipping flag. `_copy_note()` checks only that flag and that a note exists. A note containing pending proposals, reconstruction errors, contradictions, or another unresolved `error` can therefore enter the Windows clipboard and be pasted into Cliniko as soon as Task 9.1 flips the recorded decision. That violates the critical constraint that unresolved errors block copy.
- Current behaviour: `_apply_copy_binding()` enables selection and the Copy button whenever `_copy_enabled` is true. `_copy_note()` performs no blocking-warning, saved-state, or acknowledgement check. `test_copy_enabled_when_gate_passed` constructs the ordinary pending-proposal result and explicitly expects Copy to be enabled, pinning the unsafe state.
- Desired behaviour: the shipping flag is necessary but not sufficient. Copy and text selection must also require a ratified review state: no pending proposal or blocking error, the note successfully saved, and—if copy shares Complete's ratification bar—no unacknowledged review warning. The click handler must re-check the same condition.
- Pattern to follow: derive copy enablement from one pure state predicate, analogous to `complete_block_reason`, and apply it to both `copy_button` and `note_body` interaction flags. Re-evaluate it after every resolution, Save, acknowledgement, and retract.
- Pattern siblings: `save_button` already gates on `blocking_warnings()`; `TranscriptScreen` merges the live lease into Complete's state at every update.
- Invariant: no note carrying an unresolved error may reach any clipboard path, even after the Task 9.1 feature flag becomes true.
- Verification: read `_copy_note()` and `_apply_copy_binding()` at `ui/note.py:461–482`, then checked the enabled-outcome test at `tests/test_ui_screens.py:1674`; no other copy-state guard exists.
- Regression risk: disabling only the button is insufficient because selectable text retains native copy shortcuts. Both interaction flags and the explicit button must use the same predicate; the always-display-only transcript panel must remain unchanged.
- Scope-expansion disposition: In scope — Task 7.1 explicitly builds and tests both outcomes of the Task 9.1 copy binding.
- LEG-1 verification (executor `stage-7`, round 35): real=yes; materiality=behavioral (latent — the 9.1 flag is False today, but the enabled-path pins the unsafe state); severity=MED (agree — gated behind the flag); scope=IN-SCOPE; Pre-existing agreed. Evidence: `_apply_copy_binding` (`note.py:470-482`) sets note_body selectable + copy_button enabled on `_copy_enabled` alone; `_copy_note` (`:461-468`) checks only `_copy_enabled` + note-exists; `test_copy_enabled_when_gate_passed` asserts Copy enabled on the pending-proposal fixture — pinning the unsafe state.
- /fix decision: Applied
- /fix notes: Copy now shares Complete's ratification bar via one pure predicate. Added `_copy_ready()` = `self._copy_enabled and self._note is not None and models.complete_block_reason(self.current_review_state()) is None` — so copy is available ONLY when the 9.1 flag is on AND the note is fully ratified (no pending proposal, no blocking error, saved, no unacknowledged review — the same bar `complete_block_reason` enforces for Complete). `_apply_copy_binding` now derives BOTH the `note_body` interaction flags (selectable iff `_copy_ready()`, else `NoTextInteraction`) AND `copy_button.setEnabled(_copy_ready())` from it (button VISIBLE per the 9.1 flag, ENABLED per readiness); it is called from `_update_controls`, and `_acknowledge`/`_acknowledge_all` now also call `_update_controls` so acknowledgement re-derives readiness. `_copy_note()` re-checks `_copy_ready()` at click time (fail-closed). Test `test_copy_enabled_when_gate_passed` rewritten to `test_copy_enabled_only_for_a_ratified_note`: with the gate on, Copy stays DISABLED + note_body `NoTextInteraction` while proposals are pending; it becomes enabled + selectable only after confirm-all → acknowledge-all → save. `test_copy_disabled_when_gate_not_passed` / `test_default_copy_binding_ships_disabled` unchanged (flag off ⇒ never ready). Composer verification pending.
- /fix date: 2026-08-12
- /fix applied by: Claude Code (executor `stage-7`, round 35)

#### PR-MED-003: `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/ui/models.py:448` + `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/ui/note.py:422` — blocking provider-output errors advertise a Regenerate/retract clear-path that the UI cannot perform
- Classification: Pre-existing
- Triage: Fix-now
- Fix route: premium-only
- Why it matters: `source_coords_invalid` and `reconstruction_mismatch` can attach to provider/base transcript assertions. Those assertions have no proposal row, so they cannot be retracted. Their warning copy tells the clinician to "Regenerate the note, or retract the affected line," but no Regenerate/cancel-review control exists. Generate remains disabled because the current lease is held, and the only available escape is the destructive "Delete note and complete without one," which destroys the session key and eliminates the promised regeneration route. The checker fails closed, but the named recovery path is fictitious.
- Current behaviour: proposal controls are built only from `draft.note_proposals`; base assertions are display-only. The action area contains Save, delete-and-complete, and gated Copy. `TranscriptScreen` disables Generate while `_lease` is held, and no callback releases the draft without also completing the session.
- Desired behaviour: provide a visible, non-destructive Regenerate/cancel-review action that discards only the in-memory draft, releases the lease deliberately, retains the QUEUED transcript/key, returns to the Transcript screen, and makes Generate available again. Warning copy should name controls that actually exist.
- Pattern to follow: add a dedicated Note-screen callback to a `TranscriptScreen.cancel_note_review()` operation, distinct from `abandon_note_and_complete()`. It should reset `_generation_result` and review state, release the held lease, clear plaintext, and switch back to the Transcript tab without touching `note.enc`, transcript custody, or session state.
- Pattern siblings: compose failure already releases the lease while leaving the transcript available for retry; the new user-driven regenerate path should provide the same custody outcome after a draft has been presented.
- Invariant: every blocking warning displayed to the clinician has at least one visible, non-destructive clear-path when regeneration can resolve it.
- Verification: traced the warning copy at `ui/models.py:448–478`, proposal-row construction at `ui/note.py:253–296`, the complete action set at `ui/note.py:422–482`, and Generate enablement at `ui/transcript.py:268–284`. No base-assertion retract or regenerate callback exists.
- Regression risk: the regenerate action must not release the lease before the compose worker has finished, must not delete `note.enc` or the session key, and must clear both transcript-side review state and Note-tab plaintext so a stale note cannot later re-enable Complete.
- Scope-expansion disposition: In scope — Task 7.1 requires each blocking state to name its clear-path, and Flow 2 promises Regenerate as the alternative to deleting the note and completing.
- LEG-1 verification (executor `stage-7`, round 35): real=yes; materiality=behavioral; severity=MED (agree); scope=IN-SCOPE; Pre-existing agreed. Evidence: base transcript assertions are display-only (no proposal row — `note.py` `_build_proposal_rows` iterates `draft.note_proposals` only); `source_coords_invalid`/`reconstruction_mismatch` copy names "Regenerate the note, or retract the affected line" (`models.py` `WARNING_COPY`); Generate is disabled while `_lease` is held (`transcript.py` `_update_controls`); the only escape was the destructive abandon. Cancel is reachable only AFTER the draft is presented (Note tab shown ⇒ compose worker already returned), so releasing the lease at cancel is safe.
- /fix decision: Applied
- /fix notes: Added a NON-DESTRUCTIVE `TranscriptScreen.cancel_note_review()`: it `_release_lease()`s (deliberate — the compose worker has already returned by the time the Note tab shows a draft), resets `_generation_result` and `_note_review_state = NoteReviewState()`, and `_update_controls()` (re-enabling Generate), WITHOUT touching `note.enc`, transcript custody, or session state — the QUEUED transcript + key are retained for regeneration. The Note tab gained a `Cancel review and regenerate` button wired to a new `on_cancel` callback (`begin_review(..., on_cancel=...)`, default None); `cancel_review()` calls it then `clear()`s the Note-tab plaintext. `MainWindow._on_note_cancel` calls `transcript.cancel_note_review()` and switches back to the Transcript tab; `_on_draft_ready` passes `on_cancel=self._on_note_cancel`. The cancel button is enabled only while a draft is under review and not yet saved (pre-commit escape). Warning copy in `models.WARNING_COPY` updated to name controls that ACTUALLY exist: `source_coords_invalid`/`reconstruction_mismatch`/`role_unconfirmed` now say "Cancel the review and regenerate the note" (+ the role one names confirming the clinician on the Transcript screen); `contradiction`/`autofill_trigger_absent` name declining the proposed line OR cancel-and-regenerate. Tests: `test_cancel_note_review_is_non_destructive` (transcript-level: lease released, `_generation_result` cleared, no complete/discard/complete_without_note call, Generate re-enabled) and `test_cancel_review_calls_callback_and_clears` (Note-level). Composer verification pending.
- /fix date: 2026-08-12
- /fix applied by: Claude Code (executor `stage-7`, round 35)

A foreign token cannot pass the controller check. The UI's mutable-current-lease routing nevertheless lets a stale review action present the exact lease belonging to a later running worker; see PR-MED-001 below.

The new path does not delete custody. Its reset-to-no-note behaviour is incorrect when the draft is a regeneration over an already-saved `note.enc`; see PR-MED-002 below.
