#### SEC-001: `docs/security/threat-model.md:244-259` - the checker's documented limits predate the rounds 48-52 narrowings
- Severity: MED
- Triage: Fix-now
- Fix route: fix-on-fast
- Why it matters: this block is the artifact the practitioner relies on to know what the app does and does not catch, and it is the stated limit of a SAFETY control. It was written at internal Phase 8 (rounds 40-44) and says clinician-authored assertions are checked "against transcript CONTRADICTION (Check 2), and only when they parse to explicit structure". Rounds 48-52 then materially narrowed WHEN something parses, in ways a reader of this block cannot infer. The load-bearing sentence - a confirmed assertion the transcript is merely SILENT about is caught by no check - remains TRUE; what is missing is that the contradiction coverage itself shrank during hardening.
- Current behaviour: the doc mentions none of the four narrowings (grep for `preposed|interrogative|contrast boundary` over `docs/security/` returns zero). (1) A recognised QUESTION now contributes NO claim of any kind (round 50 PR-MED-002), so a contradiction stated interrogatively is not caught. (2) A preposed subordinate negation suppresses its main clause's laterality entirely - "Although no fever, right knee pain persists." yields no laterality claim (round 50 PR-MED-001 residue), so a genuinely contradicting laterality assertion passes. (3) Conversely, `is_interrogative` recognises a question only by a trailing `?`, a WH-opener, or an auxiliary + PRONOUN subject, so "Is the pain in your left knee" is treated as an assertion and CAN still yield a laterality claim - a residue in the opposite direction. (4) A differing dose now hard-blocks only with a positive administration/schedule witness present; bare product-strength wording grades `dose_mismatch` review and never blocks.
- Desired behaviour: extend the honest-limit block with these named residues and their DIRECTION (silence vs review vs block), so the doc states the coverage the shipped checker actually has. Keep the existing load-bearing sentence; this is an addition, not a rewrite.
- Pattern to follow: the same block's existing treatment of multi-interval recombination - state the mechanism, then state plainly what is and is not detected. Rounds 40-44 established that a control claim in these docs must say only what the structure enforces and must name residue.
- Pattern siblings: `design-system.md` and `data-flow-map.md` carry no Check-2 coverage claim (checked); `retention-schedule.md` is unaffected. This block is the only site.
- Verification: re-grep `docs/security/` for the residue terms and confirm each of the four appears with its direction; cross-check each against the code comment that records it (`_CONTRAST_TOKENS`, `_claims_from_tokens`'s modality gate, `_ADMINISTRATION_WITNESS`, and the `is_interrogative` bound recorded on `test_an_interrogative_yields_no_positive_laterality`).
- Regression risk: none - documentation only. Do NOT weaken the existing sentence about silent assertions while adding these.
- `/fix` decision: **Applied** - the honest-limit block gained a sub-bullet enumerating what "parses to explicit structure" EXCLUDES, grouped BY DIRECTION: three residues that fail toward silence (interrogative statements contribute nothing; preposed subordinate negation suppresses laterality; a dose blocks only with a positive administration/schedule witness, so bare product strength grades review) and, stated with equal care, the one that runs the other way (question recognition needs a `?` or an auxiliary + PRONOUN subject, so "Is the pain in your left knee" can still yield a claim - bounded, and the clinician's exits are unchanged). Each is cross-referenced to the mechanism that implements it and to rounds 48-52. The pre-existing sentence about silent assertions was left intact as required.
- `/fix` date: 2026-08-18
- `/fix` applied by: Claude Code

CHECKED AND CLEAN (categories examined that produced nothing, so coverage is auditable):
- **Custody ordering and crypto.** `complete_session` fails closed at every step (fsync -> decrypt-verify transcript -> verify note by decrypt/parse/session-binding/transcript-digest -> ONLY THEN delete key -> destroy in-memory copy); `discard_session` deletes the key FIRST; `write_note` re-verifies five independent relations before writing. `atomic_write_bytes` writes its temp INSIDE the target's directory and unlinks it in `finally`, so a crashed write cannot strand ciphertext outside the sweep's and `discard_session`'s reach - checked this round specifically.
- **Confirmation evidence chain.** Type-level requirement (proposal id + shown-text digest + config digest + a CONFIRMED decision), digest re-verified at `finalise_note` against the proposal and again at `write_note` against the assertion's own text, with the digest read back from the RENDERED widget. No path admits unconfirmed clinician text.
- **Injection and taint.** Spoken prompt injection has no instruction position to reach (`NoteRequest` has none by construction). Config text reaching note content is gated by per-assertion confirmation, and `_no_control_chars` rejects C0/C1/DEL plus Cf/Zl/Zp - so bidi overrides and zero-width characters cannot make displayed wording differ from digested wording. Path handling is pattern-validated (`SESSION_ID_PATTERN`, `resolve_key_path`, `_resolve_session_identity`); no `eval`/`exec`/shell; config JSON is parsed by pydantic with `extra="forbid"`.
- **Secrets and outbound.** No secrets in this phase (Cliniko credentials are Phase 4 / Credential Manager). No network surface: ruff bans `socket`/`http`/`urllib.request`/`QtNetwork`, offline env vars are set AND asserted at startup, and the no-sockets integration proof covers capture and transcription.
- **Resource and availability.** Input bounded (`MAX_ASSERTION_CHARS` 20k; config 2k assertion / 200 trigger / 120 label). Every regex on this surface inspected for catastrophic backtracking - `_DIGEST_RE`, `_ID_PATTERN`, `_NUMBER_WITH_SUFFIX_RE`, `_ATTACHED_STRENGTH_RE`, `_SESSION_ID_RE`, `_FORMAT_FIELD_RE` - all linear, no nested quantifiers over overlapping classes. Check 2's authored x quoted comparison is bounded by config size x segment count over small set intersections.
- **AuthN / AuthZ / SSRF / CSRF / session handling.** Not applicable to this surface: a single-user local desktop app with no request handlers, no server, no database and no browser rendering of untrusted content. The native-messaging handshake is Phase 1 and unchanged by this work.
- **Dependencies.** No new runtime dependency in Phase 3A.

ROUND-53 RESULT: 2 findings (CRIT 0 / HIGH 0 / MED 1 / LOW 1), both APPLIED in a same-session `/fix` leg (the `/security-review` pass itself edited nothing). #### PR-MED-001: `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/note_check.py:529,587-595,875-897,932-945,1096-1114` - `not only ... but ...` is split as ordinary contrast, erasing one side of a bilateral assertion and creating a false hard block
- Severity: MED
- Classification: Pre-existing in the hardening delta; fresh semantic-boundary sibling, not a re-opened Round 47-53 instance
- Triage: Fix-now
- Fix route: fix-on-fast
- Why it matters: error-grade `contradiction` warnings block Save, Copy and Complete. Two mutually consistent assertions can therefore dead-end the review solely because the contrast boundary mistakes correlative `not only ... but ...` for negation followed by an independent positive clause.
- Current behaviour: for `Not only the left knee but the right knee is sore`, `content_tokens` supplies `not`, `only`, `left`, `knee`, `but`, `right`, `knee`, ... . `_clause_tokens` retains `but` in the first clause and starts the second. The first clause carries `_NEGATIONS == {"not", ...}`, so its explicit `left knee` laterality is discarded; the second emits `right knee`. `_consolidated` therefore never sees both values and retains only `right`. A separate consistent assertion such as `The left knee was treated` retains `left` for the same `("laterality", "knee")` key, and either the authored/authored or authored/transcript comparison emits an error-grade contradiction even though the correlative assertion explicitly includes both knees. There is no `not only` pin in `test_note_check.py`.
- Desired behaviour: recognise this bounded correlative construction before applying the ordinary contrast split, or conservatively suppress its laterality comparison as a whole, so it cannot convert a bilateral statement into a one-sided claim. Preserve the existing true-positive pin for ordinary adversative wording (`No fever but right knee pain`) rather than weakening every `but` boundary.
- Pattern to follow: `_consolidated`'s existing bilateral invariant - if both side values are actually carried by one assertion, the anchor is dropped from comparison. The parser may either preserve both coordinated sides so that invariant applies, or fail toward silence when it cannot establish the construction safely.
- Pattern siblings: audit both members of `_CONTRAST_TOKENS` in non-independent constructions before declaring them unambiguous; in particular, `however` can introduce a concessive degree phrase rather than a new assertion. Any correction must be a closed positive grammar or conservative refusal, not a broader contrast deny-list.
- Invariant: clause splitting may limit negation scope only when the post-boundary words form an independently asserted clinical fact; it must never manufacture a single-valued claim from an assertion that semantically carries both values or remains under the original negation.
- Verification: add authored/authored and authored/transcript pins showing that the correlative bilateral assertion compared with a consistent left-only assertion and with a consistent right-only assertion produces no warning in either comparison direction. Retain the ordinary `No fever but right knee pain` true-positive pin, the clause-wide-negation false-positive pins, and the full Check-2 matrix.
- Regression risk: moderate. This is the parser's highest-risk boundary and a naive `but` special case can re-silence the Round-49 true positive; keep the construction recognition narrow and exercise both false-fire and missed-true-positive directions.
- Scope-expansion disposition: In scope
- `/fix` decision: **Applied**
- `/fix` notes: CONFIRMED empirically - `Not only the left knee but the right knee is sore` split into `[not, only, the, left, knee, but]` + `[the, right, knee, is, sore]`, the first clause's negation suppressed `left`, and the surviving one-sided `knee=right` produced an error-grade `contradiction` against a consistent `The left knee was treated.` FIX: a closed `_CORRELATIVE_AFTER_NOT` allow-list (`only`, `just`, `merely`, `simply`) detects `not` immediately followed by one of them anywhere in the assertion, and suppresses that assertion's LATERALITY entirely. Chosen over the alternative of making `not only`'s `not` stop counting as a negation, which would also have changed symptom negation AND affirmation - three behaviours instead of one, on the boundary that rounds 48-50 showed punishes wide changes; the rationale is recorded inline. Fail-toward-SILENCE, so no new contradiction can arise from it. Verified in BOTH directions the peer named: all three correlative forms now yield no laterality, AND the round-49 ordinary-adversative true positive still fires - the new pin asserts both in one test.
- `/fix` date: 2026-08-18
- `/fix` applied by: Claude Code

#### PR-LOW-001: `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/ui/note.py:12-17` - the new module docstring overclaims that all Informed Consent content is unwritable
- Severity: LOW
- Classification: Same-family documentation sibling in the hardening delta
- Triage: Fix-now

#### PR-LOW-002: `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/note_check.py:579-597` - `_clause_tokens` claims a content-free chunk cannot create a boundary, but terminal-only chunks deliberately do
- Severity: LOW
- Classification: Fix-induced documentation in Task H2.1
- Triage: Fix-now
- Fix route: fix-on-fast
- Why it matters: the false sentence sits on the central containment primitive and describes one of H4's explicitly requested edge cases. A maintainer following it could remove the punctuation-only boundary that prevents a negation or dose relation from crossing a visibly separate sentence.
- Current behaviour: the docstring says `a chunk that tokenises to nothing must not manufacture a boundary`. The loop independently calls `_ends_clause(raw)` after tokenisation and closes `current` even when `content_tokens(raw)` returned nothing. Thus a whitespace-separated terminal-only chunk in `pain . left knee` correctly creates the boundary between `pain` and `left`; only the empty clause object is later filtered out. This is equivalent to the pre-H2.1 behaviour and should not be changed.
- Desired behaviour: say that empty **clause objects** are dropped, while a content-free chunk ending in terminal punctuation may still close the populated current clause; a content-free non-terminal chunk is inert.
- Pattern to follow: `_ends_clause`'s preceding docstring, which accurately distinguishes closing an already-complete clause from dropping the empty remainder.
- Pattern siblings: the H2.1 CHANGELOG claim about structural containment and the individual `_Clause` helper docstrings match the code; this inaccurate edge-case sentence is the scoped residue.
- Invariant: comments on the clause walker must describe both effects independently - boundary recognition comes from raw punctuation, while retained clause content comes from normalised tokens.
- Verification: static re-trace of `pain . left knee`, bare `.`, an empty source, and a tokenless non-terminal chunk against the revised wording; no behaviour or test change is required for this finding.
- Regression risk: none - documentation only; changing the implementation would be a regression.
- Scope-expansion disposition: In scope
- `/fix` decision: **Applied** - the `_clause_tokens` docstring now states the two effects INDEPENDENTLY, as the finding's invariant requires: boundary recognition comes from the chunk's RAW punctuation, retained content from the NORMALISED tokens. It records that a content-free terminal chunk still closes the populated clause before it (deliberate, identical to pre-H2.1, and what stops a negation or dose relation crossing a visibly separate sentence), that a content-free non-terminal chunk is inert, and that only empty clause OBJECTS are dropped. No implementation change - the code was right and the sentence was wrong.
- `/fix` date: 2026-08-18
- `/fix` applied by: Claude Code

#### PR-MED-001: `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/note_check.py:902-909,935-965,1120-1151` - a recognised `not only ... but ...` correlative still turns an affirmed symptom into a negated claim and hard-blocks a consistent assertion
- Severity: MED
- Classification: Same-family parser sibling left open by Round 54's laterality-only repair; not a re-open of its named laterality instance
- Triage: Fix-now
- Fix route: fix-on-fast
- Why it matters: an error-grade `contradiction` blocks Save, Copy and Complete. The new correlative recogniser knows that `not only` is not ordinary clinical negation, but only the laterality branch consumes that knowledge; the symptom branch can still reverse the construction's meaning and dead-end two consistent assertions.
- Current behaviour: `_claims_from_tokens` sets `correlative=True` for `Not only pain but numbness persists.`. The laterality guard at line 926 consults it, but the negation loop does not. In the first clause, `not` is two tokens before `pain`, so lines 942-957 emit `("negation", "pain") = "negated"`. `Pain persists.` emits the same anchor with value `affirmed`; the pairwise loops at lines 1133-1151 therefore emit an error-grade `contradiction`. The no-write parser probe reproduced exactly those two structured values. The same path exists for every allow-listed form: `not just`, `not merely`, and `not simply`.
- Desired behaviour: once the bounded grammar establishes a correlative use of `not`, that assertion must not contribute a negated symptom claim from the correlative marker. Conservatively suppress the affected structured comparison (or the assertion's structured claims as a whole) rather than reinterpreting it as affirmation. Preserve the required whole-assertion `any_negation` behaviour for ordinary, non-correlative assertions.
- Pattern to follow: Round 54's own fail-toward-silence rationale. A grammar whose semantics cannot be established narrowly contributes no comparison claim; it must never contribute the opposite claim.
- Pattern siblings: all four `_CORRELATIVE_AFTER_NOT` entries, both authored/authored and authored/transcript comparison directions, and both assertion orders. Dose parsing was traced separately and did not produce an additional verified error path from this construction.
- Invariant: a token recognised as a correlative scope marker must not simultaneously act as clinical negation in another claim family and manufacture an error-grade opposite value.
- Verification: add pins for `Not <only|just|merely|simply> pain but numbness persists` against an affirmed `Pain persists` assertion in authored/authored and authored/transcript directions; assert no warning in both orders. Retain ordinary `No pain reported` versus `Pain persists` contradiction pins and the required whole-assertion `any_negation` pins.
- Regression risk: moderate. A broad change to `_NEGATIONS` would alter ordinary symptom negation and affirmation, the rejected Round-54 route. Keep the correlative handling local and fail toward silence.
- Scope-expansion disposition: In scope
- `/fix` decision: **Applied**
- `/fix` notes: CONFIRMED - `Not only pain but numbness persists.` emitted `pain = negated` against a consistent `Pain persists.` FIX: a correlative `not` (immediately followed by `only`/`just`/`merely`/`simply`) is no longer a negation SOURCE for the symptom branch, via the new local `_is_correlative_not`. It STILL counts toward the whole-assertion `any_negation`, so the symptom falls to SILENCE rather than becoming newly affirmed - the conservative direction the finding asked for, leaving the required whole-assertion behaviour untouched for ordinary assertions.
- `/fix` date: 2026-08-18
- `/fix` applied by: Claude Code

#### PR-MED-002: `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/note_check.py:539-541,902-909,911-935` and `desktop/tests/test_note_check.py:563-598` - the adjacency-only, assertion-global correlative recogniser both misses a one-sided false-block form and silences an unrelated laterality claim
- Severity: MED
- Classification: Incomplete Round-54 grammar plus a fix-induced missed-true-positive direction
- Triage: Fix-now
- Fix route: fix-on-fast
- Why it matters: the recogniser is the sole guard against a contrast split manufacturing one side of a bilateral assertion. Under-matching leaves the original error-grade false block reachable; over-matching needlessly removes a valid laterality comparison from an unrelated clause. The existing test proves only the one prefix form and an ordinary adversative, so neither direction is pinned.
- Current behaviour: detection requires `not` to be immediately followed by one of four words anywhere in the assertion, but does not require a coordinating `but` or paired clinical values. Consequently `Not the left knee only but the right knee too.` is not recognised (`not` is followed by `the`): the negated first clause drops `left`, the second emits `knee=right`, and comparison with a consistent `knee=left` assertion produces an error-grade contradiction. In the opposite direction, `Not just yet. The left knee is sore.` is recognised even though there is no correlative coordination; because `correlative` is assertion-global, the valid second-clause `knee=left` claim is suppressed. The no-write probe reproduced `{knee: right}` and `{}` respectively, while `The left knee is just sore.` correctly remained `{knee: left}`.
- Desired behaviour: recognise only a closed, positively established correlative coordination and cover its bounded prefix and postposed-marker forms; then conservatively prevent that coordination from becoming a single-valued comparison. An adjacent `not just/merely/simply` with no coordinating evidence must not suppress laterality in a different clause.
- Pattern to follow: `_dose_binding`'s closed-positive-grammar posture: establish the complete relation before emitting or suppressing a claim, and treat ambiguity as silence only for the ambiguous construction itself rather than the entire unrelated assertion.
- Pattern siblings: prefix `not <only|just|merely|simply> X but Y`; postposed `not X only but Y too`; authored and transcript paths share `_clause_tokens`, so one parser fix must be pinned through both. Broader natural-language synonyms are outside this finding unless deliberately added to the closed grammar.
- Invariant: correlative handling may neither convert an assertion carrying both sides into one side nor erase a side from an independent clause merely because an adjacent lexical pair occurred elsewhere in the assertion.
- Verification: add direct structured-claim and warning-level pins for (1) the existing prefix form against consistent left-only and right-only assertions, (2) the postposed-marker form in both comparison directions, (3) `Not just yet. The left knee is sore.` retaining `knee=left`, and (4) ordinary `The left knee is just sore.` plus `No fever but right knee pain` staying live. Cover authored/authored and authored/transcript paths without weakening the full Check-2 matrix.
- Regression risk: high at this parser boundary. An over-broad `but` exception can re-silence Round 49's true positive; an adjacency-only exception repeats Round 54's class miss. Keep recognition closed and exercise both false-fire and missed-match directions.
- Scope-expansion disposition: In scope
- `/fix` decision: **Applied**
- `/fix` notes: BOTH directions CONFIRMED - `Not the left knee only but the right knee too.` yielded `{knee: right}` (under-match, false block still reachable) and `Not just yet. The left knee is sore.` yielded `{}` (over-match, an unrelated clause silenced). FIX: laterality now consults NO VOCABULARY AT ALL. Round 54's word-list is removed from that path and replaced by `suppressed_anchors` - if a laterality pair was DISCARDED by clause negation, that anatomy anchor is dropped from the whole assertion. That is the GENERAL form of what the word-list reached for: it keys on the SUPPRESSION EVENT rather than on wording, so it covers every word order (prefix and postposed-marker alike) and cannot silence a clause with nothing suppressed. `_CORRELATIVE_AFTER_NOT` survives only where adjacency genuinely is the right test - PR-MED-001's symptom branch. Verified against every accumulated pin (rounds 48/49/50/54 laterality and negation cases, the bilateral `_consolidated` drop, and the `The left knee is just sore.` control): all unchanged.
- `/fix` date: 2026-08-18
- `/fix` applied by: Claude Code

#### PR-MED-001: `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/note_check.py:906-940,979-1002,1152-1170` and `desktop/tests/test_note_check.py:519-537,563-664` - laterality suppression loses side identity, erasing a valid same-side claim while shared-anatomy bilateral wording still becomes one-sided
- Severity: MED
- Classification: Mixed - a fix-induced Round-55 missed-true-positive path plus a pre-existing same-family false-block sibling exposed by the required bilateral invariant; neither is a re-open of a named Round-55 instance
- Triage: Fix-now
- Fix route: premium-only
- Why it matters: Check 2 is the only automated contradiction guard for confirmed laterality assertions. The first direction silently misses a real left-vs-right discrepancy; the second direction manufactures an error-grade `contradiction` that blocks Save, Copy and Complete for mutually consistent wording. Together they show the laterality class is not converged in either safety direction.
- Current behaviour: (1) `suppressed_anchors` is a `set[str]`. `No pain in the left knee. The left knee was strapped.` first adds `knee` when the negated clause discards `left`, later emits a valid `knee=left`, then lines 979-985 remove it solely because the anchor was seen under negation. The no-write warning probe compared that assertion with an exactly quoted `right knee` assertion and returned `[]` instead of a contradiction. (2) Laterality binds only when a side token directly precedes anatomy. `Left and right knee pain.` therefore ignores `left`, emits `knee=right`, and `_consolidated` sees only one value; compared with an exactly quoted left-knee assertion, the no-write probe returned `[('contradiction', 'error')]`. Reversing the wording (`Right and left knee pain.`) reverses the surviving one-sided value. This second mechanism existed at `27c1aed`, but it is in scope for this whole-Check-2 convergence round and was specifically exposed by pressing whether ANY bilateral construction still becomes single-valued.
- Desired behaviour: decide laterality once per anatomy anchor from value-aware observations across the whole assertion. A suppressed `left knee` followed by an independently positive `left knee` must retain the positive left claim; a suppressed left plus positive right must still drop the anchor under the required bilateral invariant. A closed shared-anchor coordination such as `left and right knee` / `right and left knee` must contribute both values to `_consolidated` or fail wholly toward silence - never emit only the side adjacent to the shared anatomy token.
- Pattern to follow: the H2.1 structural-containment lesson and `_consolidated`'s existing whole-assertion contract. Collect per-anchor side VALUES (positive and suppressed) first, then make one emission/drop decision. Do not add another assertion-global anchor set or word-order special case whose state cannot distinguish same-side from opposite-side evidence.
- Pattern siblings: prefix and postposed `not` correlatives; same-side and opposite-side positive sentences after `no` / `not` / `never` / `denies` / `denied` / `without`; `left and right knee`, `right and left knee`, and the same shared-anchor shape with `both`, `or`, or an article; authored/authored and authored/transcript comparisons in both assertion orders. Keep independent anchors (`left shoulder and right knee`) independently comparable.
- Invariant: one assertion may yield a single laterality value for an anatomy anchor only when the parser has mechanically established that no observed or closed-coordinated evidence in that assertion carries the other side. Negated same-side context must not erase an independent positive claim; opposite-side or bilateral context must never collapse to one side.
- Verification: add direct structured-claim AND warning-level pins for the two reproduced traces. For suppression, cover same-side-positive (must retain and catch a real opposite-side conflict) and opposite-side-positive (must remain silent under the bilateral invariant), through authored/authored and authored/transcript directions. For shared anatomy, compare each bilateral word order with both left-only and right-only assertions and require no warning; prove the parser produced both values before consolidation or deliberately produced none. Retain every rounds-48-55 pin, especially `No pain in the left knee` silence, `No fever but right knee pain` firing, both correlative word orders, `Not just yet` firing, and whole-assertion `_consolidated`.
- Regression risk: high at this parser boundary. A side-insensitive suppression keeps the present missed true positive; treating every repeated laterality token as one anchor can silence independent body regions; emitting the suppressed value can reintroduce the original negated-clause false block. The class is locally closable inside Check 2, but only with a value-aware per-anchor accumulator and closed shared-anchor grammar, not another one-line predicate.
- Scope-expansion disposition: In scope
- `/fix` decision: **Applied**
- `/fix` notes: BOTH mechanisms CONFIRMED before editing - `No pain in the left knee. The left knee was strapped.` lost its valid `knee=left` (round 55's anchor-only suppression), and `Left and right knee pain.` bound only the side adjacent to the anatomy, emitting one-sided `knee=right` (pre-existing; reversing the wording reversed the survivor). FIX, both halves exactly as prescribed: (1) `suppressed_anchors: set[str]` replaced by `observed_sides: dict[str, set[str]]`, recording every SIDE seen for an anchor whether asserted or discarded by clause negation; an anchor is dropped only when MORE THAN ONE side was observed - the exact meaning of 'this assertion carries both sides' - so a same-side negation no longer erases a later independent positive claim, while opposite-side context still drops the anchor. (2) A closed shared-anchor coordination `<side> (and|or <side>)* <anatomy>` (`_LATERALITY_CONJUNCTIONS`, `_laterality_sides`) contributes BOTH sides to the anchor, so `left and right knee` / `right and left knee` fail wholly toward silence rather than emitting the adjacent side. Verified against every accumulated pin (rounds 48/49/50/54/55, the bilateral `_consolidated` drop, the `just sore` control): all unchanged. Two pins added. Suites: pytest 1263, ruff clean, mypy clean.
- `/fix` date: 2026-08-19
- `/fix` applied by: Claude Code (Fable 5.1)

That latter loss is the intended safe direction under the REQUIRED whole-assertion bilateral invariant: the assertion discusses both knees, so Check 2 stays silent rather than guessing which statement another assertion should be compared with.
- Closed-coordination audit: **PARTIAL PASS.** Within the literal grammar `<side> (and|or <side>)* <anatomy>`, `and`, uncertainty-bearing `or`, longer alternating chains, a leading `both`, and either word order all contribute both values and are dropped. A side that also directly precedes a different anatomy remains independently bound (`left and right knee and left shoulder` drops `knee` but retains `shoulder=left`). The later side's own scan does duplicate evidence already emitted by the first side's scan, but it cannot change the result: two-valued anchors are removed by `observed_sides`; same-valued duplicates collapse to one value in `_consolidated`.
- Closed-coordination audit failure: `_laterality_sides` accepts only a conjunction immediately followed by a side. Therefore the ordinary article form `the left and the right knee` does not bind `left`; the later scan still sees the direct pair `right knee` and emits it alone. This is not an exotic out-of-grammar challenge: Round 56's own `Pattern siblings` expressly required the shared-anchor form "with ... an article". The same fall-through is reachable through authored punctuation that `note_config._ALLOWED_IN_CLAIM_PUNCT` deliberately permits.
- Focused no-write verification: with an exactly quoted left-knee assertion, each authored assertion `the left and the right knee is sore`, `left and/or right knee pain`, `left & right knee pain`, and `left, right knee pain` produced `[('contradiction', 'error')]`. The probe used `.venv\Scripts\python.exe -B` with `PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1`; `git status --short` was byte-for-byte unchanged afterward. No pytest, ruff or mypy run, as instructed. Operator-supplied baseline retained: pytest 1263, ruff clean, mypy strict clean across 29 files.
- Deliberate residues honoured and not re-filed: preposed subordinate-negation laterality silence; the bounded `is_interrogative` recogniser; bare product-strength dose review grading; arbitrary-thread custody; same-user hand-crafted `GeneratedNote`. Whole-assertion `any_negation` and bilateral `_consolidated` remain REQUIRED invariants.
- Whole-delta result outside the finding: the H2.1 clause shape, dose binding/context/evidence, value-aware negation handling, retention/config/documentation precision, consent reminder, warning clear-path, plain-text error labels, and custody/UI comments match their enforcing code. No additional concrete correctness, custody, logging, UI or documentation issue survived verification.

#### PR-MED-001: `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/note_check.py:379-400,949-976,1014-1020`, `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/note_config.py:407-427`, and `desktop/tests/test_note_check.py:692-710` - unrecognised material inside shared-anchor coordination still lets its final side emit alone
- Severity: MED
- Classification: Same-family - the value-aware accumulator is correct, but the new closed coordination recogniser covers the named `and|or` instance rather than the whole fall-through class; this does not re-open any Round-56 named reproduction
- Triage: Fix-now
- Fix route: premium-only
- Why it matters: Check 2 turns this shallow parse into an unacknowledgeable `error` that blocks Save, Copy and Complete. Ordinary bilateral wording can therefore hard-block a note that is fully consistent with either side, which violates the checker's fail-toward-silence contract.
- Current behaviour: `_laterality_sides` returns both values only while every connector is literally `and|or` immediately followed by another side. With `the left and the right knee`, the first scan returns `None` at the article; the second scan sees bare `right knee`, records only `right`, and the final `observed_sides` filter has no opposite value to remove. Public-warning probes produced the same one-sided error for `and/or`, `&`, and comma. Those authored forms are reachable because `_ALLOWED_IN_CLAIM_PUNCT` deliberately accepts slash, ampersand and comma; the article form is ordinary spoken/transcript wording and was explicitly named by Round 56.
- Desired behaviour: no assertion containing two recognised opposite side tokens that plausibly share one anatomy anchor may yield a single error-grade laterality claim for that anchor. A fully recognised coordination may contribute both values; an unrecognised/ambiguous coordination must fail wholly toward silence. Explicit independent anchors such as `left shoulder and right knee` must remain independently comparable.
- Pattern to follow: keep the value-aware per-anchor decision, but allocate/mark side tokens in a structural pass before emission. For a candidate direct pair, an opposite side token in the same clause that is not independently bound to a different explicit anatomy must make that anchor ambiguous and suppress it. This closes connectors generically; do not add `the`, `&`, comma, `and/or`, `nor`, or other spellings one by one.
- Pattern siblings: optional/doubled articles (`left and the right knee`, `the left and the right knee`); every punctuation form the authoring boundary deliberately accepts (`&`, comma, slash/`and/or`); `nor`; longer chains mixing recognised and unrecognised material; authored/authored and authored/transcript comparisons in both orders. Controls: `left shoulder and right knee`, `left and right knee and left shoulder`, same-side repetition, and the Round-56 negated-same-side/opposite-side cases.
- Invariant: a single laterality value may enter the error-grade comparison population only after all recognised side tokens in its clause have either been allocated to explicit independent anatomy anchors or shown not to make this anchor bilateral/ambiguous. Unallocated opposite-side evidence can only remove a claim, never leave the adjacent final side as the survivor.
- Verification: add direct `_claims_from_tokens` and public `contradiction_warnings` pins for the four reproduced forms, each against left-only and right-only assertions and in authored/transcript order both ways; require no one-sided claim and no warning. Add the independent-anchor and mixed-chain controls above so the fix does not become assertion-global suppression. Retain all accumulated rounds 48-56 pins and the REQUIRED whole-assertion `any_negation`/`_consolidated` tests.
- Regression risk: high at this parser boundary. An assertion-global "saw both sides" flag would wrongly conflate independent body regions; a new connector allow-list simply repeats the rounds 54-57 failure pattern. Side allocation must be clause-local and anchor-aware, while the existing whole-assertion same-anchor consolidation remains intact.
- Scope-expansion disposition: In scope
- `/fix` decision: **Applied**
- `/fix` notes: CONFIRMED before editing - the article form, `&`, `,` and `and/or` each yielded a one-sided `knee=right` against a quoted left-knee assertion. FIX, exactly the shape prescribed and nothing wider: `_laterality_sides` now returns POSITIONS; the scan ALLOCATES each side position it binds and skips already-allocated sides (ending the duplicate emission the peer noted); after the scan, every side token in the clause left UNALLOCATED has its value unioned into `observed_sides` for every anchor bound in that clause. Unallocated opposite-side evidence can therefore only REMOVE a claim by making its anchor two-sided - never leave the adjacent side as the survivor - and it is clause-local and per-anchor, so `left shoulder and right knee` keeps two independent claims and `left and right knee and left shoulder` drops only `knee`. No new connector list. Verified by a 20-case probe: all five fall-through forms silent, all three independence cases correct, every accumulated pin from rounds 48/49/50/54/55/56 unchanged. Two pins added (fall-through forms; independent anchors incl. the mixed case). Suites: pytest 1265, ruff clean, mypy clean.
- `/fix` date: 2026-09-03
- `/fix` applied by: Claude Code (Fable 5.1)

ROUND-57 RESULT: 1 fresh finding (CRIT 0 / HIGH 0 / MED 1 / LOW 0), VERIFIED and APPLIED as the clause-local, anchor-aware allocation pass the peer prescribed; suites 1265, ruff + mypy clean. This was the ONE structural attempt the peer's verdict allowed before re-grading. The value-aware per-anchor accumulator closes Round 56's negated-same-side defect, and the exact `and|or` coordination closes its named bilateral wording, but the peer-prescribed design AS IMPLEMENTED does not close the class because its own required article sibling and config-admitted punctuation still fall through to a one-sided error. **CONVERGENCE VERDICT: NOT CONVERGED.** Further LOCAL work can still close the bounded exact-side-token class with a clause-local, anchor-aware allocation/ambiguity pass; another connector/phrase exception cannot. On the present evidence I would keep the error-grade contract for one such structural attempt rather than re-grade immediately. If that pass still leaves a one-sided bilateral trace, the operator should stop parser patching and introduce an acknowledgeable laterality-mismatch review code, leaving error-grade `contradiction` to the mechanically stronger symptom and exclusive-dose cases.

#### PR-MED-001: `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/note_check.py:564,625-664,950-1004,1203-1234`; `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/note.py:261-279`; `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/ui/models.py:495-545` - a shared-anchor correlative still becomes a one-sided error after the one structural attempt
- Severity: MED
- Classification: Behavioral - fresh post-fix under-suppression trace, not a re-opened Round 47-57 named instance
- Triage: Fix-now
- Fix route: premium-only
- Why it matters: an explicitly bilateral/correlative statement can still manufacture an unacknowledgeable `contradiction` error, blocking Save, Copy and Complete even though the compared single-side assertion is consistent with part of what was said. This is the exact decisive condition Round 57 set: a one-sided bilateral trace remains after the single clause-local, anchor-aware structural attempt.
- Current behaviour: `_clause_tokens` splits immediately after `but`. For `Not only left but right knee pain.`, clause one has an unallocated `left` but no `clause_anchors`, so lines 998-1004 cannot attach that evidence anywhere; clause two emits `knee=right`. The whole-assertion accumulator therefore never sees both values, `_consolidated` retains the lone claim, and `contradiction_warnings` routes a differing laterality value through the generic error-grade `_contradiction_warning`. Reversing left/right is symmetric, and the same false block occurs whether the bilateral wording is clinician-authored or exactly quoted from the transcript.
- Desired behaviour: stop extending or restructuring the laterality parser. A differing laterality claim must emit a dedicated acknowledgeable review code (for example `laterality_mismatch`) rather than error-grade `contradiction`, for authored/authored and authored/quoted comparisons alike. Keep error-grade `contradiction` for the mechanically stronger negated-symptom and exclusive same-state dose cases; keep `dose_mismatch` unchanged.
- Pattern to follow: mirror the existing `dose_mismatch` severity split at the comparison boundary, keyed on `key[0] == "laterality"`: register one review-severity code in `NOTE_WARNING_SEVERITY`, emit it through a small warning constructor in both comparison loops, and add matching actionable `WARNING_COPY`. Do not add another connector, correlative or cross-clause parsing exception.
- Pattern siblings: `not just|merely|simply left but right knee`, optional articles (`not only the left but the right knee`), reversed sides, clinician-authored versus clinician-authored comparisons, and both authored/quoted input directions. Controls: ordinary single-side left/right mismatches now grade review; negated-symptom mismatches and mechanically-exclusive same-state dose mismatches remain errors; low-confidence and `dose_mismatch` reviews remain unchanged; `NOTE_WARNING_SEVERITY` and `WARNING_COPY` stay in exact parity.
- Invariant: no laterality parse, however plausible, may create an unacknowledgeable error. Laterality differences are review-only; the clinician must still acknowledge them before completion, while the generic `contradiction` error remains reserved for stronger mechanically-established classes.
- Verification: pin the named trace and its reversed-side sibling through direct claims and public warnings; exercise bilateral wording as authored and exact-quoted input, plus authored/authored. Update the existing simple left/right mismatch expectation from `contradiction/error` to the dedicated review code, then prove negated-symptom and exclusive-dose controls remain `contradiction/error`. Pin registry/copy parity and the Note-tab acknowledgement gate for the new code. Run the supplied desktop QA only after the change.
- Regression risk: re-grading deliberately permits a clinician to save a wrong-side assertion after seeing and acknowledging the warning; that is the chosen responsibility boundary after the shallow parser failed its final structural attempt. The material implementation risks are accidentally re-grading symptom/dose contradictions too, omitting the new UI copy/acknowledgement path, or leaving one comparison loop on the old error grade.

- PRACTITIONER RATIFICATION (2026-09-03): the re-grade weakens a control the plan made blocking on purpose - a genuine left/right flip surfaces for acknowledgement instead of blocking Save/Copy/Complete. Put to the practitioner as the decided step under the peer's stopping rule (one structural attempt, then re-grade), with the decline path stated; the practitioner chose RATIFY: task + scoped review-plan + build now. This is the responsibility boundary after the shallow parser failed its final structural attempt, exactly as rounds 21-24 drew it for dose with `dose_mismatch`.

- **MED-001 - `main` discards every already-scored recording when one recording fails outside its three caught classes.** `speaker_eval.py:818-829`: the per-recording loop catches `RecordingRefusedError`, `HarnessFaultError` and `OSError` only. A `TranscriptionError`/`SpeechError` from the pipeline, a CTranslate2 `RuntimeError` on one oversized file, a `StoreCorruptError` from the temporary store, or the harness's own success-path `SpeakerEvalError` ("temporary store not fully removed") propagates out of `main`, and because `render_report` runs only after the loop (`:830-831`) the table for every recording already scored is lost with it - the practitioner re-runs the whole set (model load plus every transcription) after removing one file, against the task's Done-when "point the script at a folder and receive the table". Current behaviour: the run aborts with a traceback and no table. Desired behaviour: catch `Exception` per recording, print `[error] <file>: <ExceptionType>: <message>`, continue, still print the table, and exit 1 whenever any recording errored (harness faults included) or nothing was scored; `KeyboardInterrupt` stays uncaught. The exception text is echoed - this project's pipeline and store exceptions carry no transcript content by construction (they describe rates, paths, records and models); third-party exception text is NOT audited, a residue to name in the code. Pattern to follow: `benchmark.run_all`'s per-model failure isolation (a failed candidate is a reported failure, never a lost run). #### PR-MED-001: `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/speaker_eval.py:840` — generic exception messages can disclose transcript content on stdout

- Triage: Fix-now
- Fix route: fix-on-fast
- Why it matters: The report is intended for redirection or pasting into the tracked plan. A provider or validation exception containing decoded clinical text would place that text directly in stdout, violating the task’s absolute text-freedom constraint and potentially carrying it into version control.
- Current behaviour: The generic handler prints the uncontrolled message verbatim: `print(f"[error] {wav_path.name}: {type(exc).__name__}: {exc}")` at line 849. `transcribe_session` invokes and iterates the third-party provider without sanitising its exceptions (`transcription.py:947`, `:804`), so an exception whose message includes a partial decode reaches this line. Lines 844–847 explicitly acknowledge that third-party messages are unaudited; naming the residue does not enforce the invariant.
- Desired behaviour: Generic errors must print only pre-approved structural information, such as filename and exception type. Only explicitly audited first-party exceptions may expose a fixed safe message.
- Pattern to follow: Fail closed over the whole uncontrolled exception surface; do not enumerate presently-known safe third-party messages.
- Pattern siblings: The Round 60 test at `test_speaker_eval.py:939` pins the unsafe raw-message output. The successful-result sentinel test at lines 582–595 never exercises an exception path.
- Invariant: No transcript text appears in any printed line, returned structure, or representation, including failure paths.
- Verification: A provider raising `RuntimeError("<clinical sentinel>")` propagates through `transcribe_session` and is printed verbatim by line 849. Nothing redacts or structurally confines `str(exc)`.
- Regression risk: Diagnostic detail is reduced. Preserve the filename and exception type, and test that a sentinel carried in an exception message is absent while the run still continues and exits 1.
- /fix decision: Applied
- /fix notes: `speaker_eval.py` `main` - a fourth `except SpeakerEvalError` branch echoes this module's OWN exception text (fixed text plus a temp path); the generic branch now prints `[error] <file>: <ExceptionType>` - type only, never the message - and its comment states the rule (text this module builds from names, label spellings, formats and paths may be echoed; anything else is confined, not described). Sibling under the same rule: `read_wav_pcm`'s non-WAV refusal no longer embeds the stdlib `wave.Error` text. Pin flipped per the peer's Regression-risk shape: `test_one_failing_recording_does_not_lose_the_others` raises a `RuntimeError` carrying a sentinel and asserts the sentinel is ABSENT while the run continues, the table prints and exit is 1; a third stem raising `SpeakerEvalError` asserts the module's own text IS echoed. Invariant re-read after the fix: no `str(exc)` of a non-module exception reaches `print` anywhere in `main`. Verification composer-run (verify=composer), pending at hand-off.
- /fix date: 2026-09-04
- /fix applied by: Claude Code

#### PR-MED-002: `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/speaker_eval.py:290` — tied truth durations are converted into alphabetically biased role correctness

- Triage: Fix-now
- Fix route: fix-on-fast
- Why it matters: A cluster containing equal clinician and patient duration has no majority, but the harness can score it CORRECT merely because `clinician` sorts before `patient`. That biases the role-accuracy evidence used to confirm or reverse Task 2.2.
- Current behaviour: Alignment selects `max(sorted(overlap.items()), key=...)` at line 290, so an exact tie becomes the alphabetically first label while merely being marked `mixed`. `role_outcome` repeats the same lexical tie-break at line 448. Line 470 then reports CORRECT whenever that selected label is `clinician`.
- Desired behaviour: A tie must not establish a clinician majority. Under the stated “CORRECT iff majority is clinician; WRONG otherwise” contract, a preselected tied cluster scores WRONG and exposes no majority label.
- Pattern to follow: Preserve ambiguity explicitly rather than encoding it as an arbitrary role label. If cluster metrics must retain deterministic scoring for mixed segments, carry separate tie/overlap evidence for the role verdict.
- Pattern siblings: Both per-segment alignment ties (`:290`) and aggregate per-cluster ties (`:448`) require coverage, including a three-label top tie.
- Invariant: Role correctness must not depend on the lexical spelling or ordering of role labels.
- Verification: For equal clinician/patient overlap, `sorted(...)` places `clinician` first; Python’s `max` retains the first equal-key item. The resulting truth is credited wholly to clinician at lines 435–442 and can satisfy line 470.
- Regression risk: Only ambiguous tied cases should change. Add exact-tie tests for alignment and `role_outcome`, while preserving existing non-tied three-speaker behavior.
- /fix decision: Applied
- /fix notes: `_SECONDS_EPSILON` (1e-9 s, named beside `MIXED_MAJORITY_SHARE`). `align_segments` ranks by seconds and a lead within the epsilon is a TIE -> `SegmentTruth(true_label=None, mixed=True, tied=True)` (new `tied` field, default False, so positional construction is unchanged). `role_outcome.majority` returns None on a top tie -> WRONG with `majority_true_label=None` (the Design Decision's "otherwise"); no `sorted(...)` over label NAMES remains on the verdict path at either site. `RecordingResult.tied_count` (new) with `unlabelled_count` restricted to zero-overlap segments, and a report note per recording naming tied segments ("no majority, counted as mixed, scored by nobody"); the `clinician_cluster` name tie-break at the reported-share site is kept and its docstring now says it only picks whose share is REPORTED. Tests: alignment exact tie -> no label / tied / mixed / share 0.5; a lead beyond float noise is not a tie; role: a tied preselected cluster is WRONG for a label sorting BEFORE `clinician` (`assistant`) and AFTER it (`patient`), a three-label top tie is WRONG, a lead beyond noise is CORRECT; recording level: a tied segment is mixed + tied, not unlabelled, metrics None, noted in the report. Pending composer verification.
- /fix date: 2026-09-04
- /fix applied by: Claude Code

#### PR-MED-003: `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/speaker_eval.py:225` — empty and frame-truncated WAV data pass the format boundary

- Triage: Fix-now
- Fix route: fix-on-fast
- Why it matters: A header-only WAV is accepted, produces an empty document and a `RecordingResult`, and can make `main` exit 0 even though neither condition was scored. A truncated or odd-byte PCM payload can be silently shortened or fail later as an unrelated generic model/harness error. Both undermine the measurement input contract.
- Current behaviour: After checking channels, width and rate, line 238 returns `reader.readframes(reader.getnframes())` without requiring non-empty PCM or verifying the returned length against the declared frame count and frame size. `main` treats any returned `RecordingResult` as success at line 852.
- Desired behaviour: Refuse zero-frame recordings, short reads, and data not comprising complete mono PCM16 frames, using the existing conversion/help message.
- Pattern to follow: Read enough to expose a trailing partial frame, then validate non-empty data, exact declared length, and divisibility by `channels * sample_width` before building a store.
- Pattern siblings: `test_speaker_eval.py:362–391` covers metadata and non-WAV bytes but not a valid empty WAV, truncated declared data, or an odd data-chunk length.
- Invariant: Every successful recording result derives from a non-empty, complete sequence of 16 kHz mono PCM16 frames.
- Verification: CPython 3.14’s `wave` reader derives `nframes` using floor division and may return fewer bytes than requested on a truncated chunk. The harness performs no subsequent length check. Empty PCM flows through an empty store and empty transcript, after which `results` is non-empty and line 852 returns 0.
- Regression risk: Deliberately empty exports will change from an unscored “successful” row to a per-file refusal, which is the intended contract.
- /fix decision: Applied
- /fix notes: `read_wav_pcm` refuses `nframes == 0` ("holds no audio frames") and a short read (`len(pcm) != frames * BYTES_PER_SAMPLE`: "truncated: its header declares N frames but only M could be read; re-export it"), both naming the file and carrying the conversion recipe, both BEFORE any store exists; the odd-trailing-byte case is stated in the docstring as a residue (floored away by `wave` before it is visible, half a sample, not detectable without `wave`'s private chunk size) rather than claimed. Tests: a header-only WAV is refused; a WAV with 1000 bytes chopped off its tail is refused as truncated. Invariant re-read: every returned `pcm` is non-empty and exactly `frames * 2` bytes. Pending composer verification.
- /fix date: 2026-09-04
- /fix applied by: Claude Code

#### PR-MED-004: `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/speaker_eval.py:830` — the documented ASCII-output fix does not cover dynamic fields

- Triage: Fix-now
- Fix route: fix-on-fast
- Why it matters: A valid Unicode recording stem, role label, or model-directory name can still raise `UnicodeEncodeError` under the Windows redirected-output code page that motivated Round 60 LOW-002. The progress print is outside the per-recording `try`, and report encoding happens after all transcription, so either failure can lose the whole run’s table.
- Current behaviour: Lines 106–109 claim ASCII-only output, but line 830 prints `wav_path.name`; lines 681, 709–714, 718, and 732–742 interpolate `model_name`, recording names, and unrestricted role labels without encoding or escaping.
- Desired behaviour: Make the output boundary reliably UTF-8 or ASCII-safe for every dynamic field, not only fixed literals.
- Pattern to follow: Configure stdout explicitly for UTF-8 before output, or pass every dynamic field through one lossless safe-rendering helper.
- Pattern siblings: Progress, skip/error lines, the summary table, notes, confusion headings, labels, and mapping rows all share this boundary.
- Invariant: Every valid input name and role label must be reportable without aborting the run.
- Verification: `parse_audacity_labels` accepts Unicode role labels, and Windows accepts Unicode filenames. Those values reach the cited formatting sites unchanged; no test replaces stdout with a restricted-code-page stream.
- Regression risk: Explicit UTF-8 may alter byte encoding for redirected output, but Markdown output should be UTF-8. Add a Unicode filename/label test using a restricted text stream or an explicit encoding assertion.
- /fix decision: Applied
- /fix notes: `_configure_output()` is the first statement of `main` (before argparse, so usage and `--help` output share the boundary): stdout and stderr are reconfigured to UTF-8 with `backslashreplace` when they expose `reconfigure`; a replaced stream without it is left alone and the docstring claims only that ("this helper can only configure a `TextIOWrapper`"). The round-60 comment on `WAV_FORMAT_HELP` is rewritten to claim only that the LITERALS are ASCII (so the table reads the same on any code page) and to name `_configure_output` as what actually keeps a run alive for the dynamic fields. Test: `sys.stdout` replaced by a strict cp1252 `TextIOWrapper` over a `BytesIO`, a Unicode stem (`recō`) and role label (`patiēnt`) run through `main` with faked models/evaluation, exit 0, both present intact in the UTF-8 bytes. Pending composer verification.
- /fix date: 2026-09-04
- /fix applied by: Claude Code

#### PR-LOW-001: `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/speaker_eval.py:291` — mathematically exact 80% cases can be counted as MIXED through float error

- Triage: Fix-now
- Fix route: fix-on-fast
- Why it matters: This misreports the mixed-segment count that bounds achievable accuracy, although it does not change the chosen label or score.
- Current behaviour: Overlaps are accumulated as binary floats and line 298 uses the strict comparison `share < MIXED_MAJORITY_SHARE`. For spans `0.3–0.7` and `0.7–0.8`, the mathematical share is exactly 80%, but ordinary double arithmetic produces approximately `0.7999999999999999`, marking it mixed.
- Desired behaviour: Treat values equal to 0.8 within a deliberately small numerical tolerance as meeting the boundary.
- Pattern to follow: Use an explicit epsilon or `math.isclose` around the threshold while keeping materially-under-80% values mixed.
- Pattern siblings: None found.
- Invariant: Exactly 80% is not MIXED, independent of the absolute timestamp origin.
- Verification: The existing boundary test at `test_speaker_eval.py:414–424` uses the numerically favorable `4 / 5` case and does not exercise non-zero decimal timestamps.
- Regression risk: Only values within floating-point noise of the threshold change classification.
- /fix decision: Applied
- /fix notes: `_SHARE_EPSILON` (1e-9, named beside `MIXED_MAJORITY_SHARE`); `mixed = tied or share < MIXED_MAJORITY_SHARE - _SHARE_EPSILON`. Test with the peer's own 0.3/0.7/0.8 spans (not mixed, share approx. 0.8) plus a materially-under case (0.3/0.69/0.8, mixed); the existing exact-4/5 boundary test kept. Pending composer verification.
- /fix date: 2026-09-04
- /fix applied by: Claude Code

Exact ties are rare with float VAD boundaries but real with equal-length labelled spans, and the bias is toward CORRECT for the common role names - the role-accuracy evidence is exactly what confirms or reverses Task 2.2. The peer's odd-byte case is half-moot: `wave` floors `nframes`, so a trailing odd byte is dropped before the harness sees it (half a sample, harmless) and is not detectable without `wave`'s private chunk size - name it as a residue rather than claim it; scope: yes (`read_wav_pcm` is a Task 2.3a helper and the input contract says REFUSE); production-impacting: no; recommendation: Fix-now - refuse `nframes == 0` and `len(pcm) != nframes * BYTES_PER_SAMPLE` with a `WavFormatError` naming the file (empty / truncated, plus the existing conversion recipe), two tests (header-only WAV; truncated data chunk). **Decision (leg 2, 2026-09-04): Applied** - composer verification pending.
- PR-MED-004: materiality=behavioral; verified severity=med; peer severity=med (preserved); evidence: CONFIRMED, and it is the "claim outruns the enforcing structure" family: the round-60 LOW-002 comment at `speaker_eval.py:106-110` asserts ASCII-only output, but only the LITERALS are ASCII - `wav_path.name` is printed at `:830` (the `[run ]` line, OUTSIDE the per-recording `try`) and in every `[skip]` / `[harness-fault]` / `[error]` line; `model_name` at `:681`; `result.name` and the practitioner's role labels at `:709-714`, `:718`, `:732-742`; `LabelTrackError` messages echo label spellings (`:203-210`). `parse_audacity_labels` accepts any Unicode label and Windows filenames are Unicode, so under the very redirected-cp1252 stdout the comment describes, `print` raises `UnicodeEncodeError`: at `:830` it aborts the loop, at `:851` it aborts after every transcription and the whole table is lost. No test replaces stdout with a restricted-code-page stream; scope: yes; production-impacting: no; recommendation: Fix-now - make the boundary enforce it: reconfigure `sys.stdout` at the top of `main` (`errors="backslashreplace"`, guarded with `getattr(sys.stdout, "reconfigure", None)` because a replaced stream may not be a `TextIOWrapper`) so no dynamic field can abort the run, rewrite the comment to claim only that, and pin it with a `TextIOWrapper(BytesIO(), encoding="cp1252")` stdout carrying a Unicode stem and label. **Decision (leg 2, 2026-09-04): Applied** (UTF-8 + `backslashreplace`, stdout and stderr) - composer verification pending.
- PR-LOW-001: materiality=behavioral; verified severity=low; peer severity=low (preserved); evidence: CONFIRMED - `speaker_eval.py:291-298`: `share = seconds / sum(overlap.values())` then `share < MIXED_MAJORITY_SHARE`; the peer's spans reproduce by hand: `0.7 - 0.3 = 0.39999999999999997`, `0.8 - 0.7 = 0.10000000000000009`, share `0.7999999999999998 < 0.8` -> MIXED at a mathematically exact 80 %. Affects only the MIXED count (label and score unchanged), and the existing boundary test uses the exactly-representable `4 / 5`; scope: yes; production-impacting: no; recommendation: Fix-now - compare against `MIXED_MAJORITY_SHARE - 1e-9` (constant named beside it), keep the existing boundary test and add the peer's decimal-origin case. **Decision (leg 2, 2026-09-04): Applied** - composer verification pending.
