#### PR-MED-001 fix confirmation — CONFIRMED

The uncontrolled exception surface is confined on the shipped CLI path. The generic branch prints only `type(exc).__name__` at `speaker_eval.py:911-921`; neither `{exc}`, `str(exc)`, nor `repr(exc)` is emitted. The module-owned refusal/fault branches at `:903-910` echo only messages constructed at the audited raise sites (`:193-223`, `:249-270`, `:678-680`, `:706-709`). In particular, the non-WAV wrapper at `:266-271` no longer interpolates the stdlib exception.

The result structures at `:348-371`, `:438-464`, and `:527-556` contain only labels, names, counts, seconds and verdicts; no exception or `TranscriptDocument` is returned or retained. The pin at `test_speaker_eval.py:997-1023` carries `zebrafruit` inside `RuntimeError`, proves it absent from stdout, proves processing continues, and proves exit status 1. No reachable third-party or pydantic message/repr channel remains on the current `main` call graph.

#### PR-MED-002 fix confirmation — NOT CONFIRMED

The two correctness sites named by round 62 are fixed correctly:

- `align_segments` ranks by duration, detects a lead within `_SECONDS_EPSILON`, and emits `true_label=None`, `mixed=True`, `tied=True` at `speaker_eval.py:326-338`.
- `role_outcome.majority` returns `None` for an epsilon tie, making the verdict WRONG at `:486-518`.
- Ties are separated from zero-overlap segments at `:621-623` and explained in the report at `:771-775`.
- The spelling-order, three-label, non-tied and recording-level pins are at `test_speaker_eval.py:457-481`, `:614-635`, and `:701-712`.

Those changes leave non-tied results alone outside the named epsilon. However, the requested class-wide property is not complete: lexical tie-breaking remains in reported metrics at `speaker_eval.py:397-415` and `:496-509`. See PR-MED-005.

#### PR-MED-003 fix confirmation — NOT CONFIRMED

The zero-frame and short-read checks are correct and occur before store creation. `speaker_eval.py:253-265` refuses `frames == 0` and requires `len(pcm) == frames * BYTES_PER_SAMPLE`, with the filename and conversion recipe present in both messages. CPython 3.14 derives `_nframes` by floor-dividing the data-chunk size by frame size (`C:\Python314\Lib\wave.py:271-272`), and `readframes` can return fewer bytes than requested (`:360-375`), so the short-read check catches physically truncated data as intended. Pins are present at `test_speaker_eval.py:395-410`.

The odd-trailing-byte residue at `speaker_eval.py:240-242` is nevertheless closable without private state: CPython’s public `readframes` does not cap the request at `getnframes()`. Requesting `frames + 1` reads to the data-chunk boundary, exposing an odd final byte through an odd returned length. See PR-LOW-002.

#### PR-MED-004 fix confirmation — CONFIRMED

`_configure_output` safely probes `reconfigure` with `getattr` and calls it only when callable at `speaker_eval.py:845-855`. It is the first statement of `main` at `:861`, before argparse or any output. Consequently, it covers unpaired/empty-input lines (`:884-890`), progress and every error branch (`:897-921`), and the complete table, notes, confusion headings, labels, mappings and model name through the final report print (`:922-923`).

A normal replacement without `reconfigure`, such as `StringIO`, is left untouched and continues accepting Unicode. The restricted-code-page pin at `test_speaker_eval.py:1025-1048` uses a strict cp1252 `TextIOWrapper`, a Unicode stem and role label, and verifies intact UTF-8 output. The behavioural fix is complete. A stale `render_report` docstring still overclaims ASCII-only output; see PR-LOW-003.

#### PR-LOW-001 fix confirmation — CONFIRMED

The comparison direction at `speaker_eval.py:328-337` is correct: `share < MIXED_MAJORITY_SHARE - _SHARE_EPSILON` treats an exact or epsilon-under 80% share as meeting the boundary while preserving MIXED for a materially smaller share. The decimal-origin and materially-under pins are at `test_speaker_eval.py:472-481`.

No other fixed share threshold has the same float-noise shape. Both duration-majority sites use `_SECONDS_EPSILON`. The exact comparison used to select a duration-optimal cluster mapping is a separate tie-selection defect covered by PR-MED-005.

#### Post-Fix Regression Check

- PR-MED-001 changed neither `main`’s signature nor its continue/exit contract. Generic diagnostics lose message text by design; refusals and module-owned faults retain their prior classifications.
- PR-MED-002 leaves the `align_segments` and `role_outcome` signatures unchanged. `SegmentTruth.tied` has a default, preserving existing positional construction. `RecordingResult.tied_count` is new, but repository search found no construction outside `score_document` and its tests. Only ties within `1e-9` change classification. The end-to-end mock fixture has no tied segments.
- PR-MED-003 changes only invalid empty/truncated inputs into `WavFormatError`, which remains a `RecordingRefusedError` caught as `[skip]`. Valid WAVs and the end-to-end fixture are unchanged.
- PR-MED-004 changes no return type or exit code. The only production caller is `scripts/measure-speakers.py:23-26`, which delegates directly to `main`.
- PR-LOW-001 changes only `mixed_count` within numerical noise of 80%; selected labels and cluster scoring are unchanged.
- No new caller breakage or masked end-to-end expectation was found. The four issues below are incomplete-class or independently missed findings, not downstream regressions.

#### PR-MED-005: `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/speaker_eval.py:397` — lexical tie-breaking remains in measurement metrics

- Triage: Fix-now
- Fix route: fix-on-fast
- Why it matters: Duration-optimal mappings can tie while producing very different unweighted accuracies. The reported count accuracy can therefore depend on role-label spelling, and an exactly tied clinician-cluster choice can report a different talk-time share based on the predicted cluster name.
- Current behaviour: `cluster_metrics` sorts both label sets at `:397-398`, enumerates permutations at `:409`, and replaces the winner only for strictly greater seconds at `:412-415`; the first lexically induced equal-seconds mapping wins, and `correct_count` is calculated under it. Separately, `role_outcome` uses the cluster name as the final key at `:496-509`.
- Desired behaviour: Equal-duration alternatives must not let label spelling choose a different numeric metric. Use count as a secondary mapping objective within a seconds epsilon, then preserve or expose any remaining ambiguity without a lexical semantic choice. An equal-clinician-seconds cluster choice should likewise report ambiguity rather than select by cluster name.
- Pattern to follow: The new `majority` handling at `:486-494`—detect ambiguity explicitly instead of converting it into a label.
- Pattern siblings: The confusion tuple sorting at `:431` affects presentation order only, and purity at `:417-420` retains only the maximum numeric value; neither changes a score.
- Invariant: Renaming a true role or predicted cluster without changing assignments and durations must not change accuracy counts or clinician talk-share evidence.
- Verification: A matrix where `p1→A=2s/1 segment`, `p1→B=1s/1`, `p2→A=2s/1`, and `p2→B=1s/10` gives two mappings worth 3 seconds, but counts of 2 versus 11. Lines `397-415` select whichever mapping is enumerated first, so changing the lexical order of A/B changes `accuracy_count` from `2/13` to `11/13`.
- Fix recommendation: Compare mapping seconds with `_SECONDS_EPSILON`, use correct count as a secondary objective, and pin invariance under renamed/reordered labels. Represent an exact clinician-cluster tie explicitly and add a pin with equal clinician seconds but different evidence talk shares.
- Regression risk: Existing mapping rows for genuinely tied recordings may change; unique duration-optimal mappings must remain byte-for-byte equivalent.
- /fix decision: Applied
- /fix notes: `cluster_metrics`: the mapping objective is now seconds, then segment count, with seconds compared within `_SECONDS_EPSILON`; `correct_count` is the winning mapping's own count; whatever still ties is numerically invisible (same seconds, same count) and the comment says so. `role_outcome`: the clinician cluster is ranked by clinician seconds and reported as None when the top two are within the epsilon - no cluster name decides; docstring updated. Pins: the peer's 13-segment matrix under both label orderings (`alpha`/`beta` and `beta`/`alpha`) reports count 11 of 13 and the same seconds accuracy; two clusters holding the clinician equally report no share while a 5-versus-4 lead reports the leader's 0.7. Invariant re-read: no `sorted(...)` or `min(..., name)` over label or cluster NAMES remains on any numeric path (`predicted_labels` / `true_labels` stay sorted for presentation and enumeration order only, which the count objective makes irrelevant). Pending composer verification.
- /fix date: 2026-09-04
- /fix applied by: Claude Code

#### PR-MED-006: `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/speaker_eval.py:674` — a cleanup exception can leave a recoverable temporary recording

- Triage: Fix-now
- Fix route: fix-on-fast
- Why it matters: The harness promises key-first destruction on every path. If deleting `key.dpapi` raises, the DPAPI-wrapped key and encrypted audio can remain together under the temporary directory, creating an uncontrolled recoverable copy of clinical audio.
- Current behaviour: The cleanup statements at `speaker_eval.py:674-676` are sequential. `discard_session` calls `delete_session_key` before destroying the live key or removing the directory (`session_store.py:698-706`). If that unlink raises, `crypto.destroy()` and both `rmtree` calls are skipped. The generic CLI handler then prints only the exception type, giving no cleanup-residue warning or location. `temp_root` is also created before `SessionCrypto()` at `speaker_eval.py:653-656`, so a constructor failure leaves an empty harness directory.
- Desired behaviour: Live-key destruction and removal attempts must run even when key-file deletion raises; any remaining recoverable store must produce a module-safe, explicit cleanup fault.
- Pattern to follow: Nested `try/finally` cleanup in which every later safety action runs independently of earlier cleanup failures.
- Pattern siblings: `test_speaker_eval.py:881-887` verifies provider failure only when all cleanup operations succeed; it does not inject a key-deletion or removal failure.
- Invariant: After every return or exception, no temporary directory may retain both ciphertext and usable key custody, and the in-memory `SessionCrypto` must be destroyed.
- Verification: An `OSError` from `session_store.py:703` exits `discard_session` before lines `704-706`; because `speaker_eval.py:675` itself raises, line `676` is never reached.
- Fix recommendation: Put key deletion, unconditional `crypto.destroy()`, and root removal in independently guaranteed cleanup legs; verify residue on failure as well as success and raise a safe `SpeakerEvalError` naming the cleanup path/remediation. Add fault-injection pins for key deletion and directory removal.
- Regression risk: Cleanup faults may supersede or chain with the original provider exception; preserve the original as context while making any recoverable residue unmistakable.
- /fix decision: Applied
- /fix notes: `SessionCrypto()` is constructed before `mkdtemp`. The teardown is one function, `_destroy_temporary_store(temp_root, session_dir, crypto)`: the key unlink sits in a `try` whose `finally` destroys the in-memory key unconditionally (an `OSError` from the unlink is recorded by type name, never swallowed), then `rmtree(session_dir)`, then `rmtree(temp_root)` (both `ignore_errors=True`, so every leg runs), then a residue check that raises `SpeakerEvalError` naming the root and whether the key blob is STILL PRESENT (recoverable by this Windows user - delete by hand) or only ciphertext remains, plus the unlink refusal's type. `_transcribe_in_temporary_store` calls it after success and inside `except BaseException` on the failure path (bare `raise` after), so a residue fault supersedes the original exception with the original attached as `__context__` by implicit chaining. `discard_session` is no longer imported. The module docstring's custody paragraph is rewritten to exactly that structure, including "what the harness cannot do is delete a file the OS refuses to delete - named, never hidden". Pins, CI-safe on `_destroy_temporary_store` directly: a refused key unlink still destroys the key and the directory legs remove the store; with the directory legs stubbed to keep everything, the residue message names the path and the key state for both a refused and a successful unlink with `crypto.destroyed` in both; a raising `SessionCrypto` leaves no temp root. Windows pin: provider raise plus stubbed removals -> `SpeakerEvalError` whose `__context__` is the provider's `RuntimeError`, the key already unlinked in the leftover, which the test then removes. Pending composer verification.
- /fix date: 2026-09-04
- /fix applied by: Claude Code

#### PR-LOW-002: `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/speaker_eval.py:240` — odd WAV data chunks are detectable through the public API

- Triage: Fix-now
- Fix route: fix-on-fast
- Why it matters: The current code and docstring accept a malformed PCM16 data chunk while claiming detection would require private `wave` state. The ignored byte is only half a sample, so measurement impact is negligible, but the input-boundary claim is incomplete.
- Current behaviour: `read_wav_pcm` requests exactly the floored `getnframes()` count at `:253-259`; an odd final data byte remains unread and the returned even-length PCM passes validation.
- Desired behaviour: Read enough to expose any partial frame and refuse it using the existing conversion message.
- Pattern to follow: Validate the complete data-chunk payload, not only the floored frame count.
- Pattern siblings: None found.
- Invariant: Every accepted data-chunk byte belongs to a complete mono PCM16 frame.
- Verification: CPython 3.14 computes `nframes = chunksize // framesize`, but `readframes(n)` passes the requested byte count directly to `_Chunk.read`, which clamps only at the actual data-chunk size. `reader.readframes(frames + 1)` therefore returns `frames * 2 + 1` bytes for an odd chunk without private access.
- Fix recommendation: Request one additional frame, then require the returned length to equal `frames * BYTES_PER_SAMPLE` and be divisible by the frame size; add a handcrafted odd-data-chunk pin.
- Regression risk: Only malformed WAVs with a trailing partial sample change from accepted to refused.
- /fix decision: Applied
- /fix notes: `read_wav_pcm` requests `frames + 1` from `readframes`; a read longer than `frames * BYTES_PER_SAMPLE` is refused as a partial trailing sample (naming the stray byte count), a shorter one as truncated (unchanged); the docstring's wrong "not detectable without private state" residue is gone and the over-read mechanism is stated instead. Pin: a hand-built WAV (`_raw_wav`, raw RIFF header) with an odd data chunk is refused, and the same layout with an even payload reads back exactly. Pending composer verification.
- /fix date: 2026-09-04
- /fix applied by: Claude Code

#### PR-LOW-003: `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/speaker_eval.py:724` — `render_report` still claims its Unicode-capable output is ASCII-only

- Triage: Fix-now
- Fix route: fix-on-fast
- Why it matters: The stale contract obscures why `_configure_output` is required and incorrectly suggests direct `render_report` callers may safely write its result through any ASCII/code-page stream.
- Current behaviour: The docstring at `:724-729` says the table is “ASCII-only,” while model names, recording names and unrestricted role labels are interpolated at `:731`, `:759-764`, and `:786-797`. The Unicode pin at `test_speaker_eval.py:1025-1048` proves the returned report is intentionally non-ASCII.
- Desired behaviour: State that fixed literals are ASCII, dynamic fields remain Unicode, and only `main` configures the output stream.
- Pattern to follow: The corrected `WAV_FORMAT_HELP` comment at `speaker_eval.py:113-119`.
- Pattern siblings: No other surviving ASCII-only claim found in the scoped files.
- Invariant: Documentation must not attribute stream safety to the report string when the actual control is `_configure_output`.
- Verification: `render_report` has no call to `_configure_output`; it returns the interpolated Unicode string unchanged at `:818`.
- Fix recommendation: Correct the docstring; no behavioural change is needed.
- Regression risk: None; documentation-only.
- /fix decision: Applied
- /fix notes: `render_report` docstring now reads: literals ASCII; dynamic fields (recording stems, role labels, the model name) whatever Unicode the inputs carried; stream safety belongs to `main`'s `_configure_output`, not to this string. Docstring only. Pending composer verification.
- /fix date: 2026-09-04
- /fix applied by: Claude Code

#### LEG 1 verified tuples (executor `claude-fable-5-1`, 2026-09-04)
- PR-MED-005: materiality=behavioral; verified severity=med; peer severity=med (preserved); evidence: CONFIRMED - `cluster_metrics` sorts both label sets (`speaker_eval.py:397-398`), enumerates permutations over the sorted target list (`:409`) and replaces the winner only on strictly greater seconds (`:412-414`), so among duration-tied mappings the first enumerated one - the one induced by the sorted SPELLING of the true labels - wins, and `correct_count` / `accuracy_count` (`:415`, `:427`) are computed under it; the peer's matrix (p1->A 2 s/1 seg, p1->B 1 s/1, p2->A 2 s/1, p2->B 1 s/10) yields two 3-second mappings with counts 11 versus 2, and renaming A/B flips which one is reported. `role_outcome:497-501` keys the reported clinician cluster on `(-seconds, name)`, so an exact clinician-seconds tie between two majority-clinician clusters lets the cluster NAME choose whose `talk_time_share` is reported. Reachable only on exact float ties (bounded), but it is the un-closed remainder of round 62's PR-MED-002 class - renaming must not change a reported number; scope: yes; production-impacting: no; recommendation: Fix-now - mapping objective `(seconds within _SECONDS_EPSILON, then correct_count)`, after which any remaining tie is numerically invisible (identical seconds and count; only the mapping's label pairing can differ, which is presentation); on an exact clinician-seconds tie report `clinician_cluster_talk_time_share=None` (ambiguity exposed, never resolved by name); pins: label-renaming invariance of `accuracy_count` on the peer's matrix, and equal clinician seconds with different evidence shares -> None. **Decision (leg 2, 2026-09-04): Applied** - composer verification pending.
- PR-MED-006: materiality=behavioral; verified severity=med (NOT high - reasons follow); peer severity=med (preserved); evidence: CONFIRMED - `speaker_eval.py:674-676` runs `discard_session` then `rmtree(temp_root)` sequentially in one `finally`; `discard_session` (`session_store.py:698-706`) calls `delete_session_key` (`:579-582`, a bare `unlink(missing_ok=True)`) BEFORE `crypto.destroy()` and its own `rmtree`, so an `OSError` from that unlink skips the in-memory key destruction and both directory removals and leaves `key.dpapi` + `audio.enc` (+ `transcript.enc`) together under `%TEMP%\scribe-speaker-eval-*\<id>\`; the raise inside `finally` replaces any in-flight exception and, after PR-MED-001, `main` prints only `[error] <file>: PermissionError` - no residue location. `temp_root` (`:653`) precedes `SessionCrypto()` (`:656`), so a constructor failure would leave an empty harness directory (no data). Why MED and not HIGH: (1) the trigger is an OS-level refusal to unlink a file this process itself just wrote in the user's own temp directory (a scanner or indexer holding it open at that instant) - never a normal path; (2) exposure is bounded to the same Windows user (DPAPI current-user scope), who already holds the plaintext WAV on the same disk - nothing crosses the same-user boundary the threat model already accepts as its residual; (3) the run still FAILS visibly (non-zero exit, exception type) - what is missing is the residue's location and the destroy/removal fallbacks, not the failure signal; (4) the shape is inherited from the app's own `discard_session`, the shipped custody primitive the Task 2.3a Design Decision deliberately reuses "without a new custody path", whose retain-on-I/O-error behaviour is the documented best-effort posture (rounds 42-44) - the harness does not weaken the app. What is genuinely wrong is the module docstring's "every path" promise outrunning the structure (the project's central lesson); scope: yes; production-impacting: no; recommendation: Fix-now - construct `SessionCrypto()` before `mkdtemp`; independent cleanup legs (key unlink, unconditional `crypto.destroy()`, session-directory removal, temp-root removal, each guaranteed by its own `try/finally`); residue check on BOTH the success and the failure path, raising or chaining a `SpeakerEvalError` that names the leftover path and the by-hand remediation (module-own text, so `main` echoes it); fault-injection pins for a raising key unlink and a failing directory removal asserting `crypto.destroyed`, the residue message, and the original provider exception preserved as context. If the practitioner grades custody findings HIGH on principle, the composer should surface this one; the evidence above is why this seat did not. **Decision (leg 2, 2026-09-04): Applied** at MED (composer-disposed, practitioner notified) - composer verification pending.
- PR-LOW-002: materiality=behavioral; verified severity=low; peer severity=low (preserved); evidence: CONFIRMED on the module's public contract, not on its source - this seat's read of `C:\Python314\Lib\wave.py` was permission-refused and not retried, so the evidence is (a) the peer's cited lines (`_nframes = chunksize // framesize` at `:271-272`; `readframes` passing `nframes * framesize` straight to `_Chunk.read`, which clamps only at the data-chunk boundary, `:360-375`) and (b) `wave`'s long-standing documented behaviour that `readframes(n)` returns UP TO the data chunk's remaining bytes - so `readframes(frames + 1)` returns `frames * 2 + 1` bytes for an odd chunk, `frames * 2` for a clean one and fewer for a truncated one, all through the public API. My round-62 docstring claim at `speaker_eval.py:240-242` that this needs private state is therefore WRONG and is itself part of the fix; the fix leg's handcrafted odd-data-chunk pin proves the mechanism under composer verification; scope: yes; production-impacting: no; recommendation: Fix-now - request `frames + 1`, require exactly `frames * BYTES_PER_SAMPLE` bytes back (longer = partial final frame, shorter = truncated, both `WavFormatError` with the recipe), correct the docstring, add the odd-chunk pin (a 44-byte header hand-built with an odd data size). **Decision (leg 2, 2026-09-04): Applied** - the pin is the mechanical proof of the public-API claim; composer verification pending.
- PR-LOW-003: materiality=docs-only; verified severity=low; peer severity=low (preserved); evidence: CONFIRMED - the `render_report` docstring (`speaker_eval.py:724-729`) still says "ASCII-only on purpose ... meant to be redirected to a file on Windows" while the function interpolates `model_name`, recording stems and role labels unchanged (`:731`, `:759-764`, `:786-797`) and the round-62 pin proves the returned string is Unicode; the corrected `WAV_FORMAT_HELP` comment (`:113-119`) already states the true contract, so this is the last sibling of round 60 LOW-002's overclaim; scope: yes; production-impacting: no; recommendation: Fix-now - reword to "literals ASCII, dynamic fields Unicode, `main`'s `_configure_output` owns stream safety - not this string"; no behaviour change. **Decision (leg 2, 2026-09-04): Applied** - composer verification pending.

### Round 64 - 2026-09-04 - Phase 2 Task 2.3a, post-round-63-fix independent cross-family codex peer review (confirmation)

- Round status: Closed (2 of 2 Applied 2026-09-04; composer-verified green - pytest 1359, ruff + mypy clean; round 65 CONFIRMED both fixes on their named cases and continued the residues PR-MED-007 -> PR-LOW-009, PR-MED-008 -> PR-LOW-010)

#### PR-MED-005 fix confirmation — NOT CONFIRMED

The clinician-cluster half is fixed correctly. `role_outcome` sorts clinician-majority clusters only by clinician seconds at `speaker_eval.py:521-525`, then explicitly returns no cluster when the top two are within `_SECONDS_EPSILON` at `:526-533`. Consequently, cluster spelling cannot select a different `clinician_cluster_talk_time_share`. The exact-tie and clear-lead pin at `test_speaker_eval.py:682-693` matches that contract.

The mapping now uses count as a secondary objective, but the class-wide label-order invariant is still incomplete. `cluster_metrics` derives permutation order from lexically sorted labels at `speaker_eval.py:414-415`, enumerates those permutations at `:432`, and compares each candidate only with the current winner using `tied = abs(seconds - best_seconds) <= _SECONDS_EPSILON` at `:436-438`. Because epsilon-nearness is not transitive, a chain of three mappings can walk the winner downward by count in one enumeration order but not another, changing `correct_count`, `accuracy_count`, and `accuracy_seconds` at `:439-451`. See PR-MED-007.

#### PR-MED-006 fix confirmation — NOT CONFIRMED

The named key-unlink instance is fixed: `delete_session_key` is guarded at `speaker_eval.py:691-695`, `crypto.destroy()` is in that guard’s `finally` at `:696-697`, ordinary directory-removal failures are suppressed so both removal calls normally execute at `:698-699`, and a visible residue produces a module-owned, path-bearing `SpeakerEvalError` at `:700-712`. Cleanup is invoked on both the failure and success paths at `:744-747`; when it raises while handling a provider failure, normal exception chaining preserves the provider exception as context. The existing pins at `test_speaker_eval.py:911-942` and `:1020-1043` correctly cover a refused unlink, no-op removals, residue reporting, and provider-context preservation.

The full independent-leg/every-path class is not closed. `crypto.destroy()`, both `rmtree` calls, and the residue probe are still sequential, so an exception from one skips everything after it. The crypto object also exists before `mkdtemp`, while the cleanup-protected `try` does not begin until after `mkdtemp` and `secrets.token_hex` at `speaker_eval.py:723-727`; those two failures bypass destruction/cleanup entirely. Finally, `if not temp_root.exists(): return` is not a fail-closed residue check: CPython 3.14 implements it through `os.path.exists`, which returns `False` on `OSError` (`C:\Python314\Lib\genericpath.py:16-21`), so an inaccessible surviving root can be mistaken for an absent one. See PR-MED-008.

#### PR-LOW-002 fix confirmation — CONFIRMED

`speaker_eval.py:262-282` now gets the declared frame count, refuses zero frames, calls `reader.readframes(frames + 1)`, and requires exactly `frames * BYTES_PER_SAMPLE` bytes. A longer read is classified as a partial trailing sample at `:271-275`; a shorter read is classified as truncated at `:276-281`; both messages carry the re-export recipe. The handcrafted odd-data-chunk pin and its even-payload control are present at `test_speaker_eval.py:423-435`. The fix is correct and complete for this finding.

#### PR-LOW-003 fix confirmation — CONFIRMED

The `render_report` docstring at `speaker_eval.py:790-797` now states that literals are ASCII, dynamic fields preserve their input Unicode, and stream safety belongs to `main`’s `_configure_output`, not the returned string. `_configure_output` is implemented at `:913-923` and is the first statement of `main` at `:929`. The documentation now matches the enforcing structure.

#### PR-MED-007: `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/speaker_eval.py:424` — the epsilon mapping comparator remains permutation-order dependent

- Triage: Fix-now
- Fix route: fix-on-fast
- Why it matters: With three or more labels—the exact D-S1 case this helper is designed to measure—renaming labels can still change the reported unweighted accuracy substantially. The round-63 fix removed the two-mapping example but did not establish an order-independent objective.
- Current behaviour: Labels are sorted at `:414-415`, their permutations are visited in that lexical order at `:432`, and each candidate is compared only with the current winner at `:436-438`. An epsilon-tied candidate with a higher count replaces the winner and also replaces `best_seconds`; later comparisons therefore use a shifted epsilon anchor. Epsilon-nearness is non-transitive, so the final winner can depend on traversal order.
- Desired behaviour: Compute the mapping objective globally and independently of enumeration order: establish the maximum seconds first, retain every mapping within `_SECONDS_EPSILON` of that fixed maximum, then maximise correct count. Among equal-count finalists, select a numerically deterministic seconds value/mapping so every remaining traversal tie has identical reported seconds and count.
- Pattern to follow: Separate candidate enumeration from winner selection; never mutate the tolerance reference while traversing candidates.
- Pattern siblings: `role_outcome` at `:521-533` is already safe because it sorts by the exact numeric value once and compares the top two without moving the reference. Presentation sorting at `:455` does not affect a score.
- Invariant: Renaming predicted or true labels without changing segment assignments and durations must not change `accuracy_seconds`, `accuracy_count`, `correct_count`, or any aggregate derived from them.
- Verification: Let `e = _SECONDS_EPSILON` and use three predicted clusters and three true labels. Give the six relevant mappings seconds/count objectives `ABC=(30,100)`, `ACB=(30+0.75e,90)`, `CBA=(30+1.5e,80)`, with the other three well below 30. These objectives are realisable with positive confusion cells—for example seconds rows `p1=(10,10-10e,10+1.5e)`, `p2=(10-10e,10,10+0.75e)`, `p3=(10,10,10)` and counts rows `p1=(30,1,20)`, `p2=(1,30,30)`, `p3=(30,30,40)`. Under lexical order `A,B,C`, traversal finishes at `CBA`, reporting `80/212`; renaming the same roles so the column order is `C,B,A` visits the chain in reverse and walks through `CBA -> ACB -> ABC`, reporting `100/212`. Lines `:436-438` permit each step.
- Fix recommendation: Materialise `(seconds, count, mapping)` candidates, anchor the epsilon band to the global maximum seconds, select the maximum count within that band, then use exact seconds as a traversal-independent tertiary numeric selector. Add the three-cluster matrix above under two reversed label orderings and assert every reported numeric field is identical.
- Regression risk: Genuinely near-tied three-or-more-speaker mappings may change. Unique duration winners and the existing two-mapping count tie must remain unchanged.
- /fix decision: Applied
- /fix notes: `cluster_metrics` now materialises every (seconds, count, pairs) candidate, takes S* = max seconds, the band = candidates within `_SECONDS_EPSILON` of that FIXED S*, K* = max count in the band, s* = max exact seconds among the count winners, and among numerically identical finalists shows the smallest pairs tuple (presentation only); the moving-anchor loop and its comment are gone. Pin: the peer's 13-cell / 212-segment matrix built under both label orderings asserts `correct_count` 90, `accuracy_count` 90/212, and bit-identical `accuracy_seconds`, `labelled_seconds` and `labelled_count` across the two runs (the pre-fix code gave 80 one way and 100 the other); the two-mapping count-tie pin and every unique-winner pin are unchanged. Invariant re-read: every reported number is a function of the candidate SET (max, band, max, max), none of the traversal. Pending composer verification.
- /fix date: 2026-09-04
- /fix applied by: Claude Code

#### PR-MED-008: `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/speaker_eval.py:680` — temporary-store teardown is not independently guaranteed across every cleanup leg

- Triage: Fix-now
- Fix route: fix-on-fast
- Why it matters: A cleanup fault after encrypted audio and its DPAPI-wrapped key exist can skip later deletion attempts or make an inaccessible surviving root appear absent. That defeats the module’s “every path, residue named” custody promise and can leave a recoverable temporary copy without the required path-bearing diagnostic.
- Current behaviour: `_destroy_temporary_store` protects `crypto.destroy()` only from an `OSError` raised by key unlink at `:691-697`. A raise from `crypto.destroy()` skips both removals and the residue check; a raise from the first removal skips the second and the probe; a raise from the second skips the probe. `shutil.rmtree(ignore_errors=True)` suppresses ordinary removal errors, but its call can still propagate an audit-hook or non-filesystem exception before/around the implementation (`C:\Python314\Lib\shutil.py:831-852`). The final `temp_root.exists()` at `speaker_eval.py:700` converts any stat `OSError` into `False`, permitting a hidden residue. Separately, failures at `mkdtemp` or `secrets.token_hex` occur before the protected `try` at `:723-727`; the former leaves the fresh in-memory key undestroyed, and the latter additionally leaves an empty temporary root.
- Desired behaviour: Every cleanup action must be attempted regardless of every earlier cleanup failure, and the final residue determination must fail closed. Any leftover, indeterminate probe, or cleanup failure that can leave custody uncertain must raise a module-owned `SpeakerEvalError` naming the relevant path and preserve the original provider/harness exception as context.
- Pattern to follow: One outer lifecycle guard beginning immediately after `SessionCrypto()` construction, plus nested `try/finally` or a failure accumulator for key unlink, key destruction, session-directory removal, temp-root removal, and a final fail-closed residue probe.
- Pattern siblings: The failure handler at `:733-746` correctly clears provider traceback frames before cleanup and preserves an original exception when cleanup itself raises. The missing guarantee is inside lifecycle setup and teardown, not provider handling.
- Invariant: Once `SessionCrypto` exists, its destruction is attempted on every exit; once a temporary root exists, both directory-removal legs and a conclusive residue check run on every exit. No path may silently retain both ciphertext and usable key custody.
- Verification: A fault injected at `crypto.destroy()` at `:697` bypasses `:698-712`; a fault injected into the first `rmtree` at `:698` bypasses `:699-712`; a fault at the second bypasses `:700-712`. If key unlink first records `PermissionError`, both removals fail, and `os.stat(temp_root)` is denied, `Path.exists()` returns `False`, so line `:700` returns even though `key.dpapi` and `audio.enc` may remain together. Existing tests at `test_speaker_eval.py:911-942` replace `rmtree` with a non-raising no-op and therefore do not exercise these paths.
- Fix recommendation: Start the guarded lifecycle immediately after constructing `SessionCrypto`, allow `temp_root`/`session_dir` to remain unset during early failures, attempt every cleanup leg while recording failures, and perform a residue probe that distinguishes absent from inaccessible instead of using `Path.exists()`. Add pins for raising `mkdtemp`, `token_hex`, `crypto.destroy`, each removal call independently, and an inaccessible residue probe on both success and provider-failure paths; assert later legs were attempted, the error is `SpeakerEvalError` with the path/state, and the original exception remains its context.
- Regression risk: Cleanup failures that currently escape as raw exception types—or are hidden by an indeterminate `exists()` result—will become explicit `SpeakerEvalError`s. Normal success, ordinary provider failure, and successful deletion must retain their current contracts.
- /fix decision: Applied
- /fix notes: Teardown restructured. `_attempt_all` runs the legs through a `finally` chain (a leg's `Exception` is recorded by type name; a `BaseException` propagates only after the remaining legs ran). `_probe` is a fail-closed existence probe over `os.stat` (`gone` only on `FileNotFoundError`, `present` on success, `unreadable` on any other `OSError`). `_raise_if_residue` returns only when the root is positively gone AND `crypto.destroyed`; otherwise it raises a `SpeakerEvalError` naming the path, the key-blob state (`present` / `unreadable` => recoverable, `gone` => ciphertext), an undestroyed in-memory key, and every leg failure. `_destroy_temporary_store` builds the four legs (key unlink, `crypto.destroy`, session-dir removal, temp-root removal via `functools.partial`, a never-created path skipped) and probes in its own `finally`, so the fault chains to whatever exception was in flight. `_transcribe_in_temporary_store`'s guard now begins immediately after `SessionCrypto()`, with `temp_root` / `session_dir` None until they exist. Module, teardown and lifecycle docstrings rewritten to the leg-1 tuple's wording - claim: every leg attempted, key destroyed on every exit, probe fails closed, residue named and chained; residue: cannot delete or inspect what the OS refuses. Pins, CI-safe: the probe's three states; a raising `crypto.destroy` still removes the store and is reported; each removal leg raising independently (session-dir: the root leg still clears everything, no fault; temp-root: the fault names the leg and the path); an unreadable root is a fault saying "treat the store as recoverable"; a raising `mkdtemp` still destroys the key with no root created; a raising `token_hex` still removes the root; the existing refused-unlink / no-op-removal pins updated to the new failure wording. Windows pins: an unreadable probe is a fault on both the success path (`__context__` None) and the provider-failure path (`__context__` the provider's `RuntimeError`), with the store really removed. The audit-hook point is covered by the chain at zero cost. Pending composer verification.
- /fix date: 2026-09-04
- /fix applied by: Claude Code

#### LEG 1 verified tuples (executor `claude-fable-5-1`, 2026-09-04)
- PR-MED-007: materiality=behavioral; verified severity=med; peer severity=med (preserved); evidence: CONFIRMED by reproducing the peer's matrix against `speaker_eval.py:414-415` (labels sorted), `:432` (permutations in that order) and `:436-438` (each candidate compared only with the CURRENT winner, whose seconds become the new epsilon anchor). With e = `_SECONDS_EPSILON`, the six mappings score ABC=(30 s, 100), ACB=(30+0.75e, 90), CBA=(30+1.5e, 80), the other three at least 8.5e below 30 (labelled count 212). Order A,B,C visits ABC, ACB, BAC, BCA, CAB, CBA: ABC wins, ACB ties within e but 90 < 100 (kept), CBA is 1.5e above 30 - not tied - and strictly greater, so it wins: 80/212. Renamed so the sort order is C,B,A the traversal is reversed: CBA wins, then ACB ties within 0.75e with 90 > 80 (anchor moves to 30+0.75e), then ABC ties within 0.75e of THAT with 100 > 90: 100/212. Same segments, same durations, different `correct_count` and `accuracy_count` - the invariant is broken and the anchor-walk is the mechanism. Reachability is bounded: three mappings must sit within ~1.5e of each other yet not all within e, i.e. total durations differing at the sub-nanosecond scale, which sample-resolution Audacity labels cannot produce (float rounding of sums is ~1e-13 s, all mutually within e, where nearness IS transitive) - but the class is not closed by construction and the fix is small. (a) The peer's global-anchor objective CLOSES the class by construction: materialise every (seconds, count, pairs) candidate as a SET; S* = max seconds over the set; the band = every candidate with seconds >= S* - e (anchored to the FIXED S*, so it is a fixed set); K* = max count within the band; finalists = band members with count K*; s* = max exact seconds among finalists. Each step is a function of the set, never of its traversal, so `accuracy_seconds` (= s* / labelled), `correct_count` (= K*) and `accuracy_count` are provably order-independent; on the peer's matrix the band is {ACB, CBA} (ABC at 30 is 1.5e below S*, outside e) and the answer is 90/212 whichever way the labels sort. Residue, stated not hidden: among finalists with identical s* and K* the label PAIRING can still differ, so the displayed mapping is chosen deterministically by its pairs tuple - a lexical choice that touches no number; and the band edge is a tolerance boundary like any other, itself order-free; scope: yes; production-impacting: no; recommendation: Fix-now - implement exactly that objective (candidate list, fixed anchor, count, exact seconds, then pairs), replace the moving-anchor loop and its comment, pin the three-cluster matrix under both label orderings asserting every reported numeric field identical, keep the two-mapping count-tie pin and the unique-winner pins unchanged. **Decision (leg 2, 2026-09-04): Applied** - composer verification pending.
- PR-MED-008: materiality=behavioral; verified severity=med; peer severity=med (preserved); evidence: CONFIRMED - (1) `speaker_eval.py:696-699`: `crypto.destroy()` sits in the unlink guard's `finally`, but the two `rmtree` calls and the probe follow it sequentially, so anything raised by `destroy()` (no I/O - a bytearray overwrite - so unrealistic) or by either `rmtree` skips every later leg; `rmtree(ignore_errors=True)` routes filesystem errors through `onexc` and suppresses them, so the only thing that can escape it is a `sys.audit("shutil.rmtree", ...)` hook raising, or a `BaseException` - REAL BUT IMMATERIAL: this process installs no audit hook (nothing in the codebase calls `sys.addaudithook`), only the same user could, and the finally-chain below covers it at zero cost. (2) `:700` `temp_root.exists()`: on the installed CPython 3.14 `Path.exists()` delegates to `os.path.exists`, which returns False on ANY `OSError` (the peer's `genericpath.py:16-21`), so a root the OS refuses to `stat` reads as ABSENT and the residue fault is skipped - a genuine fail-OPEN probe, the one substantive defect in this class (rare: it needs the OS to refuse `stat` on the user's own just-created temp root, and the exposure is the same-user, plaintext-already-on-disk bound stated in round 63). (3) `:723-727`: the guard starts after `mkdtemp` and `secrets.token_hex`; a failure there leaves an undestroyed in-memory key (process-lifetime, no file) or an empty root (no data) - immaterial, trivially closed. (b) What user-space CAN enforce: every leg ATTEMPTED whatever the earlier legs did, for every exception type (a nested `try/finally` chain, since `finally` runs on any exception); the in-memory key destroyed on every exit after `SessionCrypto()` (guard begins immediately after construction; `temp_root`/`session_dir` may be unset on early exits); leg failures accumulated by exception type name; a probe that never says "gone" without positive evidence - `os.stat(temp_root)` directly: `FileNotFoundError` => gone, success => survives => fault, any other `OSError` => indeterminate => fault ("could not confirm removal"); the fault module-own text naming the path, the key state (`os.stat` on `key.dpapi` the same fail-closed way) and every accumulated leg failure; the original exception preserved as `__context__` by implicit chaining, including when the fault is raised inside the chain's last `finally` while another exception is in flight. What NO user-space code can enforce: that the OS actually deletes - or lets us inspect - a file in the user's own temp directory; a refused unlink, rmdir or stat leaves a residue that the harness can only NAME for the practitioner to remove by hand, and nothing holds after the process exits. Docstring wording the structure will support, no stronger: "Every leg is attempted whatever the earlier legs did, the in-memory key is destroyed on every exit, and the final probe fails closed - only a positive `FileNotFoundError` counts as gone; a surviving or unreadable root raises a `SpeakerEvalError` naming the path, the key state and every leg failure, chained to whatever exception was already in flight. What this cannot do is delete or inspect what the OS refuses: that residue is named for the practitioner to remove by hand, never hidden."; scope: yes; production-impacting: no; recommendation: Fix-now - restructure `_destroy_temporary_store` as that chain with the accumulator and the `os.stat` probe, start `_transcribe_in_temporary_store`'s guard right after `SessionCrypto()`, rewrite the module and function docstrings to the wording above, and add the peer's pins (raising `mkdtemp`, raising `token_hex`, raising `crypto.destroy`, each `rmtree` raising independently, an inaccessible probe on both the success and the provider-failure path), each asserting the later legs ran, the fault is a path-bearing `SpeakerEvalError`, and the original exception is its context. **Decision (leg 2, 2026-09-04): Applied** - composer verification pending.

#### PR-MED-007 fix confirmation — NOT CONFIRMED

The round-64 three-cluster matrix itself is fixed correctly. `cluster_metrics` materialises every candidate at `speaker_eval.py:437-444`, establishes the global maximum at `:445`, forms a band against that fixed maximum at `:446`, then maximises count and exact seconds at `:447-452`. For the peer’s objectives, the fixed band contains `ACB=(30+0.75e,90)` and `CBA=(30+1.5e,80)`, excludes `ABC=(30,100)`, and therefore selects 90/212 under either true-label ordering. The matrix construction at `test_speaker_eval.py:599-620` and exact assertions at `:621-626` confirm identical `accuracy_seconds`, `accuracy_count`, `correct_count`, `labelled_seconds`, and `labelled_count`. The corresponding confusion-cell values and per-cluster purities are also numerically identical for that specific matrix after applying the label-renaming bijection; only presentation labels/order change.

The broader spelling/traversal-order class is not closed by construction. `predicted_labels = sorted(...)` at `speaker_eval.py:416` controls the order of the operands passed to ordinary floating-point `sum(...)` at `:442`. Renaming predicted clusters can therefore reverse the addition order and change `best_seconds`. Purity has the same remaining channel: true-label spelling determines `cells` order at `:456-457`, and ordinary `sum(cells)` reaches the reported purity at `:458`. The round-64 pin reverses true-label names only, keeps predicted labels `p1/p2/p3` fixed, and does not compare purity or confusion cells. See PR-LOW-009.

#### PR-MED-008 fix confirmation — NOT CONFIRMED

The custody-critical core is substantially correct:

- Refusals happen before store construction at `speaker_eval.py:856-858`.
- The lifecycle guard starts immediately after `SessionCrypto()` construction at `:815-818`.
- `mkdtemp` and `token_hex` failures pass through teardown because they execute inside that guard at `:819-820`.
- Provider exceptions clear suspended decrypt-stream frames at `:825-837`, then reach teardown at `:838-840`.
- Success tears down before returning the document at `:841-842`; consequently, later harness faults at `:860-872` cannot retain the temporary store.
- `_attempt_all` reaches every cleanup leg through its recursive `finally` at `:694-709`. A raising key unlink, `crypto.destroy()`, or either removal therefore cannot skip later legs.
- `_probe` treats only `FileNotFoundError` as gone and turns any other `OSError` into `unreadable` at `:712-723`.
- A surviving or unreadable root raises the module-owned, path-bearing `SpeakerEvalError` at `:726-761`; when cleanup runs during another failure, normal implicit chaining preserves that original exception as context.

However, the requested failure contract and documentation are incomplete. `_attempt_all` records cleanup failures at `:706-707`, but `_raise_if_residue` returns whenever the root is gone and `crypto.destroyed` is true at `:732-734`, without checking `failures`. Thus a raising key unlink or first removal is silently discarded if a later root removal succeeds. The tests expressly pin that behavior at `test_speaker_eval.py:1008-1012` and `:1070-1078`, rather than requiring a module-owned cleanup fault.

A raising `crypto.destroy()` does produce `SpeakerEvalError` because `crypto.destroyed` remains false, but when removal succeeds the message does not name the temporary path: the path-bearing branch runs only when `root_state != "gone"` at `speaker_eval.py:736-756`; the undestroyed-key branch at `:757-758` has no path. This contradicts the requested “any cleanup failure raises and names the path” contract.

The docstring also outruns the structure. It says “the in-memory key is destroyed on every exit” at `speaker_eval.py:29-30` and again at `:770-771`, while the injected raising-destroy path at `test_speaker_eval.py:945-998` proves destruction can only be attempted and then reported as failed. The external residue—an OS that refuses deletion or inspection—is accurately named at `speaker_eval.py:36-38` and `:775-778`, but the unconditional key-destruction claim is not an exact description of the code. See PR-LOW-010.

#### Post-Fix Regression Check

- Round 64 changes no public function signature, dataclass shape, or return type. `cluster_metrics`, `evaluate_recording`, `render_report`, and `main` retain their contracts.
- The intentional mapping change is confined to epsilon-near competing mappings. Unique winners and the prior two-mapping count tie remain structurally intact.
- `main` still returns 0 only for at least one result with no errors and 1 otherwise at `speaker_eval.py:1020-1086`. `SpeakerEvalError` remains a per-recording caught error at `:1070-1072`; refusal behavior remains `[skip]`.
- The launcher remains a thin `main` → `sys.exit` delegation at `scripts/measure-speakers.py:23-26`.
- The end-to-end fixture still checks the shipped pipeline labels, pre-2.1 labels, result shape, and successful store removal at `test_speaker_eval.py:1132-1169`; provider-failure removal remains covered at `:1171-1177`.

#### PR-LOW-009: `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/speaker_eval.py:416` — floating-point aggregation still lets label spelling reach numeric metrics

- Triage: Fix-now
- Fix route: fix-on-fast
- Why it matters: Round 64 made winner selection a global set objective, but the candidate scalars themselves are still calculated in spelling-derived operand order. A label rename can therefore change raw `accuracy_seconds` or purity by an ulp and, at an epsilon-band boundary, can theoretically change which count objective qualifies.
- Current behaviour: `predicted_labels` and `true_labels` are lexically sorted at `speaker_eval.py:416-417`. Candidate seconds use ordinary `sum(...)` over pairs in predicted-label order at `:438-444`; purity uses ordinary `sum(cells)` in true-label order at `:455-458`. Floating-point addition is not associative.
- Desired behaviour: Every numeric metric must be computed from a canonical numeric aggregation independent of predicted-label spelling, true-label spelling, permutation enumeration, or dictionary order.
- Pattern to follow: Aggregate finite positive durations through a label-independent numeric order using `math.fsum`, preferably over numerically sorted values. Apply the same rule to candidate seconds, labelled totals, purity denominators, and—if full segment-traversal independence is claimed—per-cell duration accumulation.
- Pattern siblings: `labelled_seconds = sum(confusion.values())` at `:418` and per-cell accumulation at `:411` should be included in the same audit. Count fields use integer addition and are unaffected.
- Invariant: A bijective renaming of predicted or true labels must not change any numeric `ClusterMetrics` field after aligning cells through that bijection.
- Verification: Three uniquely and perfectly mapped cells of 746.67, 411.27, and 541.32 seconds sum to `1699.2600000000002` in that order but `1699.26` in reverse IEEE-754 order. Keep the input segment order fixed and rename predicted clusters so lexical sorting reverses their order: `labelled_seconds` retains its insertion order, while line 442 reverses the winning mapping’s operands, making raw `accuracy_seconds` differ despite identical assignments. With all three truth cells in one predicted cluster, renaming true labels similarly changes the purity denominator’s addition order at line 458.
- Fix recommendation: Use a single canonical finite-duration summation helper, backed by `math.fsum` over numeric-order values, throughout `cluster_metrics`. Extend the round-64 pin to reverse both predicted and true spellings and compare all numeric fields, including purity and semantically aligned confusion cells; add the non-associative-duration fixture above.
- Regression risk: Raw values may move by a few ulps. The Markdown table’s three-decimal values and all ordinary recordings should remain unchanged.
- /fix decision: Applied
- /fix notes: `_total(values) -> float` returning `math.fsum(values)`, its docstring attributing the order-independence to fsum's exact accumulation and single rounding (no sort). `cluster_metrics`: per-cell durations collected into lists and totalled once (`confusion = {key: _total(parts)}`), candidate seconds, `labelled_seconds` and every purity denominator through `_total`; `align_segments` (the sibling): per-label overlaps collected and totalled once, the share denominator through `_total`. Pins: the round-64 matrix now runs under two namings that reverse BOTH the predicted clusters and the true labels and asserts every numeric field, every purity and every bijection-aligned confusion cell identical; the peer's 746.67 / 411.27 / 541.32 fixture asserts `accuracy_seconds` exactly 1.0 under both namings and `labelled_seconds == math.fsum(durations)`. Invariant re-read: every float in `ClusterMetrics` and every share in `align_segments` is a `_total` over a collected multiset, or a max / quotient of such totals; no plain `sum` over floats remains in the module (counts stay integer sums). Pending composer verification.
- /fix date: 2026-09-04
- /fix applied by: Claude Code

#### PR-LOW-010: `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/speaker_eval.py:732` — recovered cleanup failures are silent and the teardown docstring promises more than the structure enforces

- Triage: Fix-now
- Fix route: fix-on-fast
- Why it matters: The requested teardown contract says every cleanup failure becomes a module-owned, path-bearing fault. Instead, some recorded failures disappear after successful fallback removal, and the security-facing docstring promises unconditional key destruction even though the fault-injection contract admits destruction can raise.
- Current behaviour: `_attempt_all` records exceptions in `failures` at `speaker_eval.py:703-709`, but `_raise_if_residue` returns at `:732-734` whenever the root is gone and the key reports destroyed, ignoring those failures. The session-directory and key-unlink tests intentionally expect no exception after fallback root removal at `test_speaker_eval.py:1008-1012` and `:1070-1078`. When `crypto.destroy()` raises and the root is removed, the error built at `speaker_eval.py:757-761` omits `temp_root`. Meanwhile, the module and teardown docstrings claim the key “is destroyed on every exit” at `:29-30` and `:770-771`.
- Desired behaviour: Every recorded cleanup-leg failure raises `SpeakerEvalError`, even when a later leg conclusively recovers the final state; the message names the temporary path or explicitly says no root was created. Documentation says destruction is attempted on every exit and a failed attempt is surfaced, rather than claiming success unconditionally.
- Pattern to follow: Treat cleanup as successful only when the root is positively gone, the key is destroyed, and `failures` is empty. Keep the current context-preserving raise from teardown.
- Pattern siblings: The raising-destroy test at `test_speaker_eval.py:989-998` should require the path. Both branches of `test_each_removal_leg_raising_independently` at `:1001-1020` and the refused-unlink test at `:1070-1078` should require a module-owned error while still asserting all later legs ran and the final store is gone.
- Invariant: Every cleanup leg is attempted; every cleanup-leg exception is surfaced safely with path/state; only a completely successful teardown returns normally.
- Verification: With the session-directory removal targeted, `_attempt_all` appends `session directory removal: RuntimeError`; the root removal succeeds and `crypto.destroyed` is true, so line 733 returns and drops the recorded failure. With `_StubbornCrypto`, the root is gone but the key remains live, so lines 757-761 raise without ever adding the path-bearing text guarded by line 736. Both behaviors are pinned by the cited tests.
- Fix recommendation: Include `not failures` in the normal-return condition, always include `temp_root` or an explicit “root not created” state in cleanup faults, and replace “the key is destroyed on every exit” with “key destruction is attempted on every exit; failure is reported and the key then dies with the process.”
- Regression risk: A recovered key-unlink or session-directory-removal exception will now mark that recording failed and make `main` exit 1, although the final store is gone. That is the requested fail-closed diagnostic behavior.
- /fix decision: Applied
- /fix notes: `_raise_if_residue` returns normally only when the root is positively gone AND `crypto.destroyed` AND `failures` is empty; with the root gone a fault says either "no temporary root was created" or "temporary store removed (<root>) but a teardown leg failed - nothing to remove by hand; rerun this recording", so every fault names the root or states that none existed; the undestroyed-key and leg-failure lines follow as before. Module docstring, `_destroy_temporary_store` and `_raise_if_residue` docstrings changed to the leg-1 wording (destruction ATTEMPTED on every exit, a failed attempt surfaced; normal return only on gone AND destroyed AND no failure; every failed leg surfaces even when a later leg recovered the state; residue: cannot delete or inspect what the OS refuses). Pins: the refused-unlink test and the session-directory branch of the each-leg test flipped to require the module-owned fault while still asserting the later legs ran, the store is gone and the fault says nothing is left to remove; the raising-destroy pin now requires the path; new pin: a fault before any root exists says "no temporary root was created". Cost accepted and stated: a recovered anomaly still discards that recording's result and exits 1 - fail toward reporting. Pending composer verification.
- /fix date: 2026-09-04
- /fix applied by: Claude Code

Per-cell durations are collected and totalled once at `:422-432`; `labelled_seconds` uses `_total` at `:436`; candidate seconds use it at `:460`; and purity denominators use it at `:473-476`. `accuracy_seconds` is a quotient of canonical totals, while count accuracy and both speaker-count fields derive only from exact integer counts/cardinalities at `:437`, `:461`, and `:478-490`. Lexical sorting remains only for candidate enumeration and presentation; the global set objective at `:463-470` makes every winning numeric value independent of that order. No plain float `sum(...)`, spelling-ordered float aggregation, or dictionary-order-dependent float accumulation reaches a `ClusterMetrics` number.

The non-associative 746.67 / 411.27 / 541.32 fixture is pinned at `test_speaker_eval.py:644-656`. The round-64 matrix now reverses both predicted and true label spellings and compares accuracy, labelled totals/counts, purity, and every bijection-aligned confusion cell at `:599-642`.

#### PR-LOW-010 fix confirmation — NOT CONFIRMED

The named round-65 changes are present:

- `_raise_if_residue` returns only for `root_state == "gone"`, `crypto.destroyed`, and an empty failure list at `speaker_eval.py:744-756`.
- Every fault it constructs names `temp_root` or explicitly states that no temporary root was created at `:757-790`.
- The formerly silence-pinning session-directory and key-unlink tests now require `SpeakerEvalError`, assert later legs ran, assert the root is gone, and require the recovered-state explanation at `test_speaker_eval.py:1031-1055` and `:1105-1120`.

The claim/structure fit is still incomplete. Both real removal legs call `shutil.rmtree(..., ignore_errors=True)` at `speaker_eval.py:817-828`. CPython explicitly converts `ignore_errors=True` into a no-op error callback (`C:\Python314\Lib\shutil.py:818-825`, `:832-834`), so ordinary filesystem removal errors never reach `_attempt_all` and never enter `failures`. A failed session-directory removal followed by successful root removal therefore satisfies the normal-return condition and silently drops the failed leg, contrary to the docstrings’ “every leg failure” claim. The tests replace `rmtree` with a stub that raises despite receiving `ignore_errors=True`, so they do not exercise the production call contract. See PR-LOW-012.

#### PR-LOW-012: `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/speaker_eval.py:817` — `ignore_errors=True` bypasses the cleanup-failure accumulator

- Triage: Fix-now
- Fix route: fix-on-fast
- Why it matters: The teardown claims every failed cleanup leg is surfaced even after a later leg recovers the final state. Ordinary filesystem errors inside both real `rmtree` calls are instead suppressed before `_attempt_all` can record them. This hides a custody-path anomaly and makes the documentation stronger than the implementation, although a surviving recoverable store is still caught by the final probe.
- Current behaviour: Both removal callables are constructed with `ignore_errors=True` at `speaker_eval.py:817-828`. CPython’s implementation discards errors under that flag. If session-directory removal encounters an OS error but temporary-root removal subsequently succeeds, the root is gone, the key is destroyed, `failures` remains empty, and `_raise_if_residue` returns at `:754-756`.
- Desired behaviour: Removal errors propagate into `_attempt_all`, which records their safe type names while its `finally` chain still guarantees every later leg. A recovered error must produce the path-bearing module fault promised by the docstrings.
- Pattern to follow: Let each `shutil.rmtree` use its normal raising contract and rely on `_attempt_all`—not `ignore_errors`—for continuation.
- Pattern siblings: The unconditional “destroyed key-first” wording at `scripts/measure-speakers.py:16` and `scripts/README.md:12` should be reconciled to the attempt-and-surface contract while this claim is touched.
- Invariant: Teardown returns normally only after a positively absent root, destroyed in-memory key, and zero actual cleanup-leg failures; every observed failure is surfaced safely even if a later leg removes the root.
- Verification: `C:\Python314\Lib\shutil.py:818-825` documents error suppression, and `:832-834` implements it with a callback that does nothing. The existing `_RaiseOn` fixture at `test_speaker_eval.py:959-972` does not model that behavior: it raises directly regardless of the passed `ignore_errors` argument.
- Fix recommendation: Remove `ignore_errors=True` from both partials, retain `_attempt_all` as the continuation boundary, and add a pin whose `rmtree` stand-in honors the supplied flag so the pre-fix call returns silently and the fixed call records and surfaces the recovered leg failure.
- Regression risk: Transient removal errors that are later recovered will newly fail that recording and make `main` exit 1. This matches the round-65 fail-closed diagnostic contract.
- /fix decision: Applied
- /fix notes: `_remove_tree(path)` calls `shutil.rmtree(path)` under its normal raising contract - no `ignore_errors` - treating `FileNotFoundError`, and only that, as "nothing there" (the probe's own positive signal; it also covers the never-created `session_dir`, assigned before `mkdir`); both removal partials switched to it. Module, teardown and lifecycle docstrings, `scripts/measure-speakers.py` and `scripts/README.md` reworded to the leg-1 wording: every leg failure the OS reports is surfaced even when a later leg recovered the state; a path that is already gone is not a failure; a removal that fails stops at its first error and leaves the rest to the later legs and the probe; an interrupt inside a leg propagates only after the remaining legs ran, chained to any residue fault; the OS-refusal residue named. Pins: `_HonourFlag`, a `rmtree` stand-in that honours the flag the way CPython does (silent under `ignore_errors=True`, `PermissionError` otherwise), proves the leg passes no flag and that the recovered session-directory failure is recorded and surfaced with the path and "nothing to remove by hand"; a never-created `session_dir` under an existing root tears down normally with the root removed. The existing `_RaiseOn` / `_keep` pins keep their meaning. Pending composer verification.
- /fix date: 2026-09-04
- /fix applied by: Claude Code

LOW because residue is still caught: the probe, not the leg, decides whether anything survived, so this silences an anomaly rather than opening a custody hole. On the composer's question - does dropping the flag make the claim EXACTLY true? Yes, with one benign case that must be handled and named, plus one behaviour to state: (i) with the default (`onexc=None`, `onerror=None`) CPython installs an `onexc` that re-raises, so every error `rmtree` meets propagates as the original `OSError` and nothing is swallowed; a raising audit hook or any non-`OSError` propagates likewise; `_attempt_all` records every `Exception` by type and lets a `BaseException` through only after the remaining legs ran - so every failure a removal leg OBSERVES is surfaced. (ii) The benign case: `session_dir` is assigned at `:853` BEFORE `mkdir` at `:854`, so if `mkdir` - or anything before the directory exists - raises, the session-directory leg would call `rmtree` on a path that never existed and, without the flag, get `FileNotFoundError`: a false "leg failure" that would turn every early setup failure into a misleading teardown fault. The exact structure is a small `_remove_tree(path)` wrapper that calls `shutil.rmtree(path)` and treats `FileNotFoundError` - and only that - as "nothing there", the same positive signal the probe trusts; everything else propagates to `_attempt_all`. A mid-tree `FileNotFoundError` (something else removed an entry) is benign for the LEG on the same reasoning, because the probe is the backstop for whatever survived. (iii) To state: `rmtree` without the flag stops at its FIRST error and leaves the rest of the tree in place - which is fine, because the failure is recorded, the root leg still runs, and the probe checks what remains; the docstring should say so. With (i)-(iii) the claim reads, exactly: "every leg failure the OS reports is surfaced, even when a later leg recovered the state; a path that is already gone is not a failure; a removal that fails stops at its first error and leaves the rest to the later legs and the probe" - and the residue outside user-space control is unchanged (the OS refusing to delete or inspect; an interrupt inside a leg propagating after the remaining legs, chained to any residue fault); scope: yes (the two doc siblings `scripts/measure-speakers.py:16` and `scripts/README.md:12`, "destroyed key-first", are Task 2.3a deliverables and get the attempt-and-surface wording); production-impacting: no; recommendation: Fix-now - drop `ignore_errors=True` from both partials in favour of `_remove_tree` as above, docstrings (module, teardown, the two scripts) to the wording in (iii), and two pins: a `rmtree` stand-in that HONOURS the flag (returns silently under `ignore_errors=True`, raises `PermissionError` otherwise) proving the recovered session-directory failure is now recorded and surfaced with the path and "nothing to remove by hand"; and a never-created `session_dir` under an existing root tearing down normally with the root removed. **Decision (leg 2, 2026-09-04): Applied** - composer verification pending.

#### PR-LOW-012 fix confirmation — CONFIRMED

Neither removal leg suppresses errors. `_remove_tree` calls `shutil.rmtree(path)` with no `ignore_errors` argument at `speaker_eval.py:734-745`; both the session-directory and temporary-root legs invoke that wrapper at `:835-841`.

An observed removal error therefore reaches `_attempt_all`, whose `except Exception` records the safe leg/type tuple and whose `finally` invokes all remaining legs at `speaker_eval.py:716-731`. `_raise_if_residue` returns only when the root is positively gone, the key is destroyed, and `failures` is empty (`:772-774`). A recovered failure consequently produces the path-bearing `"temporary store removed (...)"` fault at `:776-782`, with the recorded failure appended at `:806-808`.

The new stand-in accurately models the relevant flag contract: `_HonourFlag` returns silently when `ignore_errors=True` and raises `PermissionError` otherwise at `test_speaker_eval.py:977-996`. Its pin proves the production leg supplied no suppression flag (`stub.flags == [False]`), that the later root leg recovered the state, and that the path-bearing module fault still contains `session directory removal: PermissionError` at `:1152-1170`. The already-absent-directory case is separately pinned as a normal teardown at `:1172-1180`.

The documentation matches the structure:

- The module contract states that every observed failure is surfaced, already-gone paths are benign, removal stops at its first reported error, the probe handles what remains, and OS refusal to delete or inspect is the named residue at `speaker_eval.py:23-45`.
- `_remove_tree` states the precise normal-raising/`FileNotFoundError` contract at `:734-741`.
- `_destroy_temporary_store` repeats the attempt-and-surface contract and OS-refusal residue at `:811-832`.
- `scripts/measure-speakers.py:15-19` states that teardown failures and residue are surfaced by path, with OS-refused leftovers named for manual removal.
- `scripts/README.md:8-14` qualifies key-first teardown with the true failure contract: any teardown failure or residue is reported by path.

The further `rmtree` residue is stated correctly. Under the installed CPython 3.14 implementation, descendant disappearance is already treated as benign while an unsuppressed non-`FileNotFoundError` is re-raised; `_remove_tree` treats an already-gone target as “nothing there.” Any reported removal failure stops that leg, is accumulated, and leaves subsequent legs plus the fail-closed probe to resolve or report the state.

#### PR-HIGH-002

- **[HIGH]** `desktop/tests/test_ui_screens.py:2349` — Both-outcome wiring tests do not verify the clipboard action or copied payload
- Triage: Fix-now
- Fix route: fix-on-fast
- Why it matters: The new tests verify presentation state, but a regression that copies the transcript, appends transcript content to the note, or removes the click-time readiness check could still pass. This leaves a safety-critical route for unratified clinical content to become copyable without failing the tests added to pin the shipping decision.
- Current behaviour: The enabled-outcome test stops at `"assert note_screen.copy_button.isEnabled()"`, then verifies `"transcript_screen.transcript_view.textInteractionFlags() == Qt.TextInteractionFlag.NoTextInteraction"` and closes the window. It never invokes `copy_button.click()` or `_copy_note()` and never examines the clipboard. Production currently behaves safely at `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/ui/note.py:516`: `"if not self._copy_ready() or note is None: return"` followed by `"clipboard.setText(models.format_note_body(note))"`, but that contract is unpinned.
- Desired behaviour: Through the window-routed path, assert that direct and button-triggered copy attempts leave a sentinel clipboard unchanged when the flag is false and while the true path is not fully ratified. After confirm-all, acknowledge-all and Save with the flag true, click Copy and assert exact clipboard equality with `models.format_note_body(note_screen.current_note())`, excluding any transcript or appended content.
- Pattern to follow: Use the same bounded fake-seam pattern as `_fake_write_note`, replacing or isolating `QApplication.clipboard()` and restoring it deterministically.
- Pattern siblings: Both new outcome tests; `_copy_note`, `_copy_ready`, `_apply_copy_binding`, and `models.format_note_body`.
- Invariant: Only the exact fully ratified note body can enter the clipboard; transcript content remains non-copyable under both shipping outcomes.
- Verification: Static path tracing confirmed that `_on_draft_ready` reads the monkeypatched flag at call time and that the true-path test holds and releases the generation lease correctly. No existing scoped test asserts clipboard side effects or payload identity.
- Regression risk: Low implementation risk because this is test-only tightening; clipboard isolation must not leak into neighbouring Qt tests.
- /fix decision: Applied
- /fix notes: Leg 1 (composer, Claude Code `claude-fable-5-1`): CONFIRMED as a gap - no test in `desktop/tests` touches the clipboard, `_copy_note` or `format_note_body` - and DOWNGRADED HIGH -> MED: with the flag False the button is hidden AND disabled and the body non-selectable (pinned); with the flag True and the note unratified the button is disabled (pinned) and Qt delivers no click to a disabled button, so the only unpinned routes were the click-time re-check (defence in depth) and the PAYLOAD identity - a regression appending transcript text to the payload was the one case that could pass; test-only fix, no live defect. Leg 2: `_fake_clipboard` replaces `ui.note.QApplication` (used there only at the clipboard call) with a stub capturing `setText`, never touching the Windows clipboard; `_attempt_copy` drives both routes (button click + direct `_copy_note`); `test_copy_never_reaches_the_clipboard_under_a_recorded_fail` (nothing at any point, full ratification included) and `test_copy_delivers_exactly_the_ratified_note_under_a_recorded_pass` (empty while a proposal is pending / a warning unacknowledged / the note unsaved; after Save the click delivers exactly `format_note_body(note)`, containing the confirmed proposal's text, every line a section title or a provenance-tagged bullet; the direct route agrees). +2 tests (1366 -> 1368).
- /fix date: 2026-09-05
- /fix applied by: Claude Code (`claude-fable-5-1`, composer seat, verify-then-fix)

#### PR-MED-011

- **[MED]** `docs/testing/shipping-gate.md:37` — R1–R5 lack a single defined scoring snapshot
- Triage: Fix-now
- Fix route: fix-on-fast
- Why it matters: Confirming or declining proposals re-finalises and changes the displayed note. Different practitioners could score the initial draft, an intermediate state or the post-decision note and obtain different pass results from the same run.
- Current behaviour: The procedure says, `"Score R1–R6 before leaving the review"`, while R6 stops only `"after all proposal decisions and warning acknowledgements"`. Only R4 expressly refers to assertions `"that survive review"`. In production, `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/ui/note.py:364` and `:409` update proposal decisions and call `_refinalise()`, changing the note being scored.
- Desired behaviour: Define one precise snapshot for R1–R5—for example, after every proposal decision and warning acknowledgement at the R6 stop point, but before Save, Cancel or Complete—and state how a regeneration restarts or invalidates the scoring row.
- Pattern to follow: R4’s “survive review” concept, expanded into one common snapshot definition for all note-based metrics.
- Pattern siblings: R1, R2, R3 and R5 all depend on which version of the note is inspected.
- Invariant: A recording has one reproducible set of R1–R5 counts for a given completed review.
- Verification: Static lifecycle tracing confirms that review decisions mutate the finalised note before Save; the document does not otherwise identify which state supplies the counts.
- Regression risk: Documentation-only, but changing the snapshot may require corresponding clarification in the proposed scoring table.
- /fix decision: Applied
- /fix notes: Leg 1: CONFIRMED - `confirm_proposal` / `decline_proposal` reach `_refinalise()` (`ui/note.py:366`), so the note being counted changes with each decision and the doc named no single scoring state. Leg 2: step 4 of "Running it" and the "What is measured" intro define ONE scoring point - after every proposal decision and warning acknowledgement, before Save / Cancel / Complete - cite `_refinalise` as the reason, make R6's stop the same point, and state that a regeneration discards the row.
- /fix date: 2026-09-05
- /fix applied by: Claude Code (`claude-fable-5-1`, composer seat, verify-then-fix)

#### PR-LOW-013

- **[LOW]** `docs/testing/shipping-gate.md:37` — The procedure claims Note-tab plaintext is explicitly cleared on window close
- Triage: Fix-now
- Fix route: fix-on-fast
- Why it matters: This repeats the project’s known “claim outruns the enforcing structure” failure class. Normal application close releases plaintext by process exit; the close handler does not call the Note screen’s clearing operation.
- Current behaviour: The procedure says, `"The window may be closed or crashed between recordings; the tab’s plaintext is cleared on close"`. At `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/ui/main_window.py:198`, an accepted close performs `"self.microphone_screen.stop_monitor()"` and `"super().closeEvent(event)"`; it does not call `note_screen.clear()`. The production application then exits its event loop and process.
- Desired behaviour: Say that an accepted application close ends the process and releases in-memory plaintext, while a crash may leave only the encrypted recovery store. Do not claim an explicit tab-clear operation on close.
- Pattern to follow: `docs/security/retention-schedule.md:25`, which separately names explicit clear/cancel/replacement paths and process exit.
- Pattern siblings: `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/ui/note.py:28` also says plaintext is `"cleared on close"` and should be corrected if this wording is being touched.
- Invariant: Retention documentation names only lifecycle guarantees implemented by an enforcing call or by process termination.
- Verification: Downgraded from MED to LOW because the shipped close path exits the process, so the practical in-memory retention outcome remains bounded despite the incorrect mechanism.
- Regression risk: Documentation-only.
- /fix decision: Applied
- /fix notes: Leg 1: CONFIRMED - `MainWindow.closeEvent`'s accept path is `stop_monitor()` + `super().closeEvent(event)` (`main_window.py:196-200`); the four `note_screen.clear()` call sites are a new live transcript, a recovered transcript, a new generation start and transcript-closed (Complete/Discard), plus `cancel_review` - none is window close. Leg 2: step 4 lists exactly those review-ending events, says an accepted window close ends the process with no separate tab-clear (close is refused while a review is busy) and that a crash leaves only the encrypted recovery store; the sibling `ui/note.py` module docstring reworded the same way.
- /fix date: 2026-09-05
- /fix applied by: Claude Code (`claude-fable-5-1`, composer seat, verify-then-fix)
